Release history

Exo Launcher changelog

Every release note for Exo Launcher, updated automatically from the update service.

Download for Windows 11 About Exo Launcher Atom feed

3.28.0 — November 20, 2026

  • Decoupled IHostTransport, ACL-hardened Named Pipe transport, and Rune ADR. Extracts WebView2 coupling out of WebHostBridge behind IHostTransport (WebViewHostTransport and NamedPipeHostTransport with current-user SID ACL and 4 MiB NDJSON framing) and records docs/decisions/2026-11-20-native-ui-rune.md targeting anomalyco/rune (wgpu DX12).
  • Exo AI real-time optimization core & consolidated PC room. Unifies Best Settings, Autopilot, Auto-Tune, and anti-cheat-safe user-mode NVML GPU fan curves inside Exo AI with silent non-elevated background optimization and 30-minute idle drift checks, upgrades Autopilot to a 2000 ms / 0.25-core look with alt-tab priority/1 ms timer restoration, collapses already-optimal setup steps, consolidates PC top chips to Exo AI, Apps, Storage, and Devices, and merges Upscalers + vendor control panels into Graphics.
  • Real Liquid Glass, clean icons, Discord DM pinning & Apps/Startup fixes. Restores authentic blurred AMOLED Liquid Glass across page controls and panels, reverts cluttered UI icon swaps while keeping Goeist, removes the Library scroll scrubber, adds synced 1:1 & group DM pinning in Discord, and fixes Apps layout sizing, Startup list refreshing on reopen, re-enabling Task Manager-disabled entries (Turn on), grouped duplicate entry errors, and shell-host memory inflation.
  • Fix Exo Shaders elevated optimize-cache failure (exit code 1). Elevated scskiller.exe runs triggered by Exo Shaders inside addons\shaders\engine\ now recognize the bundled ExoShaders.Service.exe in Exo builds instead of refusing live data with exit code 1 before writing --result, and early CLI guard exits now always record their reason in --result.
  • Latency check. Best settings › System measures for ten seconds how long your drivers hold the processor (the DPC and interrupt times LatencyMon shows), names the driver with the longest, says plainly whether that's fine for games and audio, and compares it with your check before. It changes nothing; Windows asks permission once.
  • MSI mode that a device can't run is put back. A device that doesn't start after Device interrupts switched it to MSI mode is named in PC, and Exo puts it back by itself at the next start when Windows doesn't need to ask (Undo does it at once).

3.27.0 — November 20, 2026

  • Liquid Glass overhaul, zero-scroll game sheet, and redesigned Apps room. Top bar capsules wear true backdrop-blur Liquid Glass over pure black surfaces while static page controls avoid idle GPU readbacks, game pages display stats and lines inline without scrolling, and PC › Apps adds inline status tags (Startup, Exo replaces, Bloat), grouped dependency counts, and compact actionable notices (#72, #81, #86).
  • Discord screen sharing, Stage & activity parity, and UI fixes. Screen share capture handles disabled WGC caching cleanly with hardware cursor composition, HDR tonemapping, H.265-to-H.264 fallback, and source hot-swapping; adds Stage instances, gift embeds, voice activity status, Nitro soundboard search/volume, keyboard shortcuts, and widens the Me quick-menu caret to 16px (#74, #82, #85, #88).
  • Always-on Autopilot, smart Memory Integrity & automatic Exo EQ. Autopilot runs continuous stutter monitoring with vendor-aware BIOS Virtualization hints for Memory Integrity, Exo EQ applies AutoEq curves and automatic per-game profiles automatically, and Audio pages load Output, Microphone, and Exo Voice even when unfocused while isolating per-endpoint COM errors (#75, #80, #88).
  • Exo Shaders Phase 2, SCSKiller 1.4.8 & Voice patch fix. Merges SCSKiller 1.4.8, moves Exo Capture Native AOT MIT source into the repo (#69), improves Unreal shader cache detection/rebuild, and fixes Exo Voice suppression patching (#79).
  • Onboarding StartWithWindows, quiet --startup launch & Store/Game reliability. Onboarding offers Start with Windows, --startup launches quietly into the tray, Riot/Microsoft Store launches handle headless RiotClientServices and AppX fallbacks reliably, Steam overlay defaults off while preserving per-game opt-ins, Wuthering Waves Anti-Cheat Expert and AMD Fluid Motion Frames are supported safely, and game compression uses one automatic thread budget (#73, #75, #84).
  • Liquid Glass FPS overlay, Achievement preview & idle performance. Polishes the GPU capture pipeline and Liquid Glass FPS overlay with ProcessSnapshot reuse, pauses idle WebView animations and backstage room loops so idle Library CPU stays near zero, splits lazy room CSS and mouse driver bundles, and updates the Music bar responsive breakpoint (#76, #77, #83, #88).
  • Windows 11 26H2 WinUI HWND E2E & tooling reliability. Resolves Exo's WinUIDesktopWin32WindowClass window directly across win.ps1, exo.mjs, swap-dev.ps1, and measure-memory.ps1 on Windows 11 26H2, syncs E2E assertions, and clears dependency and knip checks (#70, #71, #78, #86, #87).

3.26.0 — November 14, 2026

  • Autopilot reports what your game sessions did. PC › Autopilot shows the game in front live (frames, 1% and 0.1% lows, stutters, processor and graphics card use, clocks, temperature, video and system memory, the standby list and driver latency) and, after you play, a report of each session: its frames, its stutters and what came with each (a background app jumping, the card's memory full, the card hot, the processor slowing, memory paging to disk, a driver holding a processor, or likely shader compiling), with what to do about it. It reads what Windows already reports (the FPS counter's present events, Windows' own counters and process list), opens nothing in a game, says nothing while you play and keeps its reports on this PC only.
  • Smaller updates. Update now downloads only the files that changed since the version you have, often a tenth of the full installer or less, and says how much as it goes (Downloading 14 MB of 17 MB). An update that stops halfway picks up where it left off. Every file is checked before anything is swapped, your current version is kept until the new one has started, and Exo uses the full installer whenever a release changes the installer itself or anything doesn't check out. The first update after this one is still a full install.
  • Report a problem, without a GitHub account. Settings › About › Report a problem opens a form: say what happened, keep Attach logs on (Show what's sent shows the scrubbed log exactly as it goes) and leave a contact if you'd like a reply. Send goes privately to Exo's team and the button turns into your report's number; Open on GitHub starts a public issue instead, with no logs. Crash reports and these messages now reach the team as issues in a private tracker, one per error with how often it was seen, instead of as emails.
  • A cleaner Library. The Library opens under one head: its name with how many games, a search in the middle, then Filter, the sort, More and Add (New folder, Add a game, Activate key). Covers sit edge to edge on one even gap, and a scrubber on the right edge (A to Z, or Today to Never, hours, size or store, as you sort) shows while you scroll and jumps to the group you press or drag to; from the keyboard a letter jumps to the next game starting with it.
  • Search your games the way you'd say it. "racing games I haven't played", "co-op installed" or "steam over 20 hours" is read on your PC, with no network and no AI service: the words Exo knows become filters, shown as chips under the search, and the rest must be in the name. Genres and modes (racing, RPG, co-op, local co-op, controller ...) come from Steam's own record of each Steam game; Ctrl+F finds, Escape clears.
  • Filters that add up. Filter holds chips for installed or not, update ready, played lately, not played, family shared, friends playing now, each store, and Steam's genres and modes. Chips of one kind widen (Steam or Epic), kinds narrow (and installed); each one on sits under the head with its ×, and Clear takes them all off.
  • Game pages, details on demand. A game opens on its cover, its name, its hours and Play, every page the same size whatever your PC supports for it. Info (or I) widens the page and opens a pane beside it with the facts, upscalers, shaders, Optimize, HDR and frame generation, and stays open from game to game; Escape closes it first. A small dot on Info says there is something to know there.
  • Every glyph is a vector now. Replaced the remaining CSS-border icons (checkmarks, spinner, close, and chevron arrows) and gave anti-cheat, compression, frame generation, and store badges distinct Lucide icons.
  • Goeist on every surface, not just the page. The FPS counter, the achievement banner, Exo Capture's windows, Exo's own dialogs and menus, Setup's screens and the installer's pages now draw Goeist like the rest of Exo, and Exoline is gone. The FPS counter's pill and the banner take the same glass edge as the app (lit along the top) instead of a gray outline, and Exo's native colors match the app's pure black.
  • First run keeps up with Exo. Best settings for this PC now shows Update DLSS, FSR and XeSS automatically (on, as it is by default) beside frame generation, its tiles and the summary on Done take the glass line instead of a gray border, and Done draws Exo's own check.
  • Exo Mouse's checks, menu arrow and heart are Lucide's. The last glyphs Exo Mouse drew itself match the Lucide icons the rest of Exo uses, and the check that draws itself in across Exo is Lucide's check.
  • A live call's dot holds still. The green dot beside how long a call has been going no longer breathes; the time already shows it is live.
  • Only what works on your PC. Rows that can't work here are gone instead of greyed out: no HDR rows on a screen without HDR; NVIDIA's, AMD's and Intel's own settings only on their own card; a controller over Bluetooth shows one line, Connect it with a cable, in place of the light, sound, vibration and Calibrate it can't take; keyboards and controllers Exo has no page for have no card in Devices; Riot ID only with the Riot Client; a game Exo will never compress has no Compress row; Exo Shaders' recording only with Exo Shaders; Spotify's Smart Shuffle only while a playlist plays. A screen's color format, depth or range it takes one way only has no row, and a single camera on Default is named instead of offered as a menu. Frame generation is set in PC › Graphics only (it was in Settings › Downloads too), Also in games with anti-cheat shows while upscaler updates are on or while global consent is still active so you can turn it off, and Auto tune says why Tune waits.
  • Your Steam Family's games all show, and say whose they are. Exo now reads your family's whole shared library from Steam's family service, installed or not, with the Steam website sign-in in Settings › Game stores when you don't use Exo's own Steam sign-in, so a game nobody on this PC has opened yet still shows with its name and cover. Before, a family game showed only once Steam on this PC had seen it, and a Steam update that moved its app list cut a family from 110 games to 3. A game's page says who shares it, games an owner keeps private are left out, and when Steam is closed or the sign-in has lapsed the family's last list stands until Steam answers again.
  • Riot games show their hours like Steam games. A VALORANT or League page shows its lifetime hours as Played and nothing more: the per-mode hours, rank, K/D, matches and top picks are gone from the page.
  • tracker.gg says why it can't import, and lets you set starting hours. tracker.gg blocks automated app requests, so Exo stops auto-scraping it on Riot ID save, lets you set Starting hours inline (keeping all tracked sessions) or open tracker.gg in your browser, and keeps counting every session you play.
  • Wuthering Waves safe optimization & AMD frame generation. Exo identifies Wuthering Waves as Anti-Cheat Expert while keeping injected/placed mods out, treats PvE Anti-Cheat Expert games as Everyday in Setups, lets AMD RX 6000+ GPUs use driver Fluid Motion Frames without blocking on NVIDIA's nvngx_dlssg.dll, and adds a safe signed NVIDIA DRS Optimize profile (DLSS 4/4.5 Transformer presets + opt-in driver Smooth Motion on RTX 40/50 when not using native DLSS-G).
  • Steam overlay off by default across installed games. Exo turns the Steam overlay off by default across installed Steam games while preserving explicit per-game opt-ins (on= / Steam on!), records pre-Exo VDF state across accounts for clean reversal without changing Steam's global setting, and warns in Properties when Steam's global overlay setting is off.
  • Single automatic compression budget. Game compression picks one optimal thread budget automatically for HDD, idle SSD, or active SSD without the Gentle/Balanced/Fast selector, while preserving all anti-cheat and DataOnly safety guards.
  • Steam DLC/properties resolver & Riot/Store open reliability. ExoLauncher.SteamIpc resolves steamclient64.dll IPC vtable slots via x64 RIP-relative string references, Exo Shaders exempts ui.visible while stopping, Microsoft Store launching allows ms-windows-store://home with AppX/AppsFolder fallback, and Riot Client account switching / visible relaunch closes tray/headless RiotClientServices safely without touching Vanguard or active games.
  • Upscalers say whether they're up to date. A game's Upscalers line reads "Up to date: DLSS 310.9.16", how many updates wait, or which upscaler's version Exo couldn't read ("Version unknown: XeSS", never up to date beside it), never the name of an upscaler mod in the game (OptiScaler from Exo's frame generation included); the Frame generation line still names the mod, and Exo still leaves a mod's files alone.
  • Every game page has the same lines. Upscalers, Shaders, Frame generation, HDR and Optimize are on every installed game's page, each the same height, and a line the game or screen doesn't support says so ("Not supported in this game", "This screen can't show HDR") instead of disappearing. On a GeForce RTX 40 or 50, a game with frame generation of its own reads "Built into the game", not unsupported; a game Exo can't read says "Could not check", and a card no mod helps shows no Frame generation line.
  • A System group in Best settings. Below Network, Exo puts back Windows defaults a tweak tool changed and that cost games: Windows' own fast timer where the slow platform clock (HPET) was forced, Windows' scheduling for programs where it favored background work, a page file Windows manages where there was none, and memory for programs before the file cache. Each appears only when it's changed on your PC, has its own switch, and Use best settings sets them with one Windows prompt that makes a restore point first. Two more only their own switch sets: two services Windows starts for nothing set to Manual, and Memory Integrity off for the last frames, which says plainly that it's less secure, asks twice, and stays on when Riot Vanguard or FACEIT is installed. Exo writes down every value first, so turning a switch off puts back exactly what was there; changes that need a restart say so.
  • A finished game stops counting as running. When a game closed, Exo could go on telling Exo Cinema and its own background work that a game was running for the rest of the session, so Cinema's upscaler, RTX Video HDR and Smooth motion stayed off and warm-ups, compression and updates kept waiting. Exo now checks again once the game's session closes, and every 15 seconds while only an open session (no game left) keeps it counted, for two minutes at most; the signal is also cleared when Exo starts and when it closes.
  • Exo Shaders lives in Exo's own source. Exo Shaders, Exo's fork of SCSKiller, is now built from Exo Launcher's repository with every release and its source published from the same commit; it is still its own program under the GPL, run beside Exo. Recording no longer comes back by itself between sessions in a Steam game you set to start without its anti-cheat, or in any game Exo keeps the recorder out of: Exo tells the engine which games those are, and only a session you start from Exo lets the recorder in. A Compile or Refresh pressed just as the engine was going to sleep now wakes a new one instead of failing, and the engine sends fewer updates while it compiles.
  • Exo EQ, Exo's own headphone EQ. Your audio's page corrects the headphones you pick with AutoEq's settings for that model (thousands of headphones, IEMs and headsets, each from its most accurate measurement, and Exo names who measured it), with a preamp worked out from the whole curve so nothing clips. Over it: Bass, Vocal, Footsteps or your own ten bands, and Compare to hear it without the EQ. Each output keeps its own EQ and plays it the moment Windows switches to it; a game can have a sound of its own while it runs; Night Shift softens the top end late at night. It plays through Equalizer APO, which Exo installs on your press (from SourceForge, checked against its published hashes, one Windows prompt) and guides you through turning on for your outputs. Changes are heard at once: nothing restarts, and nothing of Exo's runs for it.
  • Goodbye, Lucid, and thank you, tekkusai. Lucid, tekkusai's headphone EQ, came with Exo and tuned our players' headphones for years; thank you, tekkusai, for letting Exo carry it and for all the work in it. Exo EQ takes its place: the first start of this version carries your Lucid headphone, per-game EQ and Night Shift into Exo EQ, closes the Lucid Exo ran and turns its EQ off in Equalizer APO so the sound isn't corrected twice. Lucid no longer ships in Exo's download; a Lucid you run yourself is left alone.
  • Sensor angle on the Razer Viper V3 Pro. Devices › Sensor has a Sensor angle slider for the Viper V3 Pro, on its cable or its HyperSpeed receiver, from -30° to +30° a degree at a time, with Reset to 0 and Measure beside it. Exo reads the angle from the mouse when it connects; the row shows a ring while the angle goes in, a check once the mouse took it, and a plain line if it didn't. Exo sends Synapse's own command, read off a capture of Synapse setting it; other mice whose angle Exo can't set don't get the row.
  • Open Exo when Windows starts. A switch in Settings › General, off until you turn it on.
  • Captures, laid out like Photos. The Captures sheet is one grid of your clips and screenshots by day, edge to edge, under a big title with the month you're on; a slim scrubber down the right edge jumps through months and years, and On this day brings back what you captured on this date in earlier years. Clips, Screenshots, Favorites and each game are chips that combine (a game's favorite screenshots), and Snip is the one main button.
  • A viewer that gets out of the way. A capture opens edge to edge under one slim bar: Edit, Share, Favorite, Info, Delete and More; the arrow keys step through them. Info (game, date, resolution, length, size, folder) is a pane you open when you want it.
  • One editor, by what you want to do. Edit opens the picture or clip on the left and one panel on the right with a tab for each job, Revert, Undo and Redo at the top left and Save at the top right. A screenshot has Crop (free, 16:9, 9:16 or Square), Mark up (arrow, box, highlight, text), Blur and Copy text; a clip has Trim and Export (Discord under 10 MB, 16:9 full quality, 9:16 for Shorts and Reels), each a tile.
  • Blur names and chat. Blur in the screenshot editor smears what you drag over coarsely enough that the letters under it can't be read back, and it replaces the black Hide box.
  • Copy text from a screenshot. Drag over words in the editor, or press Copy text in the viewer's More, and Windows reads them on this PC (its own text recognition, nothing uploaded) and puts them on the clipboard.
  • Send to Discord, straight to the conversation. Share › Send to Discord picks up to five of your DMs and channels in Exo's own Discord client, with an optional message, and sends the capture there without leaving Captures. A clip over Discord's 10 MB, or a trimmed one, goes as its Discord copy, made first; a screenshot over 10 MB says to crop it. No upload sites are involved.
  • After a snip, your choice. Capture settings › After a snip: Open the editor (as before) or Copy only, which leaves the snip copied and saved with nothing opening. A snip taken over a game never pulls Exo in front.
  • Cloudflare DNS, encrypted. Best settings sets your network adapters in use (never a VPN, Hyper-V or other virtual adapter) to Cloudflare's DNS, 1.1.1.1 and 1.0.0.1, and 2606:4700:4700::1111 and ::1001 where the adapter runs IPv6, with DNS over HTTPS on (Windows' automatic template), so lookups are fast and private. Exo writes down each adapter's DNS first (automatic from your router stays automatic), and its switch puts that back exactly. An adapter with no IPv6 gets IPv4 only and says so; if Windows doesn't turn the encryption on, Exo says that too.
  • A Network group in Best settings. Your adapters' own settings (energy-efficient Ethernet, Realtek's Green Ethernet, Power Saving Mode and Gigabit Lite off; receive-side scaling and Intel Wi-Fi's Throughput Booster back to their defaults), Cloudflare DNS, and Windows' TCP defaults where something turned them off each have their own switch. Use best settings sets every one that's on with one Windows prompt; after each, Exo checks a lookup still answers and puts that one back at once if it doesn't. A switch turned off puts back what was there and keeps it out of Best settings. Check measures ping, jitter and a DNS lookup, before and after, and says plainly when nothing changed. Tweaks with no evidence of helping a game (interrupt moderation, NetworkThrottlingIndex, SystemResponsiveness, TcpAckFrequency and Nagle, offloads, flow control) are left alone.
  • Immersive Now playing. Music's player and Now playing have a full-window button (or press F): the cover large in its own colours, the song, its place and its controls, and the lyrics large beside it, sung line by line where the service times them. The cover flies out to it and back; the top bar fades away until you move to the top or press a key; Esc or the exit button leaves. It stays on while Exo runs and starts off next time.
  • Downloads follow your store account. Switch to another Steam, Epic, GOG or Amazon account while a game downloads, in Exo or in the store's own app, and the downloads of the account you left pause and say so, instead of carrying on or being dropped from the line (one Steam is already running stays Steam's to continue); switch back and they carry on by themselves. A download you paused yourself stays paused. Starting an app or tool from the Library (what Steam calls an application or a tool) no longer pauses downloads as a game does, and you can play another game of the same store while one downloads.
  • Compress games. A game's Properties has a Compress row, and the PC room a Compress chip listing every game with what it saved and could still save, with Compress all. Exo uses Windows' own compression, which the game reads as before, picks the algorithm for your PC and drive, samples each kind of file first and leaves alone what wouldn't shrink, and never runs while the game plays or updates. Games with anti-cheat wait for your yes and their programs stay as they are. Compress while idle and Compress again after updates are off until you turn them on.
  • Lucide icons. Every icon in Exo and Exo Mouse is now from Lucide, one consistent line family; liked, starred and pinned show filled.
  • Icons that say what they do. Every icon was checked against what its control does. The window's Maximize is arrows out and Restore arrows in (no more stacked windows); Stop is a solid square, apart from Record's circle; Discord's Now is a lightning bolt, not a nut; Remove from a list is a minus, not a boxed cross; Mark unread, Speak message, rules channels, a wired connection, Calibrate, Clean up, Exo asleep while you play and Exo Shaders each have their own plain picture; Songs is a list and Now playing a record; a Discord video's Full screen and Capture's Save replay use the same icon everywhere. Lines now draw at Exo's lighter weight that follows their size, and liked, starred, pinned and the play controls really show filled.
  • GIFs in Discord stay. A GIF that stumbled once (often while Exo was minimized) turned into a "would not load" line for good; it now tries again, and again when you come back. Imgur GIFs load too.
  • Things line up. In Discord the panes' titles share one line and every list starts at the same edge (channels, categories, messages, friends); in the PC room the This PC, Updates and Shaders titles sit on one line.
  • Downloads, in one place. Library › More › Downloads lists what's downloading, what's next and what's paused: pause or stop what runs, move what's next up or down or take it out, resume what's paused, and Pause all until you Resume all. Each game has its own Update automatically switch.
  • Uninstall several apps without a window each. Pick apps in PC › Apps and press Uninstall: each goes quietly where its installer allows it (Windows Installer, Inno Setup, NSIS, Squirrel, WiX bundles, or the app's own quiet mode), one after another, and one line says how many went. An app whose uninstaller has no quiet mode still opens its own window, and Windows still asks for permission where it needs to.
  • A restore point before you remove several apps. Restore point, on by default beside Uninstall for the apps you picked, has Windows make a System Restore point first; if Windows can't (System Restore is off, or you decline its prompt), nothing is removed.
  • Scheduled tasks come back with Put back. A task an app left is written down before Exo deletes it, so Put back registers it again, unless one of the same name is there again.
  • Drivers an app left are found. After an uninstall, a driver whose file is in the app's own folder is listed with what it left, never taken by one press: pick it yourself if no device needs it. Drivers Windows starts at boot and anti-cheat drivers are never listed.
  • Windows apps, for you or for every account. PC › Apps › Windows apps lists the Store and preinstalled apps on this PC across its accounts, including what new accounts get. Pick some (Pick them takes the ones you didn't ask for), choose For you or Every account, and Review says what each removal does and whether it can come back before anything goes: one removed for you comes back with Add for you while its files are still on the PC; one removed for every account needs Windows' permission and only the Microsoft Store brings it back. The Store, App Installer, app frameworks, WebView2, Gaming Services, Xbox sign-in, Windows Security and the parts of Windows itself are never offered.
  • A restore point wherever Windows asks. Clearing what an app left that needs Windows' permission (a forced uninstall's leftovers, a driver, a service) and the one-press Remove rows now make a System Restore point first, in the same prompt, while Restore point is on. Windows keeps one a day, so a point made in the last day stands instead of asking again.
  • A driver an app left goes with its package. Picking a driver an app left now takes its package out of Windows' driver store too, saved first so Put back adds it again; Windows refuses one a device still uses, and then the driver stays.
  • File types and firewall rules an app left. A file type that still opens with a removed app is listed with Default apps, where you choose another (Windows protects that choice, so Exo never changes it itself), and firewall rules the app grouped with its own are found too.
  • How sure Exo is, for every item. Review says Certain, Likely or Possible beside each thing an app left, and why (inside the app's folder, named after it, in its firewall group); only Certain is picked for you.
  • Windows Installer files no app needs. PC › Apps finds copies in Windows' installer cache that no installed app or update is registered with, and Clear moves them to the Recycle Bin under one Windows prompt, with Put back.
  • Goeist, Exo's new type. Exo and Exo Mouse use Goeist, a rounded take on Geist: the same proportions, every corner softened.
  • Shop beside Home. Discord's Shop is its own place at the top of the Discord room, next to Home, and opens in one press.
  • Play is gone from Discord. The Play place (Games, Party and Clips) and its Show Play switch are removed; Home, your conversations and servers are unchanged.
  • Sharing with friends on Exo is how Exo works, not a switch. The game you opened from Exo and a game you are installing, building shaders for or have ready are always shared with friends who use Exo (never through Discord, never a game you hid or added yourself, nothing while you're Invisible), and show under their name in your friends list. The two switches in Settings › Discord › Privacy are gone.
  • Badge and reaction tips sit on what they name. The name of a profile badge, a reaction's people and a call's Clips on tip now show right above it (or below it at the top of the window) and stay inside the window, instead of landing somewhere else on the card.
  • Pinned messages in a DM say so. A DM or group with no pins says so in its own words; pinning and unpinning there works as in a server.
  • Glass edges, not grey outlines. Panels, menus and controls are lit at their edge like glass, brightest along the top, instead of a grey line all the way round, and lines inside a panel are faint enough that they never stack into a grey band.
  • Old frame generation installs come out. A game that got Exo Bridge from an earlier test build (Hell Is Us) had it left behind for good; Exo now takes it out by its record and puts today's frame generation in.
  • Five rooms, in the middle. The top bar has Library, Store, Discord, Music and PC, centered in the window. Devices moved into the PC room: its button sits on the This PC line beside Apps, and nothing in it changed. The mouse's capsule left the top bar; its battery shows in the FPS counter.
  • Battery in the FPS counter. Settings › FPS counter can add Lowest battery: the wireless mouse, headset or controller lowest on charge, as "82% MOUSE", read once a minute.
  • One switch for upscalers in games with anti-cheat. Settings › General › Also in games with anti-cheat lets DLSS, FSR and XeSS update in every such game at once, instead of allowing each game. It can risk a ban; Riot games and Vanguard are never touched, and frame generation mods still stay out of games with anti-cheat. Turning the upscaler switch on or off on its own also saves now.
  • Your games' DLSS, FSR and XeSS update by themselves. With Update DLSS, FSR and XeSS automatically on, every game that has them moves to the newest signed files while you aren't playing, as Update all would, with nothing to press. Games with anti-cheat are left alone unless you allowed it for that game, and Restore in a game's Properties still puts its own files back.
  • Frame generation's switch is in Settings too. Frame generation in games without anti-cheat sits in Settings › General beside the upscaler switch, as well as in the PC room, and each game's page still turns it off for that game.
  • Pure black under Liquid Glass. Exo is AMOLED black everywhere, under its frosted glass; the Background choice is gone, and a PC that had another background picked switches to pure black.
  • Upscalers and frame generation stay current by themselves. DLSS, FSR and XeSS files, OptiScaler, MFG Unlocker and DLSSG SM86 now come straight from their makers' releases the day they ship, with Exo's own copy as the backup, and every file is still checked before it goes in. Automatic upscaler updates are on for everyone.
  • NVIDIA's frame generation on RTX 20 and 30. Best settings' Frame generation now puts DLSSG SM86 into games on an RTX 20 or 30, instead of FSR frame generation.
  • DLSS updates download again. Updating a game to DLSS 310.9.16 stopped with "Not Found"; it downloads now.
  • New and updated games compile their shaders by themselves. While nobody uses the PC, Exo Shaders now compiles a game you just installed, one it never compiled, and one an update changed, most played first, the same way it already rebuilt games after a driver update. It pauses when you come back, and a game that won't compile is left alone until the next driver.
  • Replay length fits your PC. Exo can shorten how far back instant replay goes without lowering quality. Capture settings shows the replay time you can save when the add-on reports it.
  • Capture recovers more reliably. If Capture stops, Exo keeps the last saved clip and reconnects with your settings and the game you are playing. Switching games quickly no longer sends an old game after the new one.
  • Discord, laid out like Discord. Your servers are a band across the top, each with a dot for unread, a count for mentions and a voice mark when people are in a call; hover one and its name and who is talking show under it. Home is your direct messages on the left and Friends (Online, All, Pending) beside them. A server is its channels, the conversation and its members, always shown; a DM shows the person's profile beside it. Unread channels are white and bold with a mark at their edge, read ones grey. Mute, Deafen and Discord's settings sit beside your picture at the bottom left, and Discord's settings open in the room instead of Exo's Settings. The message box stands clear of the window's edge with a Send button beside the emoji. Voice calls show who is there and that you are connected, with words on their buttons when there is room.
  • Liquid Glass is back. Menus, sheets, Ctrl+K, the player and the controls are frosted glass again, inside the same thin line.
  • The driver install is full screen. While a graphics driver goes in, the screen has no window buttons, so Exo can't be closed or hidden halfway. When it is done it says the driver is in and that your settings are back in one line, lists only what couldn't be put back, and offers Restart now or Not now.
  • No scrollbars in Discord, and one outline everywhere. Lists scroll without a bar, and every panel in Discord has the same thin line as the rest of Exo.
  • Exo says less. Settings rows are a name and a control: the lines that explained how each one works are gone, Backups is two rows (Last backup with Back up now, and Backup file with Export and Import), and Account shows your email with Sign out beside it. Sync has a row only when it is stuck.
  • The PC room shows parts, not readouts. Each part is its name (the screen adds its refresh rate, a drive its free space); cores, memory speed, BIOS dates and drive temperatures are gone. Updates lists only what you can act on, by name, with no version numbers, and a driver held back for a known problem no longer shows as a row with nothing to press.
  • A drive problem is one short line. "Running hot. Give it airflow or a heatsink.", "2 games load slowly from this hard drive."
  • One place asks you to sign in. The line under each game's playtime and the Library's banner are gone; the account screen is the one place. A game page mentions its drive only when heat slowed the game.
  • Devices is quieter. A device's port is said only when a better one would help, and the controller page keeps its readings without the sentences about where each is stored.
  • One thin line, sharp at any scale. Every outline, divider and sheet edge is the same line, one pixel of the screen wide. On a 125% or 150% screen the old outlines came out uneven and soft, and some were drawn thicker than others.
  • What you chose is a white pill, not an outline. The current room, tab or filter is white with black words. The rooms and the tools sit bare on the top bar, without a capsule around them, and nothing shows a ring inside a ring. The open page in Settings has a white marker beside it.
  • Exo has its own typeface, Exoline. Exo's own cut of Geist: a simpler a, an l with a tail so it never reads as a capital I, round dots, and an E and F with the short middle bar of Exo's mark. It is in the app, Exo Mouse, Setup, the achievement banner and the FPS counter.
  • Notices come up at the bottom right. They sat at the top right, over a room's own sort and More buttons. Newest is lowest, and in Music they sit above the player.
  • The achievement banner is smaller and says its tier. The picture is 64px, and Rare, Epic, Legendary and Complete are one word in their colour beside the name.
  • The FPS counter is a pill of its own. It sits 8px in off the corner on a darker plate, instead of stuck to the screen's edge, so white numbers stay readable over a bright sky.
  • Check for updates is in Settings › About, beside Exo's version, where it is looked for; it was at the foot of General.
  • Exo never updates itself during a game. Update now or Try again while you play downloads and checks the update, then it goes in after your game, the next time Exo opens; before, Exo closed in the middle of the game. While an update goes in, a small Updating Exo window says so until the new version opens. Exo also checks there is room on the drive before it downloads.
  • An Exo account is required. While a PC is signed out, Exo opens on its account screen at every start: create an account or sign in, with no Not now and no close. The banner in the Library and the once-only sheet are gone. If Exo's account service can't be reached, Continue without an account lets that run through and the next start asks again.
  • Errors report themselves. An error inside one of Exo's pages, or one of its own requests failing unexpectedly, now sends its scrubbed report on its own, as crashes and freezes already did, so nobody has to copy a log (one report per error a day, five a day in all). Exo's service counts each error by app and version and tells the team the first time one appears.
  • App data and crash reports are always sent. Exo's anonymous app data and crash reports now come from every install, so the team sees what breaks: the Help improve Exo screen and the two Privacy switches are gone, and a choice of Don't share made in 3.25 no longer applies. Nothing about what is sent changed: a random install ID, no account, name or hardware; PRIVACY.md lists every event. Settings › Privacy still shows what was sent and deletes what Exo's service holds.
  • The unfinished Exo Store is gone. The store tab Exo carried for its own marketplace was never switched on for anyone, so it is removed with its settings and nothing you use changes. A wish that can't be saved is no longer shown as saved, and real games such as Costume Quest and DLC Quest are no longer hidden as not games.
  • Frame generation reaches more cards. Best settings' frame generation now covers GeForce GTX 900 and newer, Radeon RX 400 and newer (Vega included), Radeon graphics in laptops and handhelds, and Intel Arc cards and graphics, through OptiScaler; an RTX 20 or 30 gets OptiScaler until DLSSG SM86 can be supplied. A game's line says what really happened: another mod sits over Exo's loader, Exo's files changed or went, the game lost DLSS Frame Generation, or the mod came out. Turning it off keeps every file another mod or a mod manager put there. Still never in a game with anti-cheat.
  • Games with anti-cheat are left alone, found from the disk. Exo now finds anti-cheat from what is in the game's folder, its services and Steam's own data, not from its title. In a game that has one, or one Exo cannot read, nothing is put in: no mod, frame generation, shader recorder or Exo Capture hook (Exo Capture records such a game from the screen only), no administrator start, and no launch option that switches anti-cheat off or loads a file. Only the game's own settings and launch options change, and the vendor's own signed upscaler file can still be swapped for a newer one when you allow it for that game. Riot games are never touched.
  • Everything you chose is in your backup, and more of it follows you. One list of what you chose now drives backup and restore: folder pictures, games you added, controller memory and per-game controller settings, mouse profiles and presets, Lucid's settings, the music service, the wishlist, what Optimize changed (so Put back works), start-up choices and the choices the page kept, such as streamer mode and text size. A backup is also made when your last game closes and when Exo closes, carries no user name, keeps each picture once, and says so when it stops. The wishlist, Riot ID, music service and the page's choices sync to your other PCs; choices that belong to one PC's hardware stay on it.
  • Covers hold when a store is down. Exo's shared cover cache asks each store on its own, so one that refuses or fails no longer stops the others, and an outage pauses it only briefly. Refresh and Find cover keep the old picture until the new one is proven, so an offline refresh never leaves a blank, and real games are no longer filtered out as junk titles.
  • A failed update is tried again. An update that failed before it replaced Exo is offered again; only one that was rolled back is skipped. If the Updating Exo window never starts, Exo opens again. An update downloaded while you played goes in at the next start after the game.
  • Signing in is steadier. A session the service ended asks you to sign in again instead of failing quietly, a busy service is waited out, the recovery code must be typed back before the account screen closes, and one account state serves setup, the gate and Settings, so setup does not ask again.
  • Crash reports are not lost, and one install cannot crowd out the rest. A report Exo's service refuses under load, fails to store or does not answer is kept on your PC (five at most) and sent at a later start. The service now limits each install and each network on its own instead of sharing one limit per app.
  • Play and Stop wait only for the game that is busy, and Exo rests while hidden. Starting one game no longer holds Play and Stop for every other game. Exo's window hider, tray check, hang watchdog and heartbeat stop polling while it is hidden, the page's sleep rules re-check when a call, music, a recording or a download starts or ends, and the browser keys F5, Ctrl+R, Ctrl+P and Ctrl+F do nothing in Exo. No file path or error internals reach the page, and a settings change is saved one way, so a late answer cannot put an old value back.
  • The Library starts below the Bar. It no longer scrolls under the top bar, which sits bare on the black.
  • Optimize stays on, and says what happened. Exo now keeps a record of every value it wrote into a game's settings, reads them back before a launch, after the game closes and when Exo starts, and puts back what a game update reset. A line you changed back yourself twice is yours: Exo keeps your change and the game's Optimize line says so. Where a game will not keep a setting, the line says that instead of Applied. Nothing is written while the game or its launcher runs.
  • Start-up items you turned off stay off. Exo watches the places Windows starts programs from. An item that comes back is turned off again once; if it returns again Exo uses that program's own switch where it knows one. An item you turn back on in Task Manager or Settings stays on. Windows' own parts, security software, anti-cheat, drivers and Exo itself are never turned off, and each row says in a sentence what Exo knows of it.
  • Driver settings come back after any driver update. Exo's NVIDIA profile and each game's profile are put back after a driver installed by Windows Update, NVIDIA's app or by hand, not only by Exo, once the new driver has been in place for two minutes and with no game running. A setting the new driver does not have is skipped and never shown as on. PC shows that the settings were put back.
  • Game Mode and Game Bar can be given back. Exo records what Game Mode and Game Bar were set to before it changed them and puts exactly that back when you turn its keeping off or uninstall Exo. If something turns Game Mode off twice, Exo leaves it to you. The power plan and the graphics card choice are read back after they are set, and a setting Windows did not take is said, not counted.
  • Store apps stay quiet, with one switch. Settings › Game stores has Keep store apps quiet, on by default: Steam's and Epic's pop-ups and sounds stay off, and turning it off puts back what was there. Exo no longer closes a running Steam to change a setting, never leaves Steam offline, no longer turns cloud saves or automatic updates back on, and accepts a game's licence only for the game you start.
  • A sign-in is never hidden. Exo hides a store app's main window by what the window is, not by its title, and never hides a sign-in, two-factor, licence or payment window: it is brought to the front, and a Riot launch waits for you instead of timing out.
  • Shaders are compiled again after a driver update, by themselves. A new graphics driver throws compiled shaders away. Exo now queues every game it had compiled, most played first, and compiles them while nobody is using the PC: no game running, on mains power. It pauses when you come back and goes on later, with no scheduled task. Shaders shows how many games are ready.
  • Best settings no longer turns RTX Video HDR on. It stays in PC › Display, as your choice.
  • Exo measures each game's frame times, on this PC only. While a game runs, Exo records how evenly it ran under the settings in place, so a setting can later be judged by what it did. Nothing of it is sent anywhere, and Riot's games are left out.
  • Steam's game details read again on the current Steam. Steam now keeps its record of your games in many small files instead of one, and Exo read none of them: names of games you own but haven't installed, a game's DLC, branches and languages in Properties, whether a borrowed game can start offline, and Steam's word on Valve Anti-Cheat all went missing. Exo reads both the new files and the old one, and a list Exo read while Steam was still writing it is read again within seconds instead of waiting ten minutes.
  • Play NBA 2K26 with or without its anti-cheat. A Steam game whose own Play menu offers a start without its anti-cheat (NBA 2K26's "without EAC (offline only)") has an Anti-cheat row in Properties: Launch with anti-cheat, as before, or Launch without anti-cheat (offline). Without, Play starts Steam's own entry for it and the game counts as one with no anti-cheat, so frame generation, OptiScaler, MFG Unlocker and the shader recorder can go in. Back with anti-cheat, Exo takes them out first, and checks again as the game starts. Riot and Vanguard games never get mods, nor does a game with Valve Anti-Cheat or one Exo can't read.
  • Without anti-cheat, Exo's mods are in the game only while you play it from Exo. Started without its anti-cheat, a game like NBA 2K26 gets frame generation and the shader recorder as Exo's Play starts it, and they come out once the game and everything it started have closed, so starting it from Steam's own Play (which runs Easy Anti-Cheat) finds nothing of Exo's in its folder. The same files go back in next time, byte for byte; the game's own files are never moved or replaced, and nothing goes in over a file the game has under the same name since an update. If Exo closed or crashed mid-game, it takes them out when it starts; if the game is started with its anti-cheat while they are in, Exo leaves files in use alone, warns you, and takes them out as it exits. Best settings no longer puts frame generation into such a game between sessions; as you press Play it moves the mod to its newest release and puts back files that went. Files in the mod's folder that aren't Exo's stay where they are, the NVIDIA frame cap Exo moves into the mod goes back between sessions, and the shader recorder stays out beside DLSS frame generation, which can hang a game as it starts.
  • Tools and icons up to date. The UI's and Exo Mouse's type check runs on the stable TypeScript 7.0.2 instead of a 7.1 nightly, and Lucide's icon set is 1.2.157 (no icon Exo draws changed).

3.25.0 — November 9, 2026

  • Your setup survives a reinstall. Exo backs up your settings, library folders, collections, custom covers, playtime, Riot hours and notes once a day to Documents\Exo\Backups\Launcher, keeping thirty days. On first run after a reinstall, Exo offers the newest backup; Settings › General has Back up now, Export to a file and Import from a file. Sign-ins are never in a backup: you sign in again once. A restore checks every file before it replaces anything, and keeps a copy of what it replaces.
  • DLSS frame generation on RTX 20 and 30 is paused. Its mod, DLSSG SM86, has no licence that lets Exo supply it, so the option now shows as unavailable with that reason instead of starting a download. Other frame generation (OptiScaler, MFG Unlocker) is unchanged.
  • Exo needs Windows 11, and says so. Exo Setup stops before it changes anything on a PC older than Windows 11 (build 22000), and a copy of Exo started on one shows the same message and closes. Uninstalling still works.
  • DLSS, FSR and XeSS can update themselves. Settings › General has Update DLSS, FSR and XeSS automatically, off by default. Off, Exo only checks Exo's own copy for newer versions of the files your games use, shows one notice when one is listed, and downloads it when you press Download. On, it downloads the newer file itself. Every file is checked for its maker's signature, version and size before it is used.
  • Exo fetches from Exo, not GitHub. The store tools, the voice model, upscaler files and frame generation mods come from Exo's own copy at updates.getexo.app, each checked against a fixed SHA-256 before use. Exo no longer asks GitHub for anything, and help and source links go to getexo.app.
  • The message box is its own panel. It sits under the chat, as tall as your profile card, and its bottom edge lines up with the card's; the chat's bottom edge lines up with the channel list's, and the member list runs down to the same line. Replies, edits and attachments sit inside the panel above the field.
  • The top bar has no account circle. The avatar, the Sign in button and their menu are gone from the top bar; your account lives in Settings › Account. The one-time accounts sheet, the Home banner and the Sign in to sync line still tell you about accounts.
  • The Music room has its volume again. The bottom player shows a short volume slider with a speaker that mutes on click; the arrow keys step it 5 percent. It drives the service you have open.
  • Settings reads in the order you use it. Account, General, Library, Downloads, In game, Discord, Music, Privacy and About. General holds the look, Start maximized, Keep PC awake and Updates (check and install, clear caches); Library holds the stores, tools and soundtracks, and Shaders; Downloads and In game are their own pages. Every row is still there, and Ctrl+K finds each one.
  • Discord's Home is Discord's own mark. The Home button at the top of the server rail shows Discord's official mark, white, on the same tile as the other servers.
  • Exo is one app in Alt+Tab. The FPS counter and the window Exo draws its page in no longer appear as extra Exo windows: Alt+Tab and the taskbar list Exo once, whichever room is open.
  • One picker button in the message box. The GIF button is gone; the emoji button opens the picker, whose tabs hold GIFs, Stickers and Emoji.
  • Discord's columns line up. The channel list and your profile card share one column, the chat and the member list share one radius and hairline, and the card sits on the channel list's edges with the same gap. Each column's header is clipped to its own panel.
  • A server's hover tip closes when you click the server. Its voice list no longer stays over the channel list after you open the server, and an answer for a server you have left is dropped. It returns only when the pointer moves.
  • Help goes to getexo.app/help. Exo's help link, in Windows' Installed apps entry and the installer, now opens Exo's help page instead of a Discord invite. Exo still has its Discord server to chat in.
  • The Phantom Blade Zero driver notice is gone. Exo no longer points Phantom Blade Zero at an older NVIDIA driver: the newest driver is accepted, and the notice stops asking.
  • Exo wakes less while it sits in the background. Exo no longer checks which games are running every 5 seconds. It notices a game when its window opens or comes to the front, and checks again when a running game closes, so an idle Exo does almost nothing. A game that runs with no window, such as a dedicated server, is found when Exo launches it. If Windows can't report window changes, Exo checks every 15 seconds instead.
  • The window's page sleeps while Exo is hidden. Once Exo is hidden or minimised to the tray for 30 seconds, its page stops running and its memory can be paged out; showing Exo wakes it at once. It stays awake during a Discord call, while music plays, while a capture or recording runs, and while a download or install runs.
  • Steam sign-in lists every game you own and your Family games, with no key. Settings › Library › Game stores › Steam library: scan a QR code in the Steam app, or type your account name and password with Steam Guard. Exo keeps Steam's sign-in token protected on this PC, renews it by itself, and Sign out removes it. Works with a private profile and with Steam closed; the owned list and hours merge with the public profile so nothing counts twice. the sign-in calls and their answers are tested from recorded fixtures; the live Steam answers, the password path and Family games on a real account were not tried.
  • Cleaning keeps your sign-ins and saves. Junk and leftover cleaning never removes browser or store sign-in data, credentials, SSH keys, game saves or Exo's own data (its old update backups still go). A folder that holds one of them stays whole.
  • A program that is running keeps its files. Cleaning a Steam download or a leftover waits until the program that owns it is closed, and checks again right before each delete. If Exo can't tell which programs are running, it keeps the files.
  • Shader caches are Exo Shaders' alone. Exo no longer cleans old DirectX, NVIDIA or AMD shader caches itself; Drives still shows their size. Exo Shaders keeps the caches it owns, and no cleaner touches one while it compiles.
  • Freed space is what the drive gained. Junk and clean messages report the change in free space on the system drive, not the sum of the file sizes.
  • Games re-prepare after a Windows cleanup. When Windows' Disk Cleanup, Storage Sense or another cleaner wipes the DirectX shader cache, Exo marks the games it had prepared as "Shaders need preparing again" and queues them when automatic preparation is on.
  • One Exo account for every Exo app. Setup asks you to create an account or sign in, right after Welcome. Your password goes to Exo's account service once and is never kept on this PC. Your recovery code is shown once, with Copy and Save as text file, and Continue waits until you type it back. Offline, Exo still works and asks again the next time it starts. the screens were run in a browser mock, not in the installed app, and the live account service was not called.
  • Your settings and playtime follow you between PCs. Your preferences sync to your account, but not paths, window positions, GPU, display or Discord settings. Each play session syncs as one entry, so a session never counts twice, and another PC's sessions are added to this PC once. the live account service was not called.
  • Exo now has accounts. After the update, a sheet offers to create an account or sign in, once. Until you sign in, Settings › Account and a Sign in to sync line in your library show it, and Home has a slim banner you can hide; it comes back a week later, at most three times. Your library never waits for an account. the screens were run in a browser mock, not in the installed app.
  • Settings › Account. Signed in: your devices (remove another PC's sign-in), sync status with anything held on this PC and why, change password, a new recovery code, export your data as a JSON file, sign out, and delete your account. Signed out: create an account or sign in there. the account service was not called live.
  • The accounts banner sits above your games. It no longer floats over the Continue row; it is part of the Library's page, on the same glass and corner radius as the other cards. Opening the accounts sheet can no longer happen again for someone who has already seen it when the saved prompt state loads late (the sheet and banner now wait for it). the screens were rendered in a headless browser against the UI's mock host, not in the installed app.
  • Keyboard focus is visible and stays put. Continue cards show a focus ring, Tab stays inside an open Settings, What's new, update, accounts or consent sheet, and focus goes back to what opened it. the screens were rendered in a headless browser against the UI's mock host, not in the installed app.
  • Sliders, buttons and cursors are Exo's everywhere. The Display popover's sliders and the controls in other popovers and sheets drew as the browser's blue defaults; they now use Exo's own. the screens were rendered in a headless browser against the UI's mock host, not in the installed app.
  • Missing art shows a plate, not a broken-image mark. Shaders posters, upscaler cards, gifts, app icons, store tiles, captures and every other picture that fails to load fall back to the title or icon. Games without a cover keep a hairline edge on black. the screens were rendered in a headless browser against the UI's mock host, not in the installed app.
  • Errors read one way. Library, Devices, Discord, Music and the Store say what failed in one plain sentence with Try again and Copy logs (never Retry or Open again); the Library no longer prints the host's raw message, and Devices can try again after a failed scan. the screens were rendered in a headless browser against the UI's mock host, not in the installed app.
  • Small layout fixes. The mouse drawing in Devices is no longer cut flat; Discord's Active Now cards keep their second line and the server rail leaves a gap above your status at 720p; the Store's banner list keeps its row height and cover shape for any number of games; folder names read in white on every folder color; muted Discord channels are readable; the welcome step is true black; close, dismiss, clear-search and tab-close buttons are 28 px to press; the Bar no longer shifts when it loads. the screens were rendered in a headless browser against the UI's mock host, not in the installed app.

3.24.4 — November 6, 2026

  • No optional keys. Settings has no Optional keys section. Exo no longer asks for a Steam Web API, itch.io, SteamGridDB or OpenXBL key, and it deletes a copy an older version saved. Riot ID moved to Settings › Library › Game stores.
  • Xbox time played keeps what Xbox already reported. Exo's Xbox sign-in is gone (Microsoft has not approved it, and it needed an app registration). Hours Xbox reported before are moved once into Exo's playtime file and kept on your Xbox games, and Exo's own sessions add to them from then on, with nothing counted twice.
  • Steam keeps your owned games and hours without a key. Owned games come from the Steam client on this PC when it runs, and from your public Steam game list when your profile is public (no key, no sign-in; a private profile is never read as empty). Hours for games you have never installed here come back, and family-shared titles are labelled again. Achievements come from Steam's local files, with each achievement's name and description from Steam's own schema.
  • itch.io shows the games you own. Games you own on itch but have not installed come from the itch app on this PC (its own database, no key). Installed itch games still show.
  • Cover art without SteamGridDB or IGDB. Covers come from Steam, Epic, GOG, the Microsoft Store and each game's own icon.
  • Help improve Exo is your choice. Share app data and Send crash reports are both off until you choose, in one screen with nothing pre-selected. View what's sent shows the exact text of what was sent, and Delete my data asks Exo's server to remove what it holds for this PC and makes a new install ID. Settings › General › Privacy has the same switches.
  • Updates come from Exo only. Exo's own updates are fetched from Exo's update address, never from GitHub.
  • The PC stays awake while Exo works. Downloads, installs and shader warming hold the PC awake, and Settings › General has Keep PC awake during downloads and shader warming to turn that off.
  • Uninstall says why an app stays. When an app can't be removed (Windows, a game or Exo needs it), System › Apps says why beside its row.
  • A file in use names its program. When a game's update or uninstall fails on a file another program has open, the message names the programs holding it. Exo closes nothing; you decide.
  • Exo Shaders is on SCSKiller 1.4.6. Compiles that kept restarting or timing out now finish, Exo Shaders no longer uses SCSKiller's servers or Patreon, and it never works on a game with anti-cheat.
  • Riot hours are kept for good. Save your Riot ID in Settings › Library › Game stores and Exo reads its Valorant and League lifetime hours from tracker.gg once; after that Exo's own sessions add to them. A Riot game's page shows hours by mode and tracker.gg's stats, and Re-import from tracker.gg runs only when you press it. Ranked and Competitive count once, and a mode split never adds up above tracker.gg's total. When tracker.gg blocks a profile, Exo says so, and that game's seed stays empty until Re-import later succeeds.
  • Game clients step. After Welcome, Exo checks that Epic's new launcher (0.113 or later) and Steam's Beta are in place and offers the fix; the Game clients card says so on each start until they are. A Fix that fails says why (no connection, Steam didn't close, UAC declined) and offers Continue for now, which lets you go on until the next start; the library keeps working meanwhile. Fix says only what it will change on this PC.
  • Stores read their current layouts. Epic's install list and art come from its 0.113 cache, Riot installs are found by their product settings, a store folder that appears after Exo starts is picked up, and an installed store that finds no games says so in a quiet line.
  • Discord sign-in says what Exo is. The Discord room, first run and Add an account now say before the code that Exo connects as its own app, not Discord's official one, and that Discord's terms don't allow third-party clients and may lock an account it flags. The old note under the room's code is gone. Learn more opens the Discord accounts section of HELP: what Exo does to stay low-risk, the commonly reported lock triggers and how to appeal.
  • Discord reconnects back off longer. After a drop, the gateway waited a second and doubled up to a minute, but it started over at a second on every READY, so a connection that dropped right after each READY came back every second or two. The wait now starts over only after a connection that stayed up a minute; Discord's rate limit still waits a minute.
  • See who is in voice before you open a server. Hover a server in the Discord room and its name bubble lists each voice channel with people in it, their pictures overlapping (up to six, then how many more); the AFK channel is left out, as Discord does.
  • Unread conversations on the Discord rail. Under Home, up to five conversations with unread words show as round pictures, newest first, and leave the rail once you have read them. Home is Discord's own mark.
  • Mic and speakers are in Settings. The mute and deafen buttons on your card and in a call no longer open a device menu from a chevron; Settings › Discord › Voice holds the devices. The call bar is grouped into its controls, with its band under the call instead of over the tiles.
  • A junk clean that can't delete names the program. When PC › Apps or Graphics can't remove junk files because a program has them open, the message names that program, and the log records which files stayed.
  • Music sleeps when hidden. The hidden music player was refused its sleep while Windows was still telling it the view had hidden, so it kept its memory and logged a warning; Exo now waits for that.
  • Dictation and clip captions are gone. The mic in Notes, the words over a clip and the copy burned into an export are removed, and the speech model Exo downloaded is deleted.
  • Epic games start without Epic's window. Rocket League and other Epic titles Legendary already has start through Legendary, which opens nothing; Epic's own handoff remains the fallback.
  • Spotify's desktop app is closed each time Spotify starts. Exo closes a running Spotify app when its player opens (window close first, then the process if it stays after 3 seconds), so it can't take playback from Exo's player. When Spotify is playing on another device, the player says which and offers Play here.
  • Riot install check reads Riot's full answer. VALORANT's install state is read even when Riot wraps it in JSON, so a normal install no longer logs "remained unknown".
  • Exo Mouse uses OpenMouse's protocol 0.58.0. The newest release, checked against the Finalmouse drivers Exo Mouse patches (none of their files changed) and built with the same fixes.
  • Discord avatars and server icons fall back to an initial. A picture that fails to load shows its first letter on its tile.
  • Discord's type follows the design system. Names, statuses and titles use the set sizes, and chat bodies, embeds and the composer read at 15 px.
  • The Discord call bar has one icon size and one hit size. Icons are 20 px and their buttons 32 px.
  • Discord's Keybinds page has an empty state. With no keybinds yet it shows a keyboard icon and one sentence.
  • Discord sign-in reads cleanly. Learn more is a link, and the Discord mark sits at 70%.
  • Active Now names stay on one line. A name cut short says the rest in a tip.
  • Games without art show an initial. Library and Store put the game's first letter on a tinted tile.
  • The game sheet shows a skeleton while it loads. Its extras get a placeholder until they arrive.
  • The Bar shows its names down to 960 px wide. They used to hide at that width.
  • Store cards line up their titles. A fixed meta row keeps every title on the same line.
  • A signed-in Epic account never says Sign in. Its row in Settings › Library keeps the signed-in state.
  • The last Settings row clears the bottom fade. Nothing sits under it.
  • The PC page's facts fill their grid. No cell is left empty, and a drive's warning has its own row.
  • Devices shows its picture in a padded box. The picture is contained, not cropped.
  • Shader tiles let a game's name run to two lines.
  • Menus open from their trigger. They fade and scale out of the control that opened them.
  • The first click after Exo starts reaches the Bar. The boot splash takes no clicks once it starts to fade.
  • A continue card's Play stays inside its card at every size.

3.24.3 — November 5, 2026

  • The Discord room lines up. A server's and the DMs' lists end above the call and user panel, not under it; the "N new messages" bar sits above the conversation instead of over its first message; Friends' list keeps its width beside Active Now on a narrower window; a voice channel's count stays clear of its icons; the header search, a Jam invite, an embed and a voice chat's side panel fit their boxes at 1000 to 1920 px and large text. A geometry audit of the room ran in the dev preview, not in the installed app.
  • Crash reports are on by default, with no switch. When Exo hits an error, or you copy an error's log, it sends one short report to Exo's service. It holds Exo's version, your Windows build (numbers only), the error's type and a log excerpt of up to 8 KB, with paths, links, names, ids and tokens taken out on your PC first. No IP address or account is stored, and reports are deleted after 30 days. Settings › General no longer has the Send crash reports switch or its preview; Settings › About says in one line what is sent.
  • Unhandled crashes wait for the next start. An unhandled crash (an error that closes or breaks Exo) is saved on your PC in a pending report file and sent once, on the next start, under the same daily limits and scrubbing as every other report. Copying a log is unchanged.
  • Freezes are reported too. When Exo's window stops answering for more than 5 seconds while it is on screen, a report of the freeze goes to Exo's service the same way.
  • Failures are reported. A WebView2 browser that exits, Discord's connection or voice failing five times in a row, Exo Capture exiting with an error, a store launch or install failing, and an update failing each send one report.
  • Reports are limited. A PC sends at most one report of the same error and cause per UTC day, and five reports per UTC day in all. The count is kept in a local file with hashed keys only.

3.24.2 — November 5, 2026

  • Setup's Clean up scan starts later, and Apple Music's pages come back faster. Setup no longer scans for junk as it opens; the scan starts when setup reaches Discord, and Continue waits for it only on the step that leads into Clean up. A music page read in the last 20 seconds shows at once, and any action that changes your library forgets what was read before it.
  • Crash reports are yours to turn on, and off by default. Settings › General has Send crash reports. Turned on, copying an error's log also sends one scrubbed report of that error to Exo's service: the app's version, your Windows build, the error's type and a log excerpt with paths, links, names and ids taken out on your PC first. Preview shows the exact text before you decide, and nothing is sent while it's off.
  • The call bar has device menus and a quieter stream. The mic and speakers buttons have a chevron that opens their device menu, and a watched stream shows its controls only while the pointer is over it or a control has focus.
  • The call panel is one piece at the bottom left. A call's voice row and your user card are one glass panel on the same edges, with a single hairline between them; it grows and shrinks up from its bottom edge as a call starts and ends (Reduce motion: no motion), and the rail and sidebar leave room for it.
  • Captures is leaner, and a clip plays as soon as you open it. A clip starts on its own with the sound as you last left it (Reduce motion keeps it still). The list drops the game covers, the size on each card and the game's name under a game filter, and its groups sit closer together. With a clip open, Back takes the X's place in the header, and Escape or the mouse's Back button goes back to the list. The top bar's Capture button uses Tabler's capture icon.
  • HDR goes on for the screen your game is on. While a game with real HDR runs, HDR turns on for the screen its window is on, before the window opens, or for the screen it used last time, and turns back off a few seconds after it closes, so a restart doesn't flash. Nothing switches under a fullscreen game. HDR and the screen's gamma ramp come back after a crash, an update or a power-off.
  • Each game's HDR line says why. Exo's verdict in plain words: its own settings, what it saw on this PC, PCGamingWiki, or not known yet.
  • HDR for HDR games is on for new installs. Settings you already saved keep their choice.
  • A screen without HDR says so. PC > Display shows that the main screen can't show HDR, with no HDR switches.
  • Maximize answers again after a switch. The Maximize button responds after Exo is activated or restored from the taskbar, where it could stop answering.

3.24.1 — November 5, 2026

  • Discord looks like Discord. The room now follows the official app's sizes: the rail, the sidebar with Find or start a conversation, Friends and Shop, Direct Messages, a server's channels and voice members, the people with their counts, the title line's buttons, messages, the message box, the profile panel and the Friends page with Active Now. Your card lines up across the rail and sidebar with your microphone and speaker menus, and the Inbox no longer sits on the search.

3.24.0 — November 5, 2026

  • Tabler icons, everywhere. Every icon in Exo is now Tabler's (tabler.io/icons, version 3.50): light outline icons, each chosen to fit what it does, with solid play, pause and skip, in place of the hand-drawn set.
  • Discord is laid out like Discord. A rail of your servers runs down the left with Home on top, then the sidebar (Find or start a conversation, Friends, Shop and Direct Messages, or a server's channels), the conversation and the people, in Liquid Glass, with Geist and Tabler. Friends is back as its own page with Online, All, Pending and Active Now, your card sits across the foot of the rail and sidebar, and the Inbox is at the top right. The Lobby, the Servers page and Pin to top bar are gone, since the rail holds all your servers.
  • Changelog. What's New is now the Changelog: Settings › General opens every release, all the way back to 1.0.0.
  • Start maximized. Settings › General has a Start maximized switch. Off, Exo opens the way you left it, as before; on, it always opens maximized.
  • The FPS counter stays out of games that have their own. Over a game that draws its own FPS (VALORANT, Counter-Strike, Call of Duty, Fortnite and more), Exo's counter stays off and costs nothing while it would show only the FPS. Add items to it in Settings (frame time, 1% lows, GPU, CPU, memory) and it comes back over those games, since it then shows what theirs can't.
  • Spotify loads, or says why. If Spotify's player takes longer than 45 seconds to come up (a big library can), Exo reloads it twice and then tells you, in place of loading forever. It waits longer for a slow start, and closes the Spotify app when it starts its own player.

3.23.1 — November 5, 2026

  • Spotify loads every time. If the Spotify app was running, Exo's own Spotify player could fail to load ("Spotify is not answering"). Exo now closes the Spotify app when it starts its player, and ends it if it stays in the tray, so the player always comes up.

3.23.0 — November 3, 2026

  • Lucid comes with Exo. tekkusai's headphone EQ is now inside Exo, with his permission: nothing separate to find or download, no tray icon and no window, and your audio's page is how you run it. Set up says plainly what it will do before it does anything: on a PC without Equalizer APO that's a download, Windows' permission and a restart. Your settings carry over as they are. Lucid's start switch and its status are one row that says what it is doing, with Repair beside the switch (the one button that opens Lucid's own window), and Onboarding's row says it runs out of sight while Exo is open. Turning on Start Lucid with Exo moves you from your own copy to this one and stops yours starting at sign-in, unless yours is newer; your file is left where it is, and Apps > Startup says to remove a sign-in entry for the Lucid that comes with Exo, since Exo starts it. Lucid now closes when Exo closes: what you hear stays, but switching EQ per game waits for Exo. Exo doesn't fetch newer Lucid releases by itself; a newer Lucid comes with a newer Exo.
  • Lucid no longer holds Exo's folder. Lucid, which Exo runs out of sight, didn't answer Windows' polite close, so it stayed running after Exo closed and could stop an update, an install, an uninstall or a developer swap from replacing Exo's folder. Exo now asks it to close, waits a few seconds, and only then ends that one Lucid, and only if it runs from Exo's own folder: a Lucid you run from anywhere else is never closed or ended. The same happens when Exo closes, before an in-app update starts, and in the installer and uninstaller; if the update doesn't start, Lucid is started again, and after an update Exo starts it with itself as before. A change to Lucid's settings no longer fails with "Lucid didn't close" for the same reason.
  • Dictation in Notes, and captions on your clips, on this PC. Capture settings › Speech has one speech model (66 MB, fetched only when you press Get, asked in place; Remove takes it away) that runs on your processor and sends nothing anywhere. Then a mic in a note's tools types what you say while you hold it, or Ctrl+Shift+Space, and your microphone is open only while it's held; and Caption new clips, or Captions on any clip, finds a clip's words while no game plays and the PC is quiet, shows them over the video word by word, and burns them into For Discord, Full quality or Vertical only when Captions is on. Neither runs while a game plays. Exo Capture is updated with it.
  • Make reel joins a game's clips into one video. In Captures, a game's group has Make reel (its latest session's clips, or its starred ones when two or more are starred), and Select has Make reel for the clips you pick. Exo joins them in the order they were made: clips that match are copied as they are, with no re-encode, and only clips that differ are encoded again, fitted with black bars. It shows its progress with a Cancel and saves a new clip named for the game and the day, with Send to Discord and the rest. It never runs during a game (a running game offers Wait, and a game starting stops it), runs at low priority, and never touches the clips it was made from. Trim now keeps a clip's sound tracks in order, and For Discord and Vertical take the mix: Windows lists a file's tracks from the last to the first, so Trim wrote them in reverse and the two exports took your microphone's track instead. A clip's trim bar shows its frames along the bar, dims what the trim leaves out, and scrubs the video when you drag anywhere on it.
  • Play: Exo's own rooms for playing with friends. Turn on Show Play in Settings › Discord › Chat and the Discord room gets a fourth place with three rooms. Games shows each game with who is on it, and opens a room with its players, your time and achievements, Optimize and Shaders, and its clips. Party shows who is ready to play one game together (playing, ready, compiling shaders, installing), you first, with Join voice and Launch together, which sends nobody a message. Clips lists your captures with one press to share one to a friend or group, asking Send? first and offering Shrink to share over 25 MB. Friends on Exo see that a game is installing, building its shaders or ready only if you turn on Share when I'm getting ready in Settings › Discord › Privacy, which is off until you do. Home, Messages and Servers are unchanged.
  • Your game can reach friends on Exo, not Discord. Share my games, in Settings › Discord › Privacy, tells only friends who use Exo which game you opened from Exo, end-to-end encrypted through Exo's relay, which can't read it; Discord and its apps no longer show it. It is off until you turn it on, for each Discord account, and hidden games and games you added yourself are never named. The old "Through Exo Launcher" activity is gone, and Exo no longer changes Share my activity on your Discord account. If you turned Share my games on in 3.22.0, turn it on again: it works differently now. Friends who use Exo need this version to see each other.
  • Home is the Lobby, and a page you can leave open. On the page's black, places with the faces of who is there: Playing VALORANT, a voice room, Listening to music, each with one press to go there too.
