A place grows with who is in it, and everyone else on sits in Hanging out; a quiet night says so. Beside your friends' games are who is in voice across your servers with one press to join, your unread DMs and mentions with who, where and a line of the message, how long a game has been going, and what people online are listening to. Unread is a count that goes to Messages, and Add friend is one field and Send. The room says Discord again in the Bar, Ctrl+K and everywhere it names the room; only its first place inside stays Home.
  • Servers and Messages are worth looking at. Servers are bigger tiles with the server's banner, its mentions, an unread dot and the faces of who's in voice. Messages opens on your latest conversation without marking anything read, and each row says what was said last and when, with unread marks you can see and nameplates that no longer cover names; the list no longer touches the conversation, and ends on the message box's line. The Inbox opens on For you. Right-click any server, DM or group for Pin to top bar: it stays beside Home, Messages and Servers in your order, with its unread and mention marks, saved for that Discord account, and a server you've left or a DM that's gone disappears quietly.
  • A server's channels and people are always on screen. Channels down the left as Messages has its list, then the conversation, then the people, with no switcher, chips or people button; on a smaller window people give way first, then the channels fold into a dropdown on the title, and neither covers the chat. On a voice channel or a server's page the people column always fills in (it stayed empty before, since only a chat asked Discord for the list), with a quiet skeleton while it waits and another try if nothing comes. Pinned messages and notifications are in the three dots, which are as large as the icons beside them; the search is exactly as wide as the people or profile column under it; the top-right pill sits on the page's edge; and the current channel's mark lands on the channel instead of a row above it.
  • Conversations read better. Lines stop at a comfortable width in one centered column, a short conversation sits on the message box, a message shows its time alone with the date on hover, and your own name is quieter than theirs. A DM's profile stays closed until you press their name, and offline members can be read. A bot's embed colored black or nearly black no longer loses its left edge on the black page: the outline runs all the way round, with any real color as an accent inside it. Recording voice messages is gone from Exo's Discord, button and all; voice messages people send you still play.
  • A link made to look like Discord, Steam or PayPal asks first. In a message, a note or the picture viewer, a link whose address imitates a well-known one (disc0rd, steamcommunity.com.evil.ru, discord-nitro-gift, a Cyrillic letter in the name, a name before an @) turns into "Fake Steam link? Open anyway?" in place, and opens only on a second press. It checks the address's spelling on your PC against a short list of brands and looks nothing up.
  • Notifications are calmer and easier to answer. Three show at most and the rest wait their turn, each with a title, one short line and one button at most. A passing one stays as long as its words take to read, stays while you point at it or while Exo is out of sight, and goes with a swipe to the right, its ×, or Escape. Unpinning from Discord's top bar says so with Undo, in place; screen readers hear each one once.
  • Achievement banners, redone. One solid black card with the achievement's real picture, large and sharp, its name in white, what it asked of you, and how far along you are and how few players have it. Each rarity has its own moment, longer the rarer: common lands, rare runs silver along the edge, epic drops in with weight, legendary opens from its picture on the hit in gold, and finishing a game takes eleven seconds over three hits. Steam's pictures had been asked for at an old address that now answers nothing, so a real unlock showed a broken picture; Exo now keeps each game's icons from when the game starts, so a banner never waits on the network and always has a picture, down to the game's own art. Banners fit at every display scale and follow Exo's Reduce motion rather than Windows'. A deep thock replaces the chime: heavy, dampened and made here, kept ready instead of made per banner, with its weight below 250 Hz and enough knock above that to read on small speakers. Rarer is deeper and longer, the top tiers build into a second and third low hit, and the volume slider still sets it.
  • Auto tune for your graphics card. PC has a new Auto tune chip where Exo can do it: one press finds a lower voltage or a faster clock for a GeForce or a Radeon, tests each step with its own check, and shows what it measured before and after. It's off until you press it and never part of Best settings, it warns you first about your warranty and crashes, it goes back to stock by itself if a game starts or anything goes wrong, and Undo is always one press. Ryzen processors are not offered yet: that route needs a driver, PawnIO, that no anti-cheat has been shown to accept.
  • AMD's Fluid Motion Frames 3 preview, if you want it. On a Radeon RX 7000 or 9000 with AMD Software 26.10.2 or later, PC > Graphics has one switch for AMD's unreleased preview, labelled as that. It's off until you press it and off again in one press, puts back what was there, isn't part of Best settings, and takes hold when AMD Software restarts. It rests on one community report, and nobody outside AMD knows all it changes. When AMD releases it, the switch goes and Exo takes its value off. Other cards see nothing.
  • Optimize covers 12 more games, follows BenchmarKing, and Applied now means it. Red Dead Redemption 2, Cyberpunk 2077, Alan Wake 2, Dying Light: The Beast, Dying Light 2, Crimson Desert, Star Wars Outlaws, Oblivion Remastered, Metal Gear Solid Delta and Remnant II take BenchmarKing's settings for your card, and Counter-Strike 2 and Path of Exile 2 get your window mode held. Where he has measured a game, its graphics follow his guide: Modern Warfare 4 takes his competitive list, so the costly settings stay low while what costs almost nothing, like screen space shadows and occlusion, comes back up; CONTROL Resonant takes his values too, with 8 GB cards on lighter textures; Hell is Us gets Optimize for the first time, with his settings, DLSS Quality with Reflex on an RTX card, your window mode held, and DLSS Frame Generation kept on when Exo's frame generation is in the game. Rocket League has no guide from him and stays as it was. The line says Applied only when Exo found and wrote everything it asked for, and Partly optimized or Settings not found when it couldn't. A game whose settings Steam Cloud keeps gets nothing written.
  • Optimize lets you choose Borderless or Fullscreen. On Modern Warfare 4, Phantom Blade Zero and CONTROL Resonant, the Optimize line now has the choice, remembered for each game, and picking one applies Optimize with that mode. Exo holds the mode you pick, setting it back as the game closes, as Exo starts and just before Play if it has changed, until you put it back. Rocket League keeps its display mode with your account, so it has no choice.
  • No more frame caps from Exo. Optimize no longer caps Modern Warfare 4 or Rocket League under the refresh, the AMD and Intel profiles no longer cap frames on a FreeSync or Adaptive Sync screen, and competitive games no longer get a G-SYNC override in their NVIDIA profiles. A cap or override Exo wrote goes back to what it held the next time you press Optimize, pick a mode or put it back, or when Exo starts.
  • Games with Exo's frame generation start again when they were recording. The shader recorder and DLSSG SM86 together could hang a game before its window opened, as in Hell is Us. Now recording stops in such a game before it launches.
  • Frame generation stays where you have it, and starts every game. Opening a game's page could take DLSS Frame Generation out of it on an RTX 20 or 30 card, as it did in Phantom Blade Zero; now only a different graphics card, anti-cheat or a game update takes it out. When a session left no log, Exo renamed the mod's file, and a game that needs the real DLL of that name then wouldn't start at all; Exo no longer renames it and puts back any it renamed. On a game's page, what Exo's frame generation put in the game no longer lists under Upscalers, and the Frame generation line names it and its release, such as DLSSG SM86 0.4.1.
  • Best settings is a green check or a red X. Each step now says only whether it's set, and Use best settings sets the rest. The Undo rows for the power plan, network and drives are gone.
  • Weekly optimize counts a disabled service as off. Windows' optimize task could read as ready with its service disabled, which also stops Optimize Drives opening. Exo now shows that as not set, and Use best settings puts the service back to Manual.
  • PC > Apps is one clear list, and Uninstall clears up after itself. Every app with its publisher, when you last used it, its size and whether it starts with Windows, sorted by pressing a column. Uninstall shows on the app you point at, not on all of them, and what Windows, games and Exo need waits out of the way. Pressing Uninstall is all it takes: once the program's own uninstaller is done, what it left goes by itself, its folders too, and one line says what went and how much space came back. Windows asks once for the whole clear-up. Anything shared with another program, in use, or matched only by its name stays and says why, and anti-cheat, drivers and Windows' own are never touched. Removed lists what Exo took off this PC, and Put back returns what was cleared: files from the Recycle Bin, registry entries from a saved copy; leftovers in Program Files go to the Recycle Bin too, instead of being deleted for good.
  • Exo has its own icon by the clock, and Steam's really leaves it. While Exo runs, its icon sits in the taskbar's hidden icons, where Windows puts new ones: press it to open Exo, right-press it for Open and Quit; closing Exo closes the icon with it. Exo said it kept Steam's notification-area icon hidden, but Windows records programs in Program Files by a folder id, which Exo never read, so the icon stayed, and so did those of other stores installed there; Exo now reads that id and removes them while it runs them, and NVIDIA's tray icon goes again after a fix for Steam's had stopped Exo finding it.
  • Epic runs lighter while Exo has it. When Exo starts the new Epic launcher for you, it now asks for Epic's own low-resources preset and turns off the debug log it writes all session, once; one you change back in Epic stays as you left it, and uninstalling Exo puts both back.
  • Modern Warfare 4 is in your Library by name, and Ctrl+K finds games by short names. Steam has one Call of Duty app and sells each game as a DLC of it, so MW4 only ever showed as "Call of Duty®". That card now takes the name and art of the newest Call of Duty you own, Ctrl+K finds it by "mw4", "cod mw4" or "modern warfare 4", and by the name of any other Call of Duty you own, and the Store's page for any of them leads to it. It's still one card: one install, one Play, the same hours. Ctrl+K now also finds any game by its short name, such as "bg3".
  • Games you own say Library, not a price. On a Store card and a Store game's page, a game you already have, on any store, through Steam Family, installed or not, shows a Library button with an arrow in place of its price and Get on Steam. It opens the game in your Library. Owning an old game no longer takes the price off a remake of the same name on Steam. The Store's calendar shows every game on its day, the ones you own and Early Access too, with nothing to set, and a store page's About is Steam's whole description, cleaned to plain paragraphs and lists, where it was one short line.
  • Exo plays Spotify in its own player. Spotify stays a music service, always through Exo's built-in player; Exo no longer uses, starts, installs or offers the Spotify app, its switch and install button are gone from Settings › Music, and one you run yourself is left alone. Signing in to Apple Music holds too: Apple's window handed the player its session and the player dropped it a moment later, because its own cookie for it was missing; it is saved there now, so the sign-in lands in the room and is still there next time.
  • Listen along keeps in step, and leaves your Spotify Jam alone. If your player stalls or drifts away from your friend's, Listen along now moves it back (more than 1.5 s off for a song from their Exo, 5 s from their Discord activity), and aims each seek a little ahead for the time it takes to land. In a Spotify Jam, Listen along on Spotify no longer starts, since it would change what the whole Jam plays.
  • Your controller stops forgetting. Calibration and settings seemed to reset for two reasons, both fixed. Exo knows a controller by its serial, and one serial read that failed (it has three seconds, on a busy PC at Exo's start) stood until the next unplug: Exo then couldn't find what it had saved, sent nothing, drew the page's defaults and a white controller, and saved new changes aside where the next run couldn't see them. Now it asks again and folds any such file back in by itself, newest choices first. And after a range calibration an Edge doesn't answer for a moment, so Exo kept the calibration from before it, and that older one is what went back on each connect; it now keeps what it wrote, and reads it back when it connects. Calibrate once more after this update: your last one was never saved. An Edge's stick calibration can't stay on the controller from any app, since its stick modules are write-protected until it's opened and modified, so it lasts until unplugged and Exo sends it back each time it connects; a controller's page has What Exo remembers to say what's saved, whether it holds your calibration right now, and when it last went back on.
  • A controller's page, filled in, and your Edge is black. Battery, measured polling rate, color and firmware in the hero, the polling rate measured as you open it. Where each stick sits right now, and your last stick test with the deadzone that hides its drift. How far each trigger has gone and an Edge's stop sliders. An Edge's back and Fn buttons lit while held, and the profiles it holds and which is on. Nothing is read while the page isn't on screen. A DualSense's serial carries its color (an Edge's says Midnight Black), and Exo draws it so, lifted a little with one hairline around it so it holds its shape on a black page, no glow. The controller's and audio's pictures, names and facts stand in the same rail as Exo Mouse's, at the same width, the room's whole height.
  • The Controller Lab tests triggers and buttons. A trigger test says at least how many of 256 steps each trigger sends and whether it covers its travel. A button test counts presses and chatter, and says what's too quick to see. The stick test now runs five seconds and tells drift from noise. Polling shows whether reports come in batches, and a pad that sends only on a change reads "At least", with its idle gaps no longer read as stutter.
  • Your audio's page, filled in. How Exo Voice is set, and for your headphones and microphone a real Volume slider, Mute, and Format: the formats that device really takes, as Windows' Sound settings set them. Exo reads the new format back and says so if it didn't change. All of it works during a game or a call. Reading formats and volume was checked on this PC's real devices; a format change hasn't been tried on real hardware.
  • The mouse test checks a wireless mouse and says how your reports arrive. On a receiver or Bluetooth, the test counts its dropouts and says how they fall: across the test, in one stretch, or on a steady beat. When there are some, it says what's nearby (Wi-Fi on 2.4 GHz, connected Bluetooth devices, a USB 3 device on the receiver's controller, and that a short USB extension may help) and compares with your last test; it reads gaps between reports, not the radio, so it never calls any of these the cause. Devices' CPU core panel also reads how steady the reports are, whether they come in batches, how full the mouse's slots are and whether a late report's skipped slots turned up right after or went missing, and says these are the times Windows handed them over, not input lag. A test Exo's own memory cleanup disturbed asks to be run again instead of offering a fix, and the panel's tests and changes wait until your game is closed.
  • The mouse test can name the driver, and spread your other devices. When your mouse's reports come late, Record runs the test again while Windows records what its drivers did on the mouse's CPU, in one permission prompt. Exo names a driver only when its own time there explains the gap, gives its version and who aimed its interrupts, and otherwise says nothing explains it; a recording that Windows cut short or that won't line up with your mouse names nobody, and some anti-cheat blocks the recording, which Exo says. When other devices take their interrupts on the mouse's CPU, Devices offers Spread them: network, audio and spare USB controllers each move to a quiet core, one permission prompt and a restart, off the mouse's half of a dual-CCD Ryzen, with Undo. It isn't part of Best settings, it waits until your game is closed, and it says it's a rule of thumb, so measure again after.
  • Devices has a USB map. Every USB controller and what hangs from it, each device's link speed, the hubs it goes through and the polling rate it asks for, and who shares a controller with your mouse, keyboard or controller. It names no sockets, because Windows can't say which is which, and says a controller's place is an estimate. The hub reads were checked against this PC's real USB.
  • Exo Mouse's hero takes Exo's rail. Inside Exo, Exo Mouse's left side is the width Exo sends; on its own it still takes what its columns leave.
  • Every icon, redrawn by hand as one set. All of Exo's icons and Exo Mouse's few are new drawings: a lighter line that matches the text beside it at every size, plainer shapes with less inside them, the same folder, person, mic and bell wherever they appear, and one place for a small plus, check or lock. Nothing moved and nothing was renamed. The PC room is a PC, not a gauge; Servers are a server rack, not a camera; forums are a written bubble; threads are a message off a line instead of a hatched smudge; Uninstall is the trash, not a share arrow; Select games, Listening, Mark as spoiler and Downloaded use the icons those things have everywhere else; Ctrl+K marks voice, private and announcement channels as their list does; sound off has the same slash as mic off; and a muted mic looks the same in a call as in the band. Settings is a gear again, with six large teeth, so it stays a gear down to the smallest size it's drawn.
  • Comfortable, not bare. The last clean-up cut too far, so the facts people come for are back, easy to read, with room around them; taglines and repeated labels stay gone. A game's page has played, last played, storage and achievements as large facts with their names under them ("12 of 50", with a still bar). The Library keeps covers bare until you point at or focus one, then a small plate gives its name, hours and storage; Continue says hours, last played and Update ready again, and All games and each folder say how many they hold. PC has every part's numbers back: cores and threads, video memory and DirectX, memory speed and slots, refresh, sync and HDR, the BIOS, link speed, Windows against its newest, and each drive's free of total, life and heat with a still bar, with a warning under them instead of replacing them. A Store card shows its price with any discount (or its date, or Free) and what players think; the Wishlist counts, a Steam sale on now or within the week is named, and the banner says why it picked a game. Shaders shows a game's name under its poster; Captures and Notes show date and size on every card; Music's Library rows say whose playlist it is; Settings has a short plain line where a setting's name doesn't say it all; Ctrl+K names its groups again; and secondary words are brighter everywhere (Muted #b4b4b4, Faint #9a9a9a). The monitor's color sliders in Display now match the rest of Settings.
  • What's loading now looks like what's coming. Skeletons in every room take the exact shape of the rows, cards and pages they stand in for, so things stay where they are when the real content lands, and they rest while Exo is in the background or a room is out of sight. A long release date on a Store card now ends in an ellipsis instead of being cut off.
  • Exo starts lighter, and rooms answer quicker. A start no longer walks your Unreal games' folders for a Settings switch, or fetches Store pages from Steam for a room you haven't opened, the QR code and new-folder code load only when you open them, and your audio device stops being read while Exo is hidden or behind a game; the Shaders page lists your games at once instead of after about four seconds. PC and Upscalers no longer queue their reads behind a slow thread pool, Discord's account page and its shop ask for your profile once and together, Exo Capture waits longer between tries when your graphics card's encoder isn't ready instead of retrying every 3 seconds, and a Library update that one part of Exo fails to hear no longer keeps the others from it. An Apple Music playlist or album opens in about half the time, since the page draws as soon as Apple answers with its songs; the hearts and library state follow a moment later. Game runtimes no longer wait on Microsoft: the first showing waits at most a second and a half, shows the last answer or the built-in versions, and finishes the lookup behind the page (installing still waits for the real answer). The junk scan counts its kinds side by side, measures shader caches only when they are old, and is shared when Onboarding and Apps ask at the same moment. A Store page waits less when your connection blinks: when Windows briefly can't find Steam's address, Exo asks again after 0.3, 0.7 and then 1 second, and the calendar reads what you play beside Steam's lists instead of after them. A slow call in app.log now ends with what it was for, such as key=view:playlist, and never an id, a name, a path or any text.
  • First launch says what it's doing. A Shaders poster now says Opening the game as soon as you confirm, and one that fails says Did not start.
  • Community recordings say the truth. Settings could read No supporter plan while your Patreon was downloading recordings fine; it no longer says that.
  • The FPS counter is only ever over games. Settings' Preview, which drew it over whatever was in front, is gone.

3.22.0 — November 2, 2026

  • Home and Music, with less in the way. A server is one page now: the conversation fills the width, the channel you're in opens every channel from a panel (folding, voice, threads and all), and what else wants you sits beside it on one line. Member lists and profiles open when you ask, from the person's name in a DM. Inbox and your microphone and speakers moved to your corner of the band; Now is games and who's online; Friends holds requests and everyone. Music starts on your music, with a quieter library and player.
  • PC, Shaders, Captures and Exo Mouse say less. This PC is one line per part, with a second line only for what you act on: a drive's free space, or what's wrong. Updates read "581.42 → 581.57". Best settings has a Use on every step, your motherboard opens its own menu (BIOS, Recovery, Secure Boot, the TPM) and the Control Panel chip on a GeForce gave way to the card's and the screen's own menus. Shaders' posters are their art and state, with its time on the title line; startup apps say what stops without them only where that matters.
  • The Library, a game's page, the Store and first run say less. Covers stand alone with no names under them and Continue has no heading or hours; a game's facts are one line and Uninstall moved into Properties, which lost its title and close button; the Library's tools are the sort menu and More, which also holds Select games and Refresh library; the Store's banner, cards and top line lost their extra words; first run shows a row only for what you can do. Friends' games now live on Home alone, and everything else is still where a press finds it.
  • Settings is labels and controls. Rows lost their explanations and second lines, and a value now sits beside its button. About joined General, Discord's call and message sounds are one list under Notifications, Developer Mode moved to Chat, Show tools and soundtracks moved to Library, and Discord's soundboard has one slider where Off is mute. Ctrl+K now finds Settings by what's in it.
  • Coming back to Music just works. After a music service's page has been closed for being idle, the next page you open or button you press loads it and carries on, instead of saying it isn't answering.
  • The volume slider never hangs. Moving it answers at once, even while the music page is waking or nothing is playing; it used to hold for 24 seconds.
  • Discord is now Home, and leads with your friends' games. The room is called Home, in the Bar, Ctrl+K and everywhere it names the room. It opens on what your friends are playing, with Play, Install or Store for your PC, then who is online.
  • No more server rail. A band across the top holds Home, Messages and Servers with their counts, then named chips for what wants you now, then you. Servers is a page of named tiles you can search, drag and fold; everything the rail did is still there.
  • Share my games. Turn it on in Home, or in Settings › Discord › Privacy, and friends see the game you opened from Exo as your Discord activity, in any Discord app, and on their Home if they use Exo, marked Through Exo. It stays off until you turn it on, and off if Share my activity is off on your Discord account; hidden games and games you added yourself are never named. It goes through your own Discord connection, with no server of Exo's.
  • An FPS counter over your games. Turn it on in Settings › Library: one small line, "142 FPS", flush against the top of the game's screen, in the top right corner, the top middle or the top left. Switches add 1% low, frame time, GPU use and temperature, CPU use, VRAM, RAM and the time. It counts frames through Windows itself, with nothing put in the game, so anti-cheat has nothing to see; it works at once with no restart, does nothing while the game isn't in front, and stays out of your recordings and streams.
  • Apple Music's sign-in reaches the player. Exo's blocker was taking a tracking-looking part off Apple's own sign-in address, so its window said you were signed in and closed, and Music stayed signed out. It leaves Apple's addresses alone now.
  • Your music service is chosen in Settings. The service buttons left the top left of Music; pick Spotify, Apple Music or YouTube Music in Settings › Music. A signed-out Music room or one that won't open has Change music service, which goes straight there.
  • Your headphones stay picked. A headphone or other Lucid change made during a game now waits on disk and goes in within seconds of the game ending, even if Exo restarts first; it used to be forgotten.
  • Lucid's window comes when you ask. If Lucid's window doesn't appear after Open, Exo asks again.
  • Minecraft and Roblox have art on Home. A game friends are in now gets its Microsoft Store banner when it isn't on Steam or Epic.
  • Clicking away keeps the arrow. The empty space around a sheet or Discord's settings no longer shows the hand.
  • The PC room is still in a small window. Under 1280 by 800 Shaders' posters resized on every frame, chasing their own height.
  • Updates takes only the room it needs. With everything up to date it is just its title line, not a large empty box.
  • Friends' games with no art of their own. A modpack or anything else with only Discord's small picture fills its card with that picture, blurred, instead of a tiny icon in a blank card; a name with the word Minecraft in it gets Minecraft's banner.
  • A store page's small words read clearly. Fact names and system requirements are brighter over a busy Store behind the page.
  • A press right after Ctrl+K lands where you aimed. Picking a game and pressing its page at once closed the page, because the search box fading out took the press.
  • The PC room's drives show at once. A drive's row came about seven seconds after the rest, while Exo walked the disk for a detail that row only uses on a hard drive.
  • A store page has no empty row. The other stores now sit under Wishlist and appear only if one sells the game.
  • No nameless DLC. A DLC Steam gives no name for and that can't be switched no longer shows as a number in Properties.
  • A better volume icon. The top player's speaker now shows one wave or two with the level.

3.21.0 — November 1, 2026

  • Noise suppression is built in. Exo's own AI noise suppression (DeepFilterNet) now ships inside the app, so it cleans your microphone from the first call with nothing to download; if it ever can't start, Windows' noise suppression takes over.
  • Streams keep playing. A friend's stream that paused, or stopped in picture-in-picture and never came back, now asks Discord for a fresh picture each time you open or reattach it, so it picks up again within a moment.
  • No false voice warnings. The "noise suppression couldn't be set up" notice and the red "your mic seems silent" warning no longer show while your mic and noise suppression are working.
  • Settings has its sidebar again. Settings lists each room first (Library, Discord, Music) and then Exo's own pages (General, About), and Discord's settings now live there.
  • Dictation and profiles are gone. Dictation and Exo profiles are removed; Exo deletes dictation's 670 MB speech model from your PC on its next start.

3.20.0 — October 31, 2026

  • Exo sleeps while you play. With a game running and Exo hidden, its page suspends entirely (no drawing, no timers) and comes back exactly where you left it; if a suspend can't finish or is cancelled, the page simply stays awake.
  • Every cover arrives at the size it's drawn. The Library grid, your profile, folders, the Exo Store and Discord's Tonight now ask for a cover scaled to its card instead of the full file, so pages dense with covers open faster and use less memory.
  • Motion and glass match your PC. Exo reads your graphics card, its memory and your screen's refresh rate and sets its own motion tier: a light PC draws the Solid material and simpler motion by itself, with nothing to set.
  • Exo moves as one piece. Every spring is solved once for your screen and baked into the page's own timing, and a thrown mini-stream tile flies home at the speed you let it go.
  • A new version shows up within a minute. Exo's update address kept its answer about the newest version for five minutes, so a just-shipped release could stay invisible for a while; the answer now turns over within a minute, while the release files themselves keep their long cache.

3.19.1 — October 31, 2026

  • Go Live reaches full quality on a line that always drops a little. A connection that asks for a few percent of the stream again at every rate, even a wired one, held Go Live at 0.5 Mbit/s for the whole stream while Discord's server would take 9. Exo now learns the share your line always loses, counts only what goes beyond it as congestion, and climbs back to the stream's full rate within about a minute.
  • The Steam overlay switch is back. A Steam game's Properties › General has its Steam overlay switch again, and what you pick sticks, Call of Duty included. If you change it in Steam afterwards, Steam's choice wins.
  • Every DLC you own has its switch. DLC you own but haven't downloaded showed no switch, since Exo only asked what was on disk; Exo now asks Steam which DLC you own. With Steam closed, a DLC row says Open Steam to change instead of standing blank.
  • Stream controls in the voice bar. Beside Disconnect at the bottom left of Discord: share your screen, and while you're live, change what you share or stop streaming, without opening the call.
  • Roomier menus. Menus are wider, with taller rows and more space around them, in the Library, its sort menu and Discord.

3.19.0 — October 31, 2026

  • One browser engine for Exo's apps. Exo Launcher, Exo Cinema and Exo OS now run their pages in one shared WebView2 instead of one each, every app on its own profile so nothing of one app's data reaches another's. With all three open that is about 580 MB less memory (1,349 to 772 MB measured), and Exo Cinema opens about 45% faster when Launcher is already running. Launcher moves its own folder into the shared one once, whole, so your music sign-ins stay; the others join only after that, and an app on its own keeps working exactly as before. Launcher trims and lowers only its own pages, and leaves the shared engine alone while Cinema or Exo OS is in front, so a film never stutters because Launcher went to the background.
  • Exo never takes the focus from a game as it starts. Started while a game runs or a full-screen window is in front, Exo opens without coming to the front, as it already did after an update during a game.
  • Steps back further while you play. While Exo is in the background or a game runs, its own disk reads (library scans, covers) yield to the game's, and nothing Exo starts while playing inherits its lowered priority. On PCs where a tweak tool turned Windows' power throttling off, Exo says efficiency mode is unavailable there once and keeps the rest. Store downloads run behind the game too: Epic downloads use the fastest CDN, with download workers sized to your PC.
  • Quiet microphones no longer cut out. A microphone whose own gain can't go up reached noise suppression and the gate as a whisper, so a friend's voice was cut out. Exo now hears talking that stands well above the room even when the gate held it back, and lifts such a microphone on its own, up to 18 dB, before suppression and the gate decide; no Voice Tune needed.
  • Clips mix themselves. Each source in a clip's mix is levelled on its own and a soft limiter keeps the whole from clipping, so a loud microphone no longer drowns the game; each source's own track stays as recorded.
  • Startup apps cleaned up for real. PC › Apps › Startup has Clean up: it turns off every startup entry Exo knows isn't needed and the hidden scheduled tasks apps and Windows add (Windows' telemetry tasks included, which aren't listed and can't be put back), in one prompt, and says which one didn't take. Entries of apps you've uninstalled are deleted for good. Apps Exo itself uses (Spotify, Xbox) can't be uninstalled from there.
  • One press, one answer. Across Exo a press asks at most once, on the button itself, and its result shows where you pressed instead of a pop-up or a toast; a panel's own actions sit on its title line in every room.
  • The PC room on one page. Drives sit in This PC with their fixes in Best settings, Shaders has room for everything, Apps opens as a sheet, the NVIDIA driver row shows only when there's an update, the Control Panel button opens your card's own panel (NVIDIA's, AMD Software or Intel's), and game runtimes list only the ones that need an update. Intel Arc gets the GPU scheduling row its frame generation needs, a laptop's Ethernet keeps its energy saving, and the Exo OS chip shows how many fixes Exo OS has ready, or Sign in.
  • Achievements in game. Settings › Achievements: banners for Steam, Epic and GOG unlocks over your game (exclusive full screen too), their volume, a preview, and an OBS browser source to show them on stream.
  • Tonight in Discord. The Discord room opens on Tonight: who's playing what, with the games' art. The Bar's profile button is a person glyph that sits in the pill's curve.
  • Go Live fits your screen. Go Live offers no frame rate above what the shared screen draws, and a saved higher rate streams at the screen's own.
  • Safer pages. Your captures, note photos, covers and thumbnails are served only to Exo's own page, so no site shown inside Exo can read them.
  • Exo's other apps work with Launcher. exolauncher:// links open a room or part of PC from Exo Browser, Exo Cinema or Exo OS; Launcher shares your installed games and when you play them (games.json) so Exo OS can keep games off power throttling and time Windows Update around your play; Appearance (Light, Dark, Void) is shared with them. Exo's idle work checks the processor's thread count, so a 4-core PC can idle and a 32-thread PC isn't called idle under load. Ctrl+K finds AMD Software and Intel's graphics app.
  • Menus and Settings. Menus never rise over the Bar, and Settings' column fades at its scroll edges. Newest dependencies throughout (OpenMouse protocol 0.49.0, GameInput 4.0.306 and the rest).

3.18.1 — October 29, 2026

  • Exo Shield blocks ads in the music players again. 3.18.0 shipped a newer Exo Shield than the copy it checks it against, so on a new install or an update from 3.17 it refused to install it, and ads came back in YouTube Music and the other players. It now checks against the copy it ships, and what the browser writes into Exo Shield's folder after loading it no longer counts as a change, so a later check can't refuse it either.

3.18.0 — October 29, 2026

  • Update before you start, never in the middle. When a newer Exo is out as Exo opens, a small sheet in What's New's look says Update available and the new version, with one button, Update now: it downloads with its bar, checks the download against the release, installs and opens the new version, and What's New then shows what changed. A version released while Exo is already open never interrupts you; it waits for the next start, and nothing checks again until then but Settings › About. Offline, or on a developer build, nothing shows. If the update fails, the sheet says why, with Try again and Not now, which keeps your version until Exo next opens; an update that didn't take the last time offers both at once. While the update sheet or What's New is up, nothing behind it scrolls or takes a key or a press, the window's own buttons still work, and Update now or Continue gives everything back. A sheet or page Exo opens by itself while it is in the background no longer brings its window to the front over your game: it takes the keyboard once you switch to Exo.
  • First run ends with Best settings for this PC. Setup's This PC step was one switch for Exo's driver profile, applied unseen as setup closed. Its last step is now Best settings for this PC, the same steps as the PC room's (Exo's power plan for your processor, its driver profile for your card, your network adapter's settings, your drives' write-cache flushing and weekly optimize, HDR while an HDR game runs, RTX Video HDR on a screen running HDR), each a row: Use best settings runs them in turn, each row turning its ring and then taking a green check, and Not now leaves them for PC. Clean up comes before it, and Start using Exo after. Nothing in setup asks about what Exo now does by itself (Game Mode, Anti-Lag, Fluid Motion Frames, Steam's cloud saves, overlay, controller and updates). An update found as Exo opens now comes before setup instead of after it, so setup is always the newest version's.
  • Exo Setup in your Appearance. Exo Setup's window was black whatever Exo looks like. It now draws in your Appearance (Light, Dark or Void, from Exo's settings; on a first install, Light or Dark as Windows' apps are), its window frame and edge too, and follows Exo's Reduce motion (on a first install, Windows' animation setting): with it on, a screen only fades in. Installed no longer says "Exo is ready." under it. Settings › About and the update's notice now say "Downloading · 54%" then "Installing", as the update sheet does, instead of "Downloading…".
  • Exo animates unless you ask it not to. With Windows' animation effects off, Exo treated that as Reduce motion even with its own Reduce motion switch off, so trailers didn't play and the motion stayed still. Settings › General › Reduce motion now decides on its own; Windows' setting only stands in for the moment before your settings load.
  • Light looks like Light. Sheets and dialogs (Settings, a game's page and its Properties, the Store's game pages, Captures, PC › Apps, What's New, Discord's settings, Ctrl+K) dimmed the page to a flat mid gray under a gray frost, so everything looked washed out. On Light the dim is now a quarter of Void's and the sheet a near-white frost that stands clear of it, and the Bar steps back with the page. Games you haven't installed recede under a veil of the page instead of a black dim, their download mark in Light's frost, and the Library's done check, a trailer's play mark, Music's Play buttons (black on black before) and a picked capture's check all show. Discord's NEW line reads white on its red, a super reaction and the unsaved-changes flash tint toward white instead of black, and a face in a call casts a soft shadow instead of a dark smudge. In every Appearance, a sheet left open in a room you switched away from no longer keeps the Bar dimmed.
  • No more grey notes. The small grey lines explaining a setting, pointing somewhere else or reassuring you are gone across Exo, or turned into the value you'd want ("Copied", "Graphics card · After a restart", "Playlists only"); grey now means a value, a result, progress or an error. What's New's lines read in full white under each title.
  • Other sites' pages never show Edge's screens. A music or store page inside Exo that can't load says why in Exo's own card ("Can't find music.youtube.com", "The connection to … isn't private") with Try again, and a page that wants to open another app asks first in Exo's look, naming the site and the app; links Windows has seen abused never open from a page.
  • Nothing pops up to ask. Uninstall, delete, decline, sign out, stop and the rest, in Discord and Exo Mouse too, ask on the button you pressed: it turns into the question ("Uninstall? 53 GB", "Restart Steam offline?"), in red when it destroys something, a second press does it, and Escape, a press elsewhere or a few seconds puts it back. In a menu the row asks and the menu stays open; a choice with more than two answers opens a menu from the button. A question that takes a field (a rename, an import, a choice of version) opens where you pressed: in its row, or hanging from the button, never in a box in the middle of the screen. The one question Exo's own window still asks, before it removes another app for good, is drawn in your Appearance with Exo's corners and type, with no Windows blue.
  • Settings without a sidebar. Settings, a game's Properties and Discord's settings (and a channel's and a server's) are one scrolling column now, in a narrower sheet the same size in all of them: no list of sections down the side taking a third of the sheet. Opening one at a section, from search or a link, brings that section to the top. Server settings load each section from Discord only as you reach it.
  • Play time counts every session. A game started from its store, or while Exo was closed, now counts from the moment it started; restarting Exo mid-game loses nothing; and a game's page shows Exo's own count when the store counted less, as Steam does in offline mode. Phantom Blade Zero showed 2 minutes after hours of play: Steam went offline minutes in, and Exo counted only games it launched itself.
  • Steam play while Exo was closed counts. Exo reads Steam's own record of the games it ran and adds each session Exo didn't count, once, from when you installed Exo, at start and as each Steam game closes.
  • Library's Refresh shines like the Store's. Your covers shine for the whole refresh, as the Store's tiles do, and for one full sweep at least: they stopped once the stores were read again, a third of a second in, before the shine could show.
  • Continue's Play is white again. The Play button on Continue's tiles had turned to dark glass like the More beside it; it's solid white again, as on a game's page, the one thing to press on the tile.
  • The mini player's song name opens Music wherever you press it. Its seek strip covered the lower half of the name, so a press there moved the song instead.
  • Similar to reads clearly over bright art. A pick's Similar to badge gets the deeper dim other badges over art have.
  • Exo's window comes back by itself after a graphics driver install. A driver install resets the graphics card, and on a Radeon Exo's window could stay white until Exo was restarted: nothing reloaded its page, and AMD's setup says it's done before its install is. Exo now waits for AMD's install to really end (AMD's installer and Windows' driver installer gone, and AMD's result or the new version in), reloads its page once any driver install is in, then puts your settings back. Whenever the page stops drawing it comes back within seconds: no new frame after the graphics card resets, or a page that crashed or hangs, reloads it, and a browser under it that stopped is started again. If that happens three times in a minute, Exo says it needs a restart, with Restart, now readable in Light too (its words were white).
  • No NVIDIA hotfix drivers for now. PC › Graphics and Exo's driver watch offer NVIDIA's newest Game Ready driver again, not a newer hotfix: hotfix 621.12 still left Phantom Blade Zero flickering, and the game's studio points NVIDIA players at 617.42.
  • Updates download from Exo's own servers. Exo's update and the website's Download button now come from Exo's own copy on Cloudflare instead of GitHub, which only fills in for the minutes before a new release's copy is up. Every Exo version gets this, since the update address does it.
  • Exo Capture records more games, and is Exo's own. For a game in exclusive full screen, the part of Exo Capture that takes frames through OBS's game hook is written anew. A game that draws into a window inside its main one is now captured, OpenGL games come out the right way up instead of upside down, a frame that isn't ready yet is asked for again instead of failing, a stuck helper can no longer hold capture from starting, and if Windows can't list what's running, the hook isn't used rather than risking an anti-cheat game. Exo Capture now comes under its own usage terms like the rest of Exo (capture/LICENSE); OBS's signed game capture files it runs keep their own license.
  • Your call stays out of your clips. Every instant replay and recording held the voices of the Discord call you were in, and while instant replay ran, Exo told everyone in the call you had clips on, so in a call with a friend who uses Exo a red line said your voice may be recorded. Exo Capture now leaves the call out: the game's sound and your microphone stay, each on its own track and in the mix, and with no game, what your apps play, except Discord and apps that play a call or a microphone again. Capture settings › Record the call's voices puts the call back in, and only then does Exo tell the call. When someone else in your call has clips on, Voice connected shows a small red dot that says so when you point at it, in place of the red line, and it goes when they turn clips off or leave.
  • Exo Store, for developers to publish their own games. The Store room gets an Exo section once Exo Store opens: games published by their developers, each with its page (screenshots, About, age and content, system requirements checked against your PC), Get to install it into Exo's games folder and Library, Update that keeps your saves, and Uninstall that removes only the game's folder. Developers publish from Store › Exo › Publish with their Exo identity, no email or password: a profile, the store page and pictures, and builds as a Windows zip linked from their own site. Every game and every change waits for review before anyone sees it, and Exo checks each zip three times (on the developer's PC, at review and on yours) for files outside the game's folder, console game files and the game's own exe. Until Exo Store goes online it runs only on a developer's own PC, and Exo contacts no store.
  • Voice messages from Exo's Discord. The message box has a voice message button after dictation's mic, wherever Discord lets you send one (a DM, or a server channel with Send Voice Messages): press it and talk, with no need to turn dictation off. The box shows a dot that turns red once the microphone is on, the time and your voice coming in; Stop lets you hear it back with its waveform, then Send it or Delete it. Enter twice records and sends, Escape deletes. It's recorded with the microphone and noise suppression in your voice settings, pauses turned down rather than cut, and in a call it takes your voice from the call itself, so the call's microphone is never opened twice or changed. It goes to Discord as a real voice message, Opus in Ogg with its length and waveform, up to Discord's 20 minutes, and plays for everyone as one. Leaving the room or minimizing Exo stops it, so it never records out of sight. A voice message's waveform now also takes the arrow keys, Home and End.
  • Listen along with anyone in your call who uses Exo. What someone plays in Exo's Music room now shows in the Jam box and in Listen along for everyone in their Discord call who uses Exo, from Spotify, Apple Music or YouTube Music, and Listen plays it on your own service, the same song directly when you both use one. It goes through Exo's own call relay, sealed with a key made from the call's end-to-end encryption, so only the people in the call can read it: Discord is never told, and the relay can't see it. A pause pauses you too, and leaving the call clears your song for everyone.
  • Sign-in windows carry the service's own mark, in Exo's look. The windows Exo opens for a service (YouTube Music's sign-in, the windows YouTube Music and Apple Music open, and the Steam, Epic Games, GOG and Xbox sign-ins) showed Exo's mark beside the service's name with its buttons drawn as text in rings, or Windows' plain title bar. Each now has the service's own mark and name in its title bar and as its taskbar icon (Xbox, whose mark isn't free to use, its name alone), the window's minimize, maximize and close as the Bar draws them, in their glass capsule with the same hover and the red close, your Appearance with a hairline under the band, and opens at its sign-in page's size, centered on the screen Exo is on. The whole band drags, and hovering Maximize opens Windows' snap layouts. The service's own page under it is untouched.
  • YouTube Music lyrics follow the song. Lyrics for a YouTube Music song now light line by line as it plays, and a click on a line jumps there, as they do for Spotify and Apple Music. YouTube Music has only untimed lyrics on the web, so Exo looks the song up in LRCLIB's open lyrics library by its title, artists, album and length; a song it doesn't know keeps YouTube Music's own text. The Better Lyrics extension is gone from the music browser: it timed lyrics only in YouTube Music's own page, which Exo never shows.
  • Exo Shield in the music players. YouTube Music and Apple Music now block ads, trackers and cookie notices with Exo Shield, the same blocker as Exo Browser, in place of uBlock Origin Lite. It's built on uBlock Origin Lite with HaGeZi's Multi PRO list added and its annoyance lists on, and Exo removes the old uBlock Origin Lite from the music players as it next starts. The music pages also can't reach the browser engine's built-in AI (summarizing, translating, detecting a language): a page that asked for it could hang, and its answers help sites tell browsers apart.
  • Your volume stays yours in a friend's Jam. Joining a friend's Spotify Jam set your volume to theirs. Exo now puts yours back the moment Spotify changes it, and the slider keeps the level you chose.
  • One volume slider for every service. Apple Music's and YouTube Music's own volume is the sound's level, so the bottom of Exo's slider was still loud: 5% was only 26 dB down. The slider is now a loudness scale on every service, as Spotify's is, so the same spot sounds about as loud on each.
  • The Jam box shows the song. The row above your call shows the song's cover where a friend's picture was, and everyone listening to it in Discord to the right of its name, their pictures overlapping, then how many more.
  • The Jam box is only for music you can join. It shows someone in your call playing a song, with Listen, or the one you're listening along with, with Stop. Your own song no longer shows there too: it's in the Bar's player.
  • Listen along says Not on Apple Music only when it isn't. It said a friend's song wasn't on your service whenever it couldn't play it, a search that failed included. Now Not on Apple Music (or Spotify, or YouTube Music) means your service's search came back without the song, and a search or play that failed says Couldn't play it and tries twice more.
  • Your stream keeps going when Discord moves your call. Discord now and then moves a call to another voice server. Exo ended your Go Live with the call's old connection without telling Discord, so it froze for your viewers, and going live again waited on the stream Discord still had open. Now only the call's own connection moves; your stream and the streams you watch go on. A stream that ends with a call is closed on Discord too.
  • A watched stream shows its picture after Exo restarts. A stream put back after a restart played its sound with no picture: the page asked for the video before Exo's Discord had signed in, was told no, and never asked again. Exo now keeps the page's ask across sign-in and reconnects, and the page asks again a second after a no. A window shown again without taking the focus also no longer leaves Exo thinking it's hidden, which held the video back the same way.
  • No LIVE tag on a stream. The stream you watch, a stream's tile and your own stream's tile no longer say LIVE: it's a stream. The call's list and a person's tile still mark who is streaming.
  • See who's watching your stream. Your stream's tile in the call shows the pictures of whoever is watching it, up to three and then how many more, in the corner across from its name, and each one goes as they stop watching. Discord sends the list again each time it changes.
  • Your stream never plays the call back to it. Sharing your screen takes each app that plays sound together with everything that app started. When Exo had been started from File Explorer, Steam or another launcher that had played a sound of its own, that app's share held Exo too, so everyone watching heard the call's voices in your stream, their own among them. Apps that Exo runs under are now left out, and the apps they started come in one by one, so nothing else goes missing. An app whose share fails no longer keeps the apps it started out with it. More apps that play your mic or a call again are left out too: TeamSpeak, Mumble, Teams, Webex, Slack and Signal; mic monitors and voice changers such as Logitech G HUB, Nahimic, MorphVOX and Voice.ai; mixers and relays such as VB-Audio Matrix, Audio Repeater and Sonic Studio; and Streamlabs and XSplit. For any app no list names, Exo listens for the call itself. An app whose sound turns out to be the call's voices or your mic is left out of the stream for as long as it is, and your stream's status line names it. Exo Capture's replay leaves out the same apps.
  • Soundboard sounds stay out of your stream. Sharing your screen takes Exo's page for the Music room, and the call's soundboard sounds played in that page, so everyone watching heard them. Exo now plays every soundboard sound, yours and everyone else's, in the call's own sound: on the call's speakers at your Output Volume, silent while you're deafened, taken out of your mic by echo cancellation, and never in your stream. Each still plays at its own volume, other people's at your Soundboard Volume too and not at all with the soundboard muted.
  • Streams you watch play smoothly beside a game. A friend's stream stuttered while you played on your other screen. Exo drew each picture into its page the moment it was decoded, so pictures that came in bunched up (a lost packet holds back the ones after it until it's sent again) replaced each other unseen, and anything else the page was busy with held the next picture back. Each picture now waits a moment in a small buffer and shows at its own time on the stream's clock, decoded and timed apart from the rest of the page, and the stream plays as a video the way Edge plays one. The wait stays as short as the stream allows, at most 80 ms. Cameras in a call and your own stream's tile play the same way, and full screen, the mini player, Stream Volume and the stream's sound work as before.
  • A stream that stutters or stays quiet says why. Every 30 seconds, Exo notes what a stream you watch did. For its sound, that's the sound packets it got, how many decrypted, decoded and played, how loud the loudest was, and your Stream Volume for it. For its picture, that's the pictures decoded and shown, those dropped or shown late, the decoding time, how long pictures waited for their time and how evenly they showed, whether the graphics card decodes it, and which game was in front. Your own stream's report adds the sound it sent and how loud that was. The next silent or stuttering stream can then be traced to the network, the decoder, the timing or the sound itself.
  • Store apps your game doesn't need close, however you start it. A game started from its store's own app, a shortcut or another launcher now gets what Exo's own launch does: the store apps it doesn't use are asked to close (Steam and Epic while a Riot game runs), and Riot Client's window closes 5 seconds after a Riot game starts. A store hosting a running game, downloading, or open in front of you stays; Riot Client's background service and Vanguard are never touched.
  • Steam's offline mode, in Settings and for family games. Settings › Accounts and keys › Game stores has Offline mode under Steam: turn it on and Steam restarts out of sight in its own offline mode for the account it signs in as, and stays offline until you turn it off (a closed Steam takes it as it next starts). A game borrowed from your Steam Family now starts with Steam offline when it plays offline: single-player or on one screen, not an MMO, nothing Steam says needs the internet, and no anti-cheat by Steam's own marks or Exo's. Once you stop playing, Steam goes back online, only if Exo put it offline. A family game that plays online, has an update waiting, or that Steam won't start offline starts online, and Play says why in a few words. Steam never restarts while a Steam game runs (Exo now also sees one Steam lost track of) or while it downloads, offline mode needs Steam's saved sign-in, and Exo writes only Steam's own two offline values. While Steam is offline, your family's games stay in the Library as Steam last listed them.
  • Properties asks only what's yours to choose. A Steam game's Properties no longer have switches for cloud saves, the Steam overlay or the controller, or an Updates page: Exo sets each one right for every game itself, and Launch options and Language stay yours. Cloud saves are on wherever Steam keeps them, turned back on for a game that had them off. Every game stays up to date: one set to update only when you launch it goes back to Steam's automatic updates, and one that always downloaded while it ran goes back to Steam's own setting, which pauses downloads while you play. The Steam overlay is on only for games that need it, the ones that sell through Steam (Dota 2, Counter-Strike 2, Call of Duty), play online with friends, use Remote Play Together or use Steam Input, and off for the rest (Hollow Knight, God of War), with one hook fewer in the game. Steam Input follows each game's controller support: off where the game reads an Xbox and a PlayStation controller itself, on where its controller support is partial or missing, and Steam's own default where it reads only Xbox controllers, so a DualSense still works there. Where Steam Input maps your controller, Controller in Steam still opens Steam's page for its layout. A choice made in Properties before gives way to these, and app.log has a line for each game changed. A switch you change in Steam itself stays yours: before, Exo turned a Steam Input you changed in Steam back on its next pass.
  • No "Grab a controller" before your game. Steam held some games at a dialog of its own until you pressed OK: "Grab a controller and kick back!" for a game best played with one, "Grab your controller!" for one that needs a controller when none is connected, and its notes on what it does with a PlayStation controller in that game. They're off now for every game and every Steam account on this PC, however you start the game, kept as Steam keeps them once you tick Don't show this on future games; Exo already sets Steam Input for each game itself. Steam takes the change only while it's closed, so Exo closes it for a moment once, while no game runs and nothing downloads, or as you press Play before the game starts, and starts it again hidden. Unclaimed content and a VR game's headset notice still show: only you can act on them.
  • No one's Discord activity on their profile, and no Active Now. A profile shows whether they're in a voice channel, with Join, and nothing of what they play or listen to. Friends no longer has an Active Now column.
  • The PC room fits on one page. PC was three screens of sections. Now everything fits on one page on a 1080p screen. Your PC's name leads, with Best settings beside it, green once everything's set, or red with how many things are left; pressing it lists each one with Use best settings, and what Exo set with its Undo. Your graphics card and your screen open their own menus: the card's driver profile, Profile Inspector file and frame generation; and Display's refresh rate, color, scaling and HDR, with your card maker's control panel at the top. Network and Apps sit beside your PC, then Drives; Drivers and Shaders fill the rest, each scrolling in its own pane. Drives now shows only each drive's name, temperature, how full it is, free space, life left and anything wrong, without the maker's tool button, and its settings are part of Best settings. Upscalers is a mark at the top (a check, or how many games to update) that opens its games. What's set or up to date no longer takes room: the Needs you, Performance and Graphics sections are gone, and so are the graphics card's live readings. A small window stacks the parts and scrolls.
  • Network has its own place in PC. Your connection has a section of its own that's always there, so it can be found: your adapter by name ("Intel Ethernet Controller I226-V"), Ethernet or Wi-Fi, and its link speed. Exo's settings read Set for games when there's nothing to fix, with Undo once Exo set them, and Optimize when the adapter has a setting that adds delay or drops the link, the same one-prompt fix Best settings for this PC runs. On Wi-Fi it says what Exo does while a game runs: Windows' background scans paused and full power on battery. A cable holding a fast adapter to 100 Mbps shows there in amber. Ctrl+K finds it by network, internet, Ethernet or Wi-Fi.
  • Best settings shows what it set. Once everything was set, PC's Best settings for this PC showed its title and a lone "Set". It now names each step with a green check beside it (drawn in as a step lands), and Use best settings stays while any is left; the power plan keeps your processor's name once Exo's plan is on.
  • Apps lists only your apps. The "Cleared by Exo" list at the foot of PC › Apps is gone; Allow Edge again, after Exo removed Edge, is under "Exo does their job".
  • Call of Duty's CNN choice now really runs, and only where it pays. Optimize set MW4's DLSS model to CNN on RTX 20 and 30 cards, but Exo's own NVIDIA profile forces the transformer's presets in every game, so the driver ran the transformer anyway (Exo's log showed Preset K in every session). Optimize now picks CNN only on an RTX 20 or 30 at 1440p and 4K, where it's 0.6 to 3 ms a frame cheaper and looks close, and lifts the override in MW4's own NVIDIA profile so the game's choice runs. At 1080p (CNN works from a 720p picture there and smears in motion) and on RTX 40 and 50 cards it keeps the transformer. Put back lifts Exo's exception again.
  • Each optimized game gets its own NVIDIA profile. Optimize now gives a game its own profile in NVIDIA's driver on top of Exo's global one, the way Profile Inspector keeps one per program, holding only what that game needs differently; everything else carries over. It's applied and put back with the rest of Optimize, the game's own values kept for Put back, and PC › Graphics › Driver and profile lists each game's profile under the global one. Modern Warfare 4's is the first: where Optimize picks DLSS's CNN model, its profile lifts the global transformer preset so CNN really runs.
  • Optimize Rocket League. Rocket League's page has an Optimize line too, for the Epic copy and the Steam one alike (both read the same settings file, so both pages show the same state). Rocket League keeps its Video menu with your account, so Exo sets only what the game reads from that file, where Psyonix's engine, Rocket Science's latency tests and the pros agree: the game's own frame pacing as Psyonix ships it, undoing the old tweaks that switch every frame cap off (the menu's included), cost frames for no measured drop in lag, or hang the game at start; on a G-Sync or adaptive-sync screen, frames held 3 under its top rate by the game's own cap (never under 120, the rate its physics runs at), with the driver's lower cap lifted in Rocket League's own NVIDIA profile so the game's runs; full resolution, never scaled down; 16x texture filtering on a card with its own memory, so the field's lines stay sharp at an angle; no motion blur, depth of field, bloom or ambient occlusion; and the ball's ground marker, its shadow on walls and see-through goalposts kept, which the "potato" configs break. It never makes the file read-only (that hangs the game's start on some PCs), changes nothing while the game or its anti-cheat launcher runs, and Put back returns what you had. A game update rebuilds the file from Psyonix's defaults at the game's first start, and Exo sets its lines again as that session closes.
  • Optimize Phantom Blade Zero. Phantom Blade Zero's page has an Optimize line too, on Steam and Epic alike. The game keeps its whole Video page (upscaler and Output Scale, frame generation, Reflex, V-Sync, the frame cap and every quality level) in a sealed file that it checks as it loads, and it stops saving your settings for the session when that file has been changed, so Exo leaves that page to you and sets only what the game reads from its own settings file: borderless full screen, which runs at your screen's highest refresh and never switches modes on Alt+Tab, where the game rebuilds what its DLSS and frame generation draw on. Nothing changes while the game runs, Put back returns your window mode, and a game update that puts full screen back is set again as that session closes. Exo adds no Engine.ini tweaks: the game's own per-PC profile from S-GAME overrides an Engine.ini line wherever both set one, none of the tweaks passed round has been shown to work or help in this game, and its menu already has sharpness, film grain, motion blur and depth of field. Exo's driver profile already gives it what it needs from the NVIDIA driver, and on a Radeon, Fluid Motion Frames stays off for it, since it has frame generation of its own.
  • The fast graphics chip for Unreal games on laptops. Graphics for games asked Windows for the fast chip for a game's programs at its top and in the usual folders, which for most Unreal Engine 4 and 5 games is only the small program that starts the game: the one that draws it sits in the game's project folder, under Binaries\Win64, and Phantom Blade Zero's in platform\steam. Exo now asks for that one too, and leaves the engine's own helper programs alone.
  • NVIDIA's frame generation on RTX 20 and 30 cards. A game's DLSS Frame Generation is greyed out on a GeForce RTX 20 or 30. Its page now has a Frame generation line on those cards: pick 2x, 3x or 4x, and Exo puts MFG Unlocker beside the game's program, so the game's own DLSS Frame Generation switch runs NVIDIA's own frame generation model at that multiplier. 2x comes first on an RTX 30: each generated frame costs a real one time there, so more frames add lag and uneven pacing; an RTX 20, where the mod calls it experimental, gets 2x, and an RTX 40, whose 2x is its own, gets 3x and 4x. It goes in where the game has DLSS frame generation through Streamline 2 on DirectX 12 with NVIDIA's 310 runtime, even where the game also has FSR or XeSS frame generation, as Phantom Blade Zero does: such a game picks DLSS's as it starts and doesn't fall back, so after each session Exo reads the mod's own status, and the line says when the game asked for frame generation and none came, with Off one pick away. Where NVIDIA's can't run (a Vulkan or Streamline 1 game, or a Radeon or Intel Arc), a game whose only frame generation is DLSS's gets a switch for OptiScaler instead, which runs AMD's FSR frame generation under the game's switch; there a game with FSR or XeSS frame generation of its own says Built into the game and gets nothing. Whichever mod it is, it's the project's newest release from its GitHub page, checked against the SHA-256 GitHub publishes for it, with the mod's own DLL and AMD's and Intel's signed files checked before it goes in, and the line names the mod and release in the game (MFG Unlocker 1.7.0). While a game has it, Exo looks for a newer release every six hours and moves the game to it while no game runs; if the next session ends within 30 seconds or no frames came, the game goes back to the release before and the new one isn't offered to it again. Best settings has it too, as Frame generation in games without anti-cheat: Exo then puts it into every game it works in by itself, and into each game you install after, so the game's own switch is there the first time it opens. That's on a GeForce RTX 20 or 30, a Radeon RX 5000 or newer, or an Intel Arc card; an RTX 40 keeps its game's own 2x, and graphics inside a processor rarely reach the 60 fps frame generation needs underneath. PC › Graphics turns it off again, Off on a game's line keeps that game out, and if a game closes within 30 seconds or no frames come in its first session with it, Exo takes it out of that game and leaves it out. Without Best settings it stays off until you turn it on for a game: it's a community mod that loads into the game, and frame generation adds lag. Nothing the game ships is replaced: the mod takes a Windows DLL name the game's folder doesn't have. A game with anti-cheat never gets it, whatever its upscalers were allowed, and its line says so; an RTX 50 has multi frame generation and no line. If a game later shows anti-cheat, a patch changes the game's program, or the card no longer needs it, Exo takes it out before the game starts, and the line says why. Off removes every file Exo wrote, checked by SHA-256, and what the mod wrote beside it, so the folder is as it was; a game update that removed or changed them says so on the line, and the upscalers never update the mods' files. With OptiScaler on a GeForce whose driver caps the frame rate (Exo's G-SYNC cap), the game's own NVIDIA profile lifts that cap and OptiScaler caps at the same rate through Reflex; NVIDIA's own pipeline keeps the driver's cap. On an Intel GPU, the driver's frame generation line is now called XeSS frame generation.
  • Optimize stays on through game updates. Once you press Optimize for a game, Exo keeps it on. When an update resets the game's settings, Exo sets them again at the first moment the game isn't running: as you close the session the update reset, or as Exo starts if that session ran while Exo was closed. A notice says the game's update reset its settings and Exo optimized it again, and the game's line reads Applied again after an update until you next play. Rocket League rebuilds its whole settings file from its defaults at its first start after an update, and the file's own [IniVersion] tells Exo so. Modern Warfare 4's files carry no such mark, so Exo takes it for an update's reset only when the game's program was updated and every setting Exo set in a file went back at once. Your own choices stay yours: a setting you changed in the game's menu, a preset you picked there, and a reset to defaults with no update in between are never written over, a group turned off in Exo stays off, and pressing Applied puts everything back and stops it. The writes follow Optimize's rules: only values the game's file allows, never while the game or its anti-cheat launcher runs, and what you had before Exo kept for Put back. A Modern Warfare 4 you optimized on an earlier version is kept on from now on.
  • Your network, set for games. PC › Network shows Optimize when your Ethernet adapter has settings that drop the link or add delay: Energy-Efficient Ethernet (Intel's own fix for the I225/I226 dropouts was turning it off), Intel's DMA coalescing, or receive and transmit buffers an optimizer shrank. Optimize fixes them in one Windows permission prompt, with Undo, and it's in Best settings for this PC too. The adapter restarts, so the connection drops for a few seconds; it waits while you're in a call or a game. An Ethernet link stuck at 100 Mbps on a faster adapter is called out as a cable or port to swap. On Wi-Fi there's nothing to press: while a game runs, Exo stops Windows' background Wi-Fi scans (each one leaves a gap of 100 ms or more in what your PC receives) and keeps the radio at full power on battery, then puts both back. The usual registry "network tweaks" (Nagle, NetworkThrottlingIndex, interrupt moderation, QoS, DNS) do nothing for a game on Windows 11, so Exo leaves them alone.
  • Your drives, in the PC room. PC has a Drives section: each drive by name with its temperature, its link ("NVMe SSD · PCIe 4.0 ×4"), its firmware, its health as the drive itself reports it (life left, data written, hours, unsafe shutdowns, read errors or bad sectors) and how full each of its volumes is, with a button to its maker's own firmware tool (Samsung Magician, SanDisk Dashboard, Crucial Storage Executive and others; Exo never flashes firmware). A line shows only when something is wrong: a link on fewer lanes than the drive can use, or slower than it can run and never faster while you play; firmware with a known fault (Samsung's 990 PRO 0B2QJXD7 and 980 PRO 3B2QGXA7) or older than the newest its maker lists; a drive that's failing, worn out, past its warning temperature or running on a storage driver other than Windows' own; write-cache flushing off; games on a hard drive; and a game that loads through DirectStorage but whose files can't use its fast path, with Windows' reason (sparse or compressed files, a filter driver). NVMe, SATA SSDs and hard drives all show; nothing needs administrator rights, and nothing is written to a drive. This PC's facts no longer repeat the drives' free space.
  • Every driver in one place. PC › Drivers lists every driver and firmware in your PC: graphics, chipset and network from NVIDIA, AMD, Intel, Killer and Realtek, the rest from Windows Update's signed catalog, and your BIOS and SSD firmware with a link to their maker. It says Up to date or how many are waiting, and Update all installs them one by one while no game, call or update runs, keeping each replaced driver to put back if one fails. It takes the place of the separate graphics and chipset rows.
  • The best graphics driver for your games. A driver a game's developer advises against isn't offered while you have that game ("621.12 skipped: unstable in Phantom Blade Zero"), and Go back returns you to the one they name, with Exo's settings put back after.
  • No driver with a known problem. Every driver and firmware Exo offers (graphics, chipset, network, BIOS, SSD and Windows Update's) is checked against the known problems for your hardware, Windows and games, and checked again just before it installs. A version with one is skipped, and its row says why, with the fix: "3636 installed: fTPM security flaws" with Update to 3645. The list keeps itself current from Exo's update server, signed, at most once a day.
  • Drivers install without their extras. AMD's graphics driver installs minimal: the driver alone, with only what Fluid Motion Frames needs on cards that have it. NVIDIA's adds its HD Audio driver only when a screen plays sound through the card. Intel's graphics and network drivers and AMD's chipset driver were already minimal. Apps Windows Update bundles with a driver are rows of their own, and Nahimic and HP's analytics are never offered.
  • Each drive set for its own model. A drive's row shows its power (an NVMe drive held at full power by Exo's power plan, what Samsung Magician's Full Power Mode and WD's Gaming Mode do), write caching, its DRAM, sector size and driver, read through Windows' own NVMe queries with no maker's tool. Write caching that's off comes back on with one prompt, with Undo. WD's SN770, SN580 and SN5000 2TB on firmware that crashes on Windows 11 24H2 say which update fixes it, beside the Samsung 990 PRO and 980 PRO firmware warnings. The warning before an install fills a drive keeps the room that drive's type needs: more on QLC and DRAM-less drives, which slow down sharply when full.
  • What's using your space. Each drive shows one bar of what fills it, games, shader caches, Exo, what removed games left in a Steam library and the rest, over its biggest games with their shader caches. Direct3D's per-game shader caches are matched to your games by the program they name, Steam's by app id. Old ones, for a game or app that's gone or an older copy unused for a month, are Clean up's now: they count toward Junk files and have their own Clean under the drives, beside Exo's old app backups. A cache in use and a game's own files are never deleted, and neither is the cache of a copy Exo Shaders compiles a game in: Exo Shaders moves those into the game's cache or deletes them itself.
  • Compiled shaders reach the game, and their copies go. On NVIDIA from driver 621, Exo Shaders compiles a game in a copy of its program and then moves what it compiled into the shader cache Windows keeps for the game itself, deleting the copy's. That move read some cache files whole, and one over 2 GB, of any game, made it fail every time: the compiled shaders stayed in caches named for the copy, which the game never reads, and took space for nothing (9.6 GB of Beast of Reincarnation's compile on this PC). Those files are now read a few megabytes at a time, so the next time Shaders runs, what a compile left behind goes into its game's cache and the copies are deleted. Another game's cache that can't be read without its log, as a game that quit mid-save leaves it, no longer keeps a compile waiting for good.
