After a graphics driver update, compiled shaders still waiting that the old driver made, which the new one can't use, are deleted instead of being written into the game's cache, and a compile on the new driver waits for the game's next start if the game's cache is still the old driver's, never deleted.
  • Write-cache flushing and weekly optimize, in one prompt. Needs you shows Write-cache flushing when a drive has Windows' "turn off write-cache buffer flushing" on (a power cut can then damage files; this PC's drive had shut down without warning 132 times), and Weekly optimize when Windows' scheduled optimize can't run (its task off, or Automatic Maintenance turned off by an optimizer), each with Turn on; Best settings for this PC sets both in one Windows permission prompt, here and in first run. Flushing finishes with a restart, which its row says; both have Undo under Performance.
  • A warning before an install fills a drive. When an install would leave its drive under 12% free (an SSD slows down when it's nearly full, and updates need room to patch), Exo asks first and names the roomiest SSD that has space; Cancel starts nothing. For Steam it judges the drive of your one Steam library, from the room the game's page asks for.
  • Your drive's heat while you play. While a game runs, Exo reads the temperature of the drive it's installed on every 10 seconds and its own record of slowing down to cool, and only then: a game's page then shows Drive last session ("79 °C · slowed 40 s") when the drive came near its limit or slowed itself. A link that never reaches its full speed while you play is said in Drives.
  • FSR 4 on more Radeon graphics. AMD Software 26.10.1 brought the FSR 4 upgrade to Ryzen AI Max, Ryzen AI 9 and Ryzen Z2 graphics and the Radeon AI PRO cards, whose names Exo couldn't place. On those, Exo's Radeon profile now turns it on wherever the driver offers it.
  • Exo leaves your upscaler mods alone. A file an upscaler mod puts in a game under DLSS's, FSR's or XeSS's name (HelixSR, OptiScaler, DLSS Enabler, Nukem's dlssg-to-fsr3, Uniscaler, fsr2dlss, PureDark's upscalers) read as an old or unknown version, so Update all would have written the vendor's file over it and undone the mod. Exo now knows a mod by what it leaves in the game's folder (its settings and log files, its own folders, the game's file it kept aside, its own build of the file), and never updates a mod's file or puts the game's original back over it: the game's Upscalers line and its card in PC › Graphics › Upscalers show the mod's name where the update would be. The vendor's own signed files beside a mod, and a game's own FSR build, update as before.
  • Optimize can be put back. Once a game shows Optimize as Applied, pressing it again asks, then puts back each setting Optimize changed to what you had. Exo always kept them, but nothing on the page could put them back.
  • A game says Closing until it has closed. Closing a game that took more than a few seconds showed Running again partway through, then Closing when it finished: the pill read its word from the status line, which clears itself after a few seconds. It now holds until the game has closed.
  • A game's page closes while it launches or closes. A press outside a game's page did nothing while it was launching or closing, though Escape closed it. Both close it now; the launch or close goes on behind it.
  • Voice Tune's gain is added once, and only as far as your voice needs it. Keeping a tuning set its gain on Windows' input level, and Exo Voice then added it again to every frame, so a quiet mic's voice reached the call up to 20 dB too loud and was squashed. Exo Voice now adds only what your voice is still short of, from how loud it actually comes in, gliding so it never jumps, and nothing once you turn the mic itself up.
  • Only My Voice learns your voice by itself, with nothing to teach. Turn it on and Exo Voice learns your voice from your first 15 seconds of talking, in a call or on Voice settings' live meter, then keeps learning from speech that is clearly yours. If it ever holds back your own voice, it stops holding back, learns your voice again from what it just heard and comes back within the call, quietly: the voice panel's red notice is gone. It learns from your voice exactly as Only My Voice judges it, after noise suppression and before Exo Voice's EQ and leveler, and from speech alone. Teach Exo my voice and Teach again are gone; Start over deletes the voiceprint and learns it again (Forget, with Only My Voice off).
  • Game sound and clicks no longer show you talking. With a quiet mic, Exo Voice's noise gate opened on loudness alone, so a single moment of a gunshot, a sword, a key or music that noise suppression let through lit your ring and went out for over half a second. The gate now opens only on a sound that lasts longer than a click and that Exo Voice's noise suppression hears a voice in; once you're talking, loudness carries your words to their end as before, and the 100 ms before you start still go out. With Standard or no noise suppression, a sound has to last too. Exo's log says once a minute why the gate opened.
  • Only My Voice keeps holding back while it learns your voice again. Learning your voice, by itself or again after it held you back, started Only My Voice over, so for about two seconds a voice in your headset went out. The new voiceprint now goes into the one already listening.
  • More mics, interfaces and mixers, with what to set on each. Voice settings now knows the Elgato Wave XLR MK.2, Wave XLR Pro, XLR Dock and XLR Dock MK.2, Wave:3 MK.2 and Wave Go; Corsair VOXARA; Roland BRIDGE CAST, BRIDGE CAST ONE and X; Yamaha AG03MK2 and AG06MK2 (and the first AG03 and AG06); Focusrite Vocaster One and Two; Zoom PodTrak P2, P4, P4next and P8; BEACN Studio; Shure MV6 Gen 2, MV7i and MVX2U Gen 2; RØDE NT1 5th Gen, Streamer X and the first RØDECaster Pro; SteelSeries Alias and Alias Pro; Audio-Technica AT2020USB-XP; Razer Audio Mixer; AVerMedia AX310; Maonocaster G1 Neo; Mackie M•Caster Studio; TASCAM Mixcast 4 and US-42B; and Steinberg UR22C: what each one's own processing does next to Exo Voice, and the steps to set it. The GoXLR's De-Esser is listed too. More interfaces and mics are known by name (Arturia MiniFuse, Native Instruments Komplete Audio, M-Audio AIR, MOTU M6, Mackie Onyx Producer, Steinberg UR12, Audio-Technica AT2020USB-X, AT2040USB and AT-UMX3, ASUS ROG Carnyx, Fifine SC3), and an XLR Dock MK.2 is no longer taken for the first Wave XLR.
  • Your mic, read by its sound. Voice settings' live mic says whether your voice clips (turn the mic's gain down), comes in too quiet (turn it up), is gated before Exo (digital silence between words: the mic's own gate or an app's) or was already cleaned before Exo, with the voice's level and the quiet between words, and app.log says it in calls when it changes. A compressor or auto-level before Exo can't be told from a steady voice by its sound, so Exo doesn't guess at one.
  • Game Mode stays on. Exo turns Windows Game Mode on by itself and keeps it on: at start, when PC's setup is read, and again the moment something turns it off (Windows Settings, a debloat script or an optimizer). It doesn't need the Xbox Game Bar app, so a PC without it gets Game Mode too, and PC's Needs you no longer has a Game Mode row to fix by hand.
  • Game Bar stays off, and Windows stops asking for an app. Exo turns Xbox Game Bar's background recording and its Win+G and controller buttons off at start, since Exo records clips itself. Game Bar's links (the game overlay one included) now open nothing: with Game Bar removed, Windows asked you to look for an app in the Microsoft Store whenever a game or Windows opened one. Only settings change, never the app, so a dual-CCD Ryzen X3D still parks its cores for games, and Game Mode stays on.
  • Steam and the other launchers let go of their memory sooner. Exo empties the memory of a launcher's page-drawing helpers (Steam's steamwebhelper, Epic's, the EA app's, Ubisoft Connect's, GOG Galaxy's and Battle.net's) while you aren't looking at it: on our test PC Steam drops from about 570 MB to 240 MB. Until now that waited for its window to be closed. A launcher window you leave open behind a game or another window now counts too, after two minutes out of front. Click it and it has its memory back at once; downloads, game launches, the overlay and Steam Input never stop.
  • Radeon Anti-Lag is always on. As asked, Exo's Radeon profile now turns Anti-Lag on for every game, however it starts, and the While you play, Always and Off choice beside Use is gone. While you play kept it off until a game Exo launched started, so a game started from Steam, a shortcut or another launcher ran without it. A profile applied with While you play or Off is written again with Anti-Lag on at the next start, and Undo still puts back your own setting. Fluid Motion Frames stays per game, as before.
  • Fluid Motion Frames, decided by Exo for each game. On a Radeon, the game sheet's Fluid Motion Frames switch is gone: Exo decides for each game on your PC, sets it as the game starts and puts back what you had once no game runs. It's off for competitive games, where it adds lag and smears the crosshair just when you flick (Fortnite, VALORANT, Counter-Strike 2, Apex Legends, Overwatch 2, Rainbow Six Siege, Call of Duty, Rocket League, League of Legends, Dota 2, Marvel Rivals, THE FINALS, Deadlock, fighting games, and any game Steam's players tag PvP, Competitive, eSports, Battle Royale, MOBA or Hero Shooter, unless they tag it Singleplayer or Story Rich first); off for games with frame generation of their own, which looks better and lags less; off where your graphics card meets only a game's Minimum, too slow for the frame rate it needs; and off on a Direct3D 9 game or a card before the RX 6000 series. Everything else gets it: single-player, story, open-world, RPG, strategy, simulation and MMO games, most of all those held at 60 fps, which it takes to 120. It reads Steam's tags and requirements and PCGamingWiki once per game, and a game it knows nothing about stays off. The sheet says On or Off. Left on in AMD Software, it's still off while a competitive game runs. Games you switched on by hand before now follow Exo's choice too.
  • A game's sheet reads cleanly on a Radeon. HDR and Fluid Motion Frames were each a heading with a separate row under it, HDR's reading floating on a line of its own: each is now one line like Upscalers and Shaders, its name, its reading and its choice on the same edges (HDR: No HDR, then Auto at the right), with no gaps between sections. Intel's frame generation is one line the same way. Play is Exo's solid primary instead of see-through glass that washed out over bright art, and the art behind the sheet's words is dimmed further where the words start, so Size and the lines' readings stay legible over a bright logo.
  • System requirements judge Radeon graphics in a processor. A Store page now places a Radeon 680M, 780M, 890M or Ryzen AI Max's 8060S on its scale, so a handheld or mini PC gets a verdict for its graphics instead of none.
  • System requirements read laptops right. Thin-laptop Intel chips (i5-1235U, i7-1165G7) are placed by their generation, and a PC whose graphics can't be placed gets no verdict, not one from its processor alone.
  • The right graphics card on PCs with two. A Ryzen's built-in Radeon beside an Arc card, or Intel's UHD listed before an Arc, no longer stands in for your card in PC, the Store, game settings or frame generation: Exo takes the card Windows uses for high performance, and talks to that card's driver (an Arc beside a Ryzen's Radeon no longer got AMD's Anti-Lag 2 written for it). Intel's Arc-named graphics inside a processor (Meteor Lake's, the 140V) and a Ryzen's Vega 11 count as built in, and a Radeon PRO card as a card.
  • Radeon FreeSync screens get their frame cap. Optimize now holds Modern Warfare 4 and Rocket League just under a FreeSync screen's refresh, as on G-SYNC.
  • Intel processors read as Intel. A Core i9-9900 or i9-9900X was taken for a two-chip Ryzen, and the PC room said half of your processor sat idle; a processor "w/ Radeon" graphics is named without them on the power plan's button, and the RX 7600S laptop card gets its graphics scheduling row.
  • Exo's power plan is made on PCs without Balanced. Exo's plan is a copy of Windows' Balanced plan, and where Windows wouldn't copy it (a tweak tool or a debloat script can delete Balanced), Use Exo's plan stopped at "Windows didn't make Exo's plan". Exo now makes its plan from the plan that's on instead, gives it Balanced's personality so Windows' Game Mode and the chipset driver's tuning still apply, and writes every value it sets. If Windows refuses that too, the line says Windows' own reason and error code, and app.log says what Windows refused for each plan.
  • A Radeon screen's color changes with fewer blackouts. Setting a screen's color on an AMD GPU wrote its pixel format and its color depth every time, each a mode change that blanks the screen; one press took 10 seconds on an RX 6600. Exo now writes only the part that changes, and app.log says how long each took.
  • 10-bit only where your cable carries it. PC › Display offered a Radeon screen 10-bit at every refresh rate, and picking it where the link can't carry it (1440p at 165 or 170 Hz needs about 21 Gbps; DisplayPort 1.2 carries 17.3) ended in "The screen runs something else than what was set": AMD's driver takes the depth and quietly stays on 8-bit. Now a color that doesn't stick puts back what ran, with nothing to read, and Exo remembers that rate as more than that screen's link carries: it isn't offered there again at any format, after a restart too, and still is where it fits, such as at a lower refresh rate. Use best keeps your highest refresh rate and picks the deepest color the link carries at it, never one that failed: a smooth refresh is what games feel, and 8-bit with the GPU's dithering shows no banding on the desktop. NVIDIA and Intel screens get the same if their driver ever doesn't keep a color.
  • Exo Mouse takes OpenMouse's newest work. It moves to OpenMouse's 0.48.1 protocol library. A Logitech PRO X Superlight 2 or PRO X Superstrike running from this PC gets BHOP mode, as G HUB sets it there, and a profile keeps it and sets it when its game starts; the mouse can't say what it has, so Exo shows what it last set, off until then. On those mice a profile's Games sheet now sets its wireless and its wired polling rate, so the rate for the cable is chosen without plugging it in, and the one the mouse is on is used when the game starts. The PRO X 2 and PRO X 3 Superstrike's button assignments take a key the mouse presses itself: F1 to F24 (F13 to F24, which no keyboard has, for push-to-talk), Enter, Escape, the arrows and the keys around them. A Logitech mouse with no profiles in its memory says None stored instead of leaving the row out. Sleep after offers only the times your mouse takes (an AJAZZ refused 30 seconds) and reads Never for an AJAZZ set never to sleep, which read as unknown before; it is a menu now, as six times ran onto two lines. Exo's Starlight X fixes carry over unchanged.
  • Exo Mouse measures your sensor angle. Measure beside a mouse's Sensor angle (Angle tuning on Pulsar Pro and Endgame Gear mice) opens a pad: click it, swipe side to side with your usual grip, then right-click or press Esc. While you swipe, Exo Mouse holds the pointer and reads the mouse's own movement, raw where it can, so Windows' pointer speed and acceleration don't skew it, and draws each swipe through the middle of the pad. Ten swipes give the angle your swipes run at ("6.1° up to the right") and how sure it is: it is the line through all your movement, with the hook as you turn and any stray diagonal left out, and too few or too scattered swipes say so instead of giving a number. Set to -6° writes your mouse's own angle, rounded to what it takes and inside its range, and six more swipes check that they now come out flat. Brands don't agree which way a positive angle turns and OpenMouse doesn't say, so if the check shows the swipes turned the wrong way, Exo Mouse turns the angle round at once and remembers that model's way; on an Endgame Gear mouse, whose angle has no unit, it also learns how far a step turns. A mouse Exo can't set the angle of, like the Starlight X or an ATK, gets the measurement under Tests. Exo Mouse's sheets (this one, Calibration and HITS speed) now have Exo's dialog look in every Appearance, and their Close and small actions are Exo's quiet buttons instead of Windows' gray ones.
  • Your devices fill the Devices room. The room's cards sat small in a band at its top, the rest of the room empty. They now fill it, laid out by how many you have, each picture as large as its card allows: four are two by two (at 1080p each picture nearly twice the size it was), one fills the room, two sit side by side, three put your mouse the room's height beside the other two, five are three over two and six three by two, and seven or more scroll in a fuller grid. An upright window stacks two and three, a very wide one sets three or four in one row, and down to Exo's smallest window nothing scrolls. A press still flies the picture whole into the device's page and back.
  • Your audio page opens again. With a newer Lucid out, opening Devices › your audio stopped the whole Devices room until Exo restarted.
  • Exo Mouse in Exo's glass. The mouse's page still drew Exo's older glass: a bright line along each panel's top, a glow inside, gray panels on Dark and Light's old frost and dim. Exo now sends Exo Mouse its own glass with your Appearance, as plain values read off Exo's own panels, controls, sheets and menus, so the mouse's panels, hero, buttons, sheets and menus are Exo's own in Void, Dark and Light, follow Settings › Material and contrast, and stay so whenever Exo's glass changes; the code and style of each program stay their own. Its panels now rise in as Exo's do, its switches have Exo's white knob in Light, its dropdowns lift under the pointer and its test pads are clear on the glass.
  • Exo Cinema in the mini player, as a film. What Exo Cinema plays shows its poster upright beside the title, where a song shows its square cover, and a film's mark instead of a music note until Exo Cinema gives Windows one. The line under it for where the video is goes: Back 10 and Forward 10 move it, and the capsule stays short.
  • Replying keeps Discord's message box whole. With Replying to, editing or files above the box, the strip drew its own rim all the way round and the box under it lost its rim on its sides and foot, so the field looked broken in two. One rim now goes round the strip and the box together, with a faint line between them.
  • The hover bar shows on a message you just sent. It didn't until you moved the pointer off the message and back.
  • Discord in Exo's glass, all of it. Its right-click menus appear at once, as Exo's do; its dialogs and Music's playlist dialog match Exo's own; app select menus open like Exo's menus; the member list lines up under the Bar; the chat beside a call and the server guide's side panel float as glass; and the picture viewer's tools are glass that stays readable over bright pictures. Every sheet, dialog and the picture viewer dims the room the same way, softer in Light.
  • Mute and Deafen are whole again. The small arrows beside them in Discord's dock covered 4 pixels of each button.
  • HDR for HDR games asks the game first. Whether a game outputs HDR is now Exo's own call from the game's own settings (Unreal's HDR switch when it's on, Call of Duty's and Frostbite's HDR lines, RE Engine's and Final Fantasy XVI's configs, a game's registry settings), then from games you've played with HDR on; PCGamingWiki is asked only when the game says nothing.
  • Unreal games' HDR at your screen's real brightness. Optimize sets an Unreal game's HDR peak to your screen's own, from Windows HDR Calibration, else the screen's EDID, instead of Unreal's 1000-nit default, in games without anti-cheat; Put back returns the game's value.
  • RTX Video HDR comes on where it does something. On a screen running Windows HDR with an RTX card, the best for that screen now includes RTX Video HDR on, so video that isn't HDR (most of the web, and Exo Browser's) is lifted into HDR instead of looking flat and grey; Best for this screen's Use and Best settings for this PC (in first run too) turn it on, and a driver install keeps it. On a screen without HDR the switch is gone from PC › Display, since it can't do anything there, unless it's on.
  • A controller plugged in mid-game gets your settings. A DualSense or Edge forgets its light, player light, sound and vibration when it's unplugged, and Exo sent them back on every connection except while a game ran, so one plugged in during a game kept the game's blue light and player light until the game closed. Now, a few seconds after it connects (after the game has set it up its own way), it gets your settings with that game's controller profile over them, and an Edge its calibration, while nobody is using it yet.
  • Update installs Lucid. Update on Lucid's row opened Lucid's own window and left the update to it, and that window often came up behind Exo or on your other screen. Exo now does the whole update itself, the way Lucid's own updater does it: the same file from Lucid's maker, checked as Lucid checks it (its size, that it's a Windows program, its version and name, and the checksum published beside it) and against your Lucid's publisher; Lucid closed the way Windows closes it at sign-out, never forced; the new one put in place, read back, and started again out of sight as before, with no administrator prompt unless Lucid's folder needs one. The row shows each step, its bar on the button. Closing Lucid leaves your EQ playing, but starting it again puts Lucid's own profile on for a moment, so during a game or a call the checked update waits until it ends, and the row says so, with Update now. Whatever fails leaves the Lucid you had running and says what to do, and a new version that doesn't start is swapped back for yours. A new Lucid's notice now opens your audio's page, where its Update is, and Open Lucid brings Lucid's window up in front, on Exo's screen.
  • Photos and links in Notes. A note takes a photo: paste one (a snip, a screenshot), drop one from Explorer, or press Photo over the note to pick one. It's kept in your Notes folder's Photos beside your notes, and Preview shows it at full width. Link sits over every note too, and a web address pasted over words turns them into its link. A text note becomes Markdown as its first photo or link goes in, since only Markdown can hold them, so the note opens with them in any Markdown app. A picture on the web in a note shows as its link, so a note never fetches from another site.
  • Every bar says how far, live. Whatever shows a bar now ends its words in a whole percent that counts up as it goes ("Downloading · 54%"): game installs and updates on the Play button, the game's card and Continue, driver updates, shader compiles, upscaler checks, Exo Store downloads and uploads, runtimes, Lucid's update, moving a game, Discord uploads, Exo's own update and Exo Setup, and notices with a bar. It never says 100% before it's done, and the figure holds its width, so nothing shifts as it counts. Work with no measure keeps its gliding bar and no number.
  • Junk cleans itself. Temporary files, crash reports, the thumbnail cache and Exo's old backups now go by themselves while nobody is using the PC, at most once a day, never during a game, a call, an install or an update. Shader caches and the Recycle Bin are never touched without your press.
  • Apps opens at once. The Apps sheet shows your apps and their icons right away and updates them quietly if anything changed: listing them took about half a second, now under 10 ms. Game runtimes only take half the sheet when one needs installing or updating; otherwise your apps fill it.
  • Your graphics card's control panel, from Display. Display has NVIDIA Control Panel, AMD Software or Intel's graphics app at its top, opened without an admin prompt. When NVIDIA Control Panel isn't installed (Exo's Clean up may have removed it), the button opens its Microsoft Store page.
  • One Exo identity for all your Exo apps. The keys and record that sign what you publish to the Exo Store now live in one place for your Windows account, shared by Exo Launcher, Exo Cinema and Exo Browser, ready for your Exo profile and sync. If you published before, your record moves there by itself the first time Exo starts.
  • Instant replay no longer halves your frame rate in anti-cheat games. A game whose anti-cheat keeps Exo Capture from recording inside it was recorded through its window, and Windows then composes every frame of the game, which takes it off its direct path to the screen: Valorant on an RTX 3070 fell from 354 fps to 170, its 1% lows from 222 to 57, with more delay. A game that fills its screen is now recorded through the screen instead, which leaves it on that path (363 fps measured with the same kind of capture). Frames are kept only while the game is in front, so a clip never shows what else was on the screen when you Alt+Tab. Exo also always runs the newest Exo Capture it has: an older copy left beside Exo's data had kept being used over the one each update installs.
  • Undo puts your NVIDIA settings back even when the driver refuses one. Undo for Exo's driver profile, or for a Profile Inspector file you imported, stopped at the first setting the NVIDIA driver no longer takes and put back nothing, with "The NVIDIA driver could not change the setting". Every other setting now goes back, and the one the driver refuses is left as it is and named in Exo's log.
  • Riot Client stays out of sight. Riot no longer starts a game straight from its command line, so a game started that way waits in Riot Client for Play. Exo's Play already asks Riot Client to start the game itself, and now keeps Riot Client's window hidden for the whole launch, not only its first 12 seconds, so a cold start no longer puts it in front before the game appears.
  • Join a group call that's already going. A DM or group DM call that started before Exo connected to Discord (on your phone, or before Exo restarted) never showed, so there was no Join. Exo now hears about every call already on as it connects, as Discord's own app does, and the call shows above the conversation with Join call. The DM's Call button joins a call that's on instead of ringing everyone again. The call now sits under the DM's own header, as in Discord, instead of above it, at about a third of the window at most (its tiles about a fifth smaller), and its buttons sit in a slimmer bar inside the call rather than on its edge.
  • One message can't stop Discord. A server invite whose server didn't send its member count stopped all of Discord with "Discord stopped". The invite card now just leaves the count out, and a message or card under it that can't be drawn says so in its place while the rest of the chat goes on. Your account's profile page had the same fault when you have no profile color.
  • Shaders compile the quickest games first. Compile queues the games that take least time on your PC first, so the most games are ready soonest; a game you drag keeps the place you put it.
  • Exo's source is no longer published. From 3.18.0, Exo Launcher's code is private, as Exo Browser's is: this page keeps the releases, the changelog, the help and the privacy policy, and LICENSE says what that changes. Exo Mouse, Exo Shaders and Exo Shield stay open source in their own repositories.
  • Installed apps names Exo. Windows' Installed apps listed Exo Launcher's maker as "Erix (ImAvgErix)", the old GitHub name, while Exo Cinema and Exo Browser each said something else. All three now name Exo, with getexo.app as their page and the Exo Discord for help, and Exo Launcher puts its entry right as it starts, so its version there is always the one you run.
  • Around a sheet, the pointer says it closes it. A store page, a game's sheet, Captures, PC's Apps, and Discord's settings and dialogs close when you press the dimmed space around them, but that space showed the plain arrow while only the Bar's buttons showed the hand. The space around now shows the hand everywhere, and the sheet itself keeps its own pointer.
  • Newer parts. Exo runs on the Windows App SDK 2.6.4 (it fixes a crash when a WebView2 took the focus while still starting), built with the .NET 10.0.403 SDK on the .NET 10.0.14 runtime, the Windows SDK build tools 10.0.28000.2957, and Exo Setup's window on the WebView2 SDK 1.0.4314.35 like the app. Dictation and Only My Voice run on sherpa-onnx 2.0.1 with ONNX Runtime 1.31.1, which writes out a 30-second take in about 1 second after Stop instead of 1.3; your learned voiceprint reads the same as before. Epic games install with Legendary 0.21.1, and GOG games with gogdl 1.4.0, which fixes GOG updates that stopped at "Task writer failed" and a patch it couldn't open that left gogdl hanging. Calls are end-to-end encrypted with Discord's libdave 1.2.2, which keeps everyone's keys in step when two people join or leave at the same moment and never reuses a key's frame counter, on OpenSSL 3.5.9 (it was 3.0.7, from 2022), which also changes the call's keys in about half the time, and your voice is coded with Opus 1.6.1, about a fifth less work to send and no longer needing Microsoft's Visual C++ runtime on the PC; Exo fixes Opus 1.6's clipping of a loud voice, so you sound as before. Upscaler updates start from AMD's FidelityFX SDK 2.4.0 (FSR 4.1.2 upscaling, 4.0.2 frame generation), and a FidelityFX release Exo had already found by itself is no longer listed twice. The web UI takes hls.js 1.7.4 and Tabler Icons 3.50.0, and Discord's emoji search words come from emojibase 18.

3.17.1 — October 27, 2026

  • Exo Voice keeps the room out while you play. Noise suppression dropped to its light mode (its first stage alone, which leaves close and tonal noise in) whenever a few runs took over 10 ms, a line from a worker thread it no longer has: with a game running it went light every few minutes and your room came through. It now goes light only when the PC would fall behind the 20 ms frame.
  • Only My Voice never holds you back when you are the loud one. A voice as loud as yours at your microphone is never held back as someone else's: someone across the room or a TV reaches your mic well under your own voice, while your voice in a game, louder and excited, was misjudged and held back, again and again. Your level is learned from what goes out as yours.
  • Exo Voice is ready when Discord is. Noise suppression's model loads as Exo's Discord connects and stays warm while it is connected, and Only My Voice's voice model loads then too, so a call's first words are cleaned and judged at once instead of after a model loads.
  • Only My Voice learns your voice as calls hear it. It learned in calls only from speech it already judged clearly yours, which your voice in a call (in a game, tired) rarely was, so only teaching it again changed it. Now speech as loud as yours at your microphone teaches it too, a step at a time and never away from the voice you taught, so a TV or someone across the room never becomes you.
  • The song above your call just shows the song. Its pause and play button is gone; the Bar's player has the controls.
  • One clean ring around whoever is talking. A call tile's green speaking ring sat 2 pixels outside the tile, with a strip of black between them that read as a second, dark line; it now sits on the tile's own edge, over their picture or camera, as the call's members wear theirs, and their picture no longer lights up as well.
  • Labels over art are one clean shape. A call tile's name, a stream's name and buttons, Continue's title plates, More and Play, and the badges on covers had the glass's thin light line around their dimmed glass, which over a bright picture read as a second edge or a shadow. They are the dimmed glass alone now.

3.17.0 — October 27, 2026

  • Exo never writes to your controller during a game, even when it starts mid-game. An Edge's calibration and a DualSense's light and sound settings go back when the controller connects, but only with no game running. Exo judged that from its library, which isn't read yet the moment Exo starts, so Exo started or restarted during a match could write to the controller then. It now waits for the library first.
  • Your controller's light and sound. A DualSense or Edge on a cable takes its light bar color or off, its player light, and its microphone, speaker, vibration and trigger effects from its page, sent again each time it connects and after each game. Game profiles change them for the games you link and put them back when the game ends. The light bar now really changes: Exo had sent each color with the flag that hands the bar back to the controller's own blue.
  • Games read your controller themselves, always. Exo turns Steam Input off for every installed Steam game that supports a PlayStation controller, always, with no switch to find, so it gets the controller at its full rate with its haptics, adaptive triggers and gyro, and turns Steam's desktop layout off. Games that only read Xbox controllers keep Steam Input, since without it a DualSense does nothing there. A Steam game's Properties still choose per game, and a change you make in Steam stays yours. Steam takes it when it next closes, or closes for a moment.
  • DualSense Edge profiles leave Exo. Exo no longer edits the four profiles an Edge switches with its Fn buttons. The profiles already in your Edge stay as they are, and game profiles set its light, sound and vibration for each game instead.
  • Listen along with anyone, on any service. When a Discord friend is listening to a song, Listen along plays the same song at the same moment on your own Spotify, Apple Music or YouTube Music, and keeps following them song after song, in a call too. It's in Jam, which every service now has, and under their song in Active Now. Exo finds the song on your service by its name, artists and length, and says so when it isn't there.
  • A Jam box above Voice connected. In the Discord room, one slim row above your call shows who you're listening along with (with Stop), a friend in your call who's playing a song (with Listen), or the friends listening to the same song as you. It shows only when there's something to show.
  • Your avatar's menu and the mute and deafen arrows, as in Discord. Your avatar at the foot of the Discord room opens Online, Idle, Do not disturb and Invisible, each for 15 minutes, an hour, 8 hours, 24 hours, 3 days or for good (kept on your account, so every device goes back to Online on time, and Exo does even if it was closed then), with when a chosen one ends; Set or Edit custom status, Edit profile, Switch accounts and Copy user ID. Switch accounts lists every account signed in on this PC (up to five, each kept as encrypted as the first), switches in one click, and Add an account signs in another with your phone without signing out of the one you're on. The arrows beside Mute and Deafen pick your microphone or speakers and set their volume.
  • Report, as in Discord. Report message (on someone else's), Report on a person, Report server (one you don't own) and Report event open Discord's own report: its questions come from Discord step by step (what happened, then more about it), with what you're reporting at the top, a summary of your choices before Report, and Block, Ignore or (for moderators) Delete message on the last step.
  • A server's right-click menu, in full. Right-click a server's icon for Hide muted channels, Show all channels, Server settings with each page you may open (it opens straight on that page, over wherever you are), Privacy settings, Edit per-server profile (Profiles opens on that server), Create channel, category and event, Move to (first or last in its folder or the list, or out of its folder), Copy server info (its icon's and banner's links) and Report server. Notification settings, here and in the server name's menu, add Suppress highlights, Mute new events and Mobile push notifications. Right-click a server folder for Mark folder as read and Folder settings (its name and color).
  • A thread's and forum post's menu, and your threads in the sidebar. Right-click a thread or forum post (in the sidebar, the Threads list or a forum) for Mark as read, Mute it for a while, Notification settings, Join or Leave (Follow or Unfollow a post), and for its owner or a moderator Edit tags (up to five of the forum's), Pin post, Close and Lock, then Copy link and Delete. The threads you're in that are open show under their channel with Discord's curved line, unread ones lit, as Discord lists them: Exo now asks for a server's threads when it opens, as Discord's client does.
  • Clips and recordings at a steady 60 fps. A game running faster than the clip (or at its own 60) was recorded short of it: a COD replay came out at 55.8 fps, a frame lost every few, because Windows handed pictures over on a beat that drifted off the clip's. A clip now runs on its own clock, each frame the newest picture, evenly spaced, with the sound in step; a game slower than the clip keeps every picture it drew. Exo Capture has a self-test that records a window of its own at 150, 60 and 45 fps and checks the frames.
  • Exo Notes, in Captures. Captures has a Notes side, and the Capture menu has New note and Open notes (without Exo Capture too); Ctrl+K reaches New note and every note by its words. Each note is a plain text or Markdown file in Documents\Exo Notes (your user folder's Exo Notes when Documents is kept in OneDrive, so notes never reach a cloud), saved as you type and named for its first line: nothing to save, nothing lost. The notes you had open come back as tabs after a restart. A file opened from elsewhere is written only when you press Save (Ctrl+S), as Notepad does; its edits wait on this PC until then, through a restart too, and its encoding and line endings stay as they were. Find and replace with match case, whole word and regular expressions, every match lit; Windows' own spell check, with its suggestions and Add to dictionary on a right-click (off for logs, settings and code); dictation; pins; search through every note's words; Markdown's formatting bar, Edit and Preview, and checklists you tick; and a note written while a game runs carries the game's name. To open text files with Exo instead of Notepad, Notes' More menu offers Exo for .txt, .md and .log in Open with and Windows' Default apps, for you only and after asking, then opens Windows' Default apps, where you choose it; Stop opening text files with Exo takes it back.
  • New drivers found without looking. Every half hour while nothing plays, Exo checks for a newer graphics driver (NVIDIA's Game Ready, Studio or hotfix on the branch you picked, AMD's or Intel's), chipset driver (when Exo doesn't keep it up to date by itself) and Lucid, and tells you once per version, with Open to where its update is. Before, a new driver showed only when PC > Graphics was open. NVIDIA's hotfixes are now also found on NVIDIA's download server, not only in its forum's announcements: hotfix 621.12 went unseen the day it came out because the forum's search hadn't caught up.
  • Search fields as bright as what's around them. A search field's words and magnifier (Discord's header, Friends, a forum, Find or start a conversation; the Store's and Music's) were a step dimmer than the icons beside them; they're now the same. _Verified: on screen (Discord's header: field, placeholder and icons all the same gray)._ A field's outline is now the same even line as every other control's; it was a third dimmer.
  • One way to find new versions, for everything. Every check for a newer version (graphics and chipset drivers, .NET, Lucid, controller firmware, Exo itself) now compares versions the same way (26.10 and 26.10.0 are one version; before, some checks showed an update for what was installed, or called a finished install failed) and looks them up the same way: one request at a time, the answer kept (on disk for drivers), an old answer shown at once while it's asked again, a failure keeping the last answer and trying again after 5 minutes, then twice as long each time, and a maker's "slow down" respected. Exo looks for a newer Exo every 6 hours while it runs, and its update banner offers one that came out since it started.
  • A device's picture flies smoothly to its page. Opening a device flies its picture from its card to the page's, whole and centred the whole way, while the page fades in around it; the whole card grown into the page used to throw the picture off its place, then snap it there.
  • A press outside a menu always closes it. Two presses never reached Exo, so the menu or popover stayed open: one on the Bar's empty space, which Windows took to drag the window (while anything is open, the Bar now takes presses but for a thin strip at its top, which still drags), and one outside the page (another app, a window Exo hosts such as Spotify's, or an embedded frame), which now closes open menus and popovers as Windows' own menus close; a sheet or dialog stays.
  • A game's page closes with one press outside it, and has no close button. A press outside a game's page (in the Library, Home or the Store) sometimes did nothing: a press soon after opening was taken as part of the clicks that opened it, wherever it landed. Now only a second click on the spot that opened the page is, so a double click on a tile still leaves it open. The close button at its top corner is gone; a press outside or Escape closes it.
  • Exo on a screen stood on end, and on 16:10 screens. Exo lays its page out as on a 1080p screen and draws it larger on a bigger one, but it judged a screen by its height alone: a 1080p screen stood on end was zoomed 1.78 times, so Exo was laid out 607 pixels wide, with the Bar's capsules running into each other. A screen now gets the zoom it has lying down (a 1080p or 1440p screen on end is laid out 1080 wide), and a 16:10 screen keeps 1080p's width with a little more height. The window's smallest size takes the same zoom, so a 4K screen at 100% can't shrink Exo below what a 1080p screen allows.
  • Nothing in the Bar overlaps, at any width. On a narrow window the mouse's battery ran under Capture and Settings. The mouse now gives way when it no longer fits (first when the song beside it would lose its title), the song steps down to play alone instead of being cut off, and on the narrowest windows the tabs and Tools close up. A mouse with no battery to show had an empty capsule where the Bar drops names; it shows a mouse there now.
  • More in a narrow or upright window. The room tabs keep their names down to about 1040 pixels wide, where they used to drop to icons at 1500, unless a call or download sits beside them and needs the room. Continue shows four games when it goes two across, two rows with no gap; with three, the game you're playing or played last takes the whole first row.
  • Discord's Home tile is Liquid Glass. The tiles in Discord's server list that aren't a picture (Home's mark, Add a server, folders, servers with no icon) are glass like every control, not a gray square.
  • Discord's data and privacy switches stay off. Each time Exo's Discord connects it turns off whatever your account has on: Discord using your data to improve and personalize itself, sponsored content from your activity or third-party data, your messages on Discord's web pages, profile-update pings to friends, Share my activity and messages from friends in games. Those switches are gone from Exo's Discord settings, and so is Language & time: your account's language and 12- or 24-hour time follow your PC's.
  • Your song above your call, and nothing shared. The song Exo's music is playing, or has paused, shows in the Discord room above Voice connected and your profile, with its cover and pause or play. It shows only inside Exo: Exo no longer tells Discord what you play or listen to, whatever your Discord account's Share my activity says, and that switch is gone from Exo's Discord settings. Your own activity from your other devices (Spotify on your phone) now shows on your own row and profile, as Discord's app shows it.
  • Everything about your PC on one card. PC › Overview now lists the processor and its cores; the graphics card with its video memory, driver, DirectX and Resizable BAR; the memory's size, type, speed and slots; every drive with its free space and disk; the motherboard and BIOS; Windows and its build; Secure Boot, TPM and Memory integrity; your connection's speed; and the screen. It's read from Windows itself: no permission prompt, nothing sent anywhere.
  • Judge a store game before you buy it. A game's page in the Store now shows its Recent reviews, the last 30 days on Steam, under the all-time score; whether it plays with a controller, and a PlayStation one; its languages; an account it needs besides Steam, such as a Ubisoft account; and its DRM, such as Denuvo's activation limits. The Store says when a Steam seasonal sale is on or a week away, and the Calendar marks its first and last day. A friend's game being looked up no longer stops the search you're typing in the Store.
  • Your screen rests; your game keeps it on. Exo's power plan now turns the screen off after 30 minutes without keyboard or mouse, so a still desktop doesn't burn into an OLED. While a game from your library runs, however you started it, Exo keeps the screen on and the PC awake, so a game played on a controller never goes dark or sleeps.
  • A picture for every device. The Devices room shows the maker's own picture for every mouse Exo Mouse knows (the same mouse its page shows), the headphones, headsets and IEMs in Lucid's list, every VAXEE but its Ninjutso collab, 197 keyboards (the Ducky One X and One X Mini among them), and the DualSense in Techno Red and the Ghost of Yotei edition (614 pictures in all, from 322), Wooting's lit and angled in each layout (a 60HE v2 with its split spacebar). A mouse is matched by its maker as Exo Mouse knows it and by its own name before the shared picture Exo Mouse gives several models (a DeathAdder V2 X HyperSpeed is no longer shown as a DeathAdder V2), and a device named for its maker never takes another maker's picture (a Delux M600 Pro showed Logitech's PRO Wireless). Exo also knows a DualSense in Techno Red and the Monster Hunter Wilds, Ghost of Yotei, God of War 20th Anniversary and Astro Bot Joyful editions by their serials. The cut keeps a white or clear shell beside a white background, finds the background behind a keyboard cropped to the frame and drops stray specks, so every picture sits clean and centered. A model whose only official pictures are on a dark or busy background, or a clear shell on white, keeps Exo's drawing.
  • Optimize Modern Warfare 4. Call of Duty's page has an Optimize line under Shaders: one press sets MW4's own settings for your PC and setup where the guides, benchmarks and shared configs agree: borderless on your screen's highest refresh (the mouse kept in the game with a second screen; on a G-Sync or adaptive-sync screen, frames held 3 under its top rate), your card's low-latency mode with V-Sync and frame generation off, render threads for your processor, full render resolution with your card's own upscaler and sharpening at the preset your screen suits (DLSS's faster CNN model on an RTX 20 or 30), no blur of any kind, textures and the video memory budget for your card's memory, the costly effects lowest (detail and terrain kept at Medium on a strong card), the widest field of view for your screen with aiming scaled to it, raw mouse input (no acceleration, filtering or smoothing, ADS multipliers eased in, no scroll delay), music and cutscenes muted and reduce tinnitus on so footsteps carry, MW4's headphones mix in stereo without Windows Sonic when you listen on headphones, no quality drop when you hold still, and no intro videos. Then it says Applied. It changes only those lines in the game's settings files, only to values the files themselves allow, keeps what they held, and changes nothing while the game runs.
  • Dictation's voice line stands out. The line across a text box while you dictate is twice as thick, rises to nearly half the box with your voice over a fainter wave running the other way, and ripples while you're quiet, so you can see it's listening.
  • Steam stays current, on its beta. Steam downloads its own updates while it runs but installs them only as it next starts, which is when its "Updating Steam" box gets in the way of Play. Exo reads Steam's update log and, once an update waits, starts Steam again out of sight while no game runs and Steam's window is closed (at most once an hour). Exo also puts Steam on its Beta Update once, as Steam's own Client Beta Participation does; leave the beta in Steam and Exo leaves it.
  • Shorter Settings, nothing that can't change. Settings › Game stores no longer has the Steam overlay switch, Count hours from all my accounts or Notifications and sounds: the overlay is off for every Steam game and a game's Properties turn it on, hours always add up every Steam and Epic account on this PC, and Steam's and Epic's own pop-ups and sounds are always off. A PC that had the overlay switch on goes back to it off once.
  • Downloads say what they're doing, nothing more. While a game installs or updates, its Play button, its Continue card and its Library tile say Installing, Downloading or Updating with moving dots instead of a bar and figures; Downloads keeps the numbers.
  • Switch to Epic's new launcher. Settings › Epic › Account offers Switch to Epic's new launcher while this PC has the old one. After you confirm, Exo downloads Epic's installer from Epic, keeps it only if Epic Games signed it, and opens it; Epic's installer moves your games, saves and purchases over and takes out the old launcher. Never while a game plays.
  • Lucid waits for your game, and knows your headset. A Lucid change made while a game runs goes in when the game ends, so its EQ never drops out mid-match (Lucid starts again to take a change); the Lucid row says so meanwhile. A USB or wireless headset that names itself to Windows ("Arctis Nova 7") is offered as Looks like Arctis Nova 7 with Use it, when Lucid has its tuning.
  • Keep your Discord messages off the web. Discord's settings › Data & privacy has Discord's newest switch, Show my message content from Discoverable channels on web pages: turned off, what you send in a Discoverable server's public channels stays off the web pages Discord now makes of them (on by default for adults in the US). Saved where Discord's own app keeps it, so it holds on every device.
  • Back and Forward in Discord, and 300-character bios. In the Discord room the mouse's side buttons, or Alt+Left and Alt+Right, go back and forward through the servers, DMs, channels and threads you've been in, as Discord's app does. Your bio takes 300 characters, as Discord now allows.
  • Discord drafts survive a restart. What you were writing in a conversation and hadn't sent is kept on this PC when Exo closes or restarts, as Discord's app keeps it.
  • Controller sound keeps your defaults. Turning a controller's speaker and mic on in Windows no longer makes Windows switch your microphone (and your call audio) to the controller: Exo puts your defaults back as they come on.
  • Security fixes from code scanning. A Steam cover is taken only from Steam's own image hosts (a look-alike name that only ends the same way no longer passes), a file you attach in Discord previews only as an ordinary picture (never an SVG), and Exo's test tools escape what they put into page code.
  • Rebuild shaders for Call of Duty. Call of Duty's Properties › Installed files shows the size of its shader cache and offers Rebuild shaders, the fix for colors or textures gone wrong after a game or driver update: it deletes the cache of each title (MW4, its campaign) on Steam or Battle.net, and the game builds it again as it starts, which takes a few minutes. It asks first and waits until the game is closed.
  • No more "Run in safe mode?" in Call of Duty. Stop now asks a game to close the way its own window's close button does and gives it up to 20 seconds to quit before closing it, so it saves and signs out cleanly. Before, it also closed Call of Duty's bootstrapper, which took the game down mid-quit; the game then thought it had crashed and asked to start in safe mode, which resets its settings. If it does crash, Exo clears its leftover mark as it ends and again before Exo starts it, so it still won't ask.
  • Smaller fixes. A game's Play button is the same glass as Uninstall beside it, and while a game starts it says Preparing with moving dots instead of a loading line, then turns to Stop; Continue's Play does the same, and Friends are playing's has no spinner. A Continue card's, a game tile's and a Store card's glass no longer blinks out and back as a page opens and closes, and a Store card flies into its page's banner the way a game opens in Library. Every glass edge is one thin line, the Discord dock and the room panels included (some drew two side by side), and the Bar no longer blinks darker as rooms switch. Exo's small grey notes are gone app-wide: no more lines explaining what a setting does, only real values such as a version, a folder, a measured result or "Signed in as". A Store page sits on its own glass, with no game art blurred behind it, and the Store's cards, banner and calendar show a game you just wishlisted. Exo's glass is clear everywhere: the Bar's capsules, buttons and a card's plate have no tint, so on the black page they are black inside one thin line, and over art they show the art, dimmed enough that their words stay readable. Instant replay can keep the last 10 minutes. A page you opened before comes back as you left it: Devices keeps its cards, PC keeps your games' upscalers for 30 minutes, and a Store page's screenshot strip loads Steam's small copies. Find ports is gone: each device's tag says what's best for it (Move to CPU USB, Plug in without the hub). The Bar's room, PC's page and a Store pick you're on show a ring instead of a grey fill, and the Bar's names are white.

3.16.0 — October 26, 2026

  • Your controller gets a page: the Controller Lab. A DualSense, DualSense Edge, DualShock 4, Xbox controller or other gamepad opens to one screen laid out like Exo Mouse: its picture, its link and charge, a DualSense's firmware against Sony's newest and an Edge's active profile; both sticks and triggers live; a stick test (center and wander with the sticks left alone, then reach and roundness rolled around the edge, with a verdict per stick); its polling rate measured, with the reports that brought nothing new counted by the controller's own clock; the USB port it's on; and what sits between it and your games.
  • Calibrate the sticks on the controller itself. A DualSense or Edge runs its own center and range routine from Exo, the way its maker's tools do: Exo measures the centers before and after, won't finish the range until both sticks have gone all the way round, and Undo writes back what the controller had. It lasts until the controller is unplugged; a DualSense can keep it, and an Edge gets it back each time it connects. Exo never changes a controller while a game runs, and only over a cable.
  • DualSense Edge profiles in Exo. The four profiles your Edge switches with its Fn buttons, edited in Exo and stored in the controller: each stick's response (Sony's presets with their adjustment and deadzone, with a picture of the curve), swapped sticks, each trigger's range, what the back buttons press, rumble and trigger effects. Save reads the profile back to check the controller kept it; Clear empties a slot; Undo puts back what the slot held. Because it lives in the controller, it works in every game and on any PC with nothing added in between.
  • Games read your controller themselves. One switch on the controller's page turns Steam Input off for every installed Steam game that supports a PlayStation controller, so it gets the controller at its full rate with its haptics, adaptive triggers and gyro, and turns Steam's desktop layout off. Games that only read Xbox controllers keep Steam Input, since without it a DualSense does nothing there. A Steam game's Properties have the same choice per game. Steam closes for a moment to take it, and switching it off puts back exactly what Steam had.
  • Where your devices plug in. Each device's page says which USB it's on: the processor's own, the chipset, an extra USB chip, a hub or a dock. The Devices room says when your mouse, keyboard or controller would gain from one of the processor's free ports, and Find them shows where each card is plugged in while you move its plug. Thanks to CPU Direct USB by Marius Heier for the idea.
  • Exo Mouse takes only what your mouse takes. Click debounce offers only the times your mouse accepts (a Hitscan stops at 8 ms, a GearHub at 10). Game profiles keep a polling rate for each connection, wired and wireless, so one never asks for a rate the other refuses, and carry a Logitech mouse's HITS tuning.
  • Exo Shaders brings in SCSKiller 1.3.3's fixes. After a compile, copying it into the game's shader cache and the driver saving its own show as steps with their progress instead of sitting at 100%, and a big cache isn't checked whole again while the game hasn't changed it. A compile no longer shows Compiling forever: one whose process is gone ends within 30 seconds, and a DirectX compile that makes no progress for 10 minutes ends with an error and picks up where it stopped next time. A compile stopped by the game or by GPU driver crashes keeps its progress and goes on past the spot where the driver kept failing, and on NVIDIA a rebuild after a launch compiles only the pipelines that are new. 32-bit games say they aren't supported yet, Call of Duty HQ and Warzone count as anti-cheat games, Gears of War: E-Day, God of War Ragnarök on AMD and Resident Evil Village compile what they use, a game moved to another drive keeps its compiled shaders on NVIDIA 621 and newer, and Clear cache works on every driver. Each game's files are read once more after the update, and a compile is asked for only where the new plan adds pipelines. The fixes inside the recorder and the compile program itself come with SCSKiller's next 1.4 build.
  • Smaller fixes. A friend's status under their name in your DMs reads clearly over a nameplate. Game controllers stay out of reach of Exo Mouse's connection to your mouse.

3.15.5 — October 26, 2026

  • Devices: what you plug in, each as itself. The Devices room is your devices: a large card each for your mouse, keyboard, what you listen on and your controller, with the maker's own picture of that exact model (a Wooting 60HE with its split spacebar, a DualSense Edge in its color), its name, how it's linked and its battery. Pictures are fetched once, cut out and framed alike, and kept on this PC. A press grows the card into its page, and Devices at its top folds it back. Keyboards and controllers show what they are, marked Coming soon, until Exo sets them up.
  • Your headphones get a page of their own. What you listen on opens to one screen laid out like Exo Mouse: your headphones large, what Windows plays them through and hears you on, and Lucid beside them (its app, Game Mode, Night Shift, its sounds). Windows can't tell which pair is on the jack, so you pick yours from the hundreds Lucid knows, and the card and page show that pair. Lucid moved here from Settings, and Night Shift from the PC room.
  • Dictation in Exo's text boxes. A mic in the search fields and Discord's boxes turns what you say into text on this PC, with NVIDIA's Parakeet model: press it, talk while a live line follows your voice, press Stop and the words land where your cursor was. Nothing leaves the PC. The installer fetches the model with the rest of what Exo needs, so it's ready on first run. Settings › General › Dictation turns it off, and Discord keeps that place for voice messages.
  • PC: one room for this PC. System is now PC: Overview (this PC's About card and Best settings), Graphics, Display, Performance and Apps, each in the page list with where it stands.
  • Game runtimes, all in one place. PC › Apps lists every runtime games use, Visual C++ 2005 to 2026, DirectX, .NET, XNA, OpenAL and PhysX, from the makers' own downloads: what's missing or older installs in one press with one Windows prompt, and Repair all puts every Visual C++ back. It replaces the runtimes row in Settings › About.
  • Exo's own uninstaller. PC › Apps lists every program on this PC, desktop and Store, as Windows lists it, each with its own icon (from its shortcut, its program or its uninstaller when Windows has none), publisher, size and install day, sorted by size, name or newest, with a search. Games stay in Library, where their store removes them. Uninstall asks in Exo's own window, runs the app's own uninstaller and waits it out to the end (NSIS and Inno Setup hand off to a copy of themselves, Squirrel to the app itself), then shows what it left behind before anything goes: what is surely its with Delete leftovers, and what only has its name apart, with Delete these too. A program whose uninstaller is gone offers Clear what's left. Exo itself, anti-cheat and the graphics driver say why they can't go here; runtimes, drivers, store clients and what Exo uses are removed with a warning. Files go to the Recycle Bin and registry entries are saved to a .reg file first. Settings › Clean up is gone: the apps Exo does the job of lead this list.
  • Microsoft Edge can go; WebView2 stays. Edge's entry refuses to uninstall outside the EEA. Exo removes it with Edge's own setup, keeps the WebView2 runtime that Exo and other apps run on, and tells Edge Update not to install Edge again, only once Edge is really gone. Allow Edge again takes that back.
  • A deep clean of what an app leaves. After an uninstall Exo finds what the best uninstallers find, tied to the app itself, never a search by a word: its folders under its own names, shortcuts (Steam's links too), startup entries, file types and Open With entries, its COM classes and shell extensions, App Paths, services, scheduled tasks, event log sources, Windows Installer records, SharedDLLs and firewall rules, and what Windows keeps about it. A folder that holds something still installed always stays, and generic or company names never match.
  • Removing Discord finishes the job. Discord's updater can't delete itself and stays behind marked as dead; Exo read that as Discord still installed and stopped before clearing anything. A dead updater no longer counts as an install.
  • Apps Exo replaces stay gone. Removing Snipping Tool, Photos, Game Bar or Clipchamp also takes them out of Windows' image for every user (Windows asks once), so a feature update can't bring them back.
  • Uninstalling a game clears what Windows kept about it. Once its store has removed it, its dead shortcuts, compatibility and recent-apps records, Game Bar's entry and crash dumps go. Its folder only goes when the store left it empty, so saves and mods stay.
  • Uninstalling Exo puts things back. Launchers Exo kept from starting with Windows start with it again, Graphics for games' choices return, Steam starts online again if Exo had put it offline (asking an open Steam to close first), and the parts Exo fetched beside itself go. Your Exo data stays for a reinstall.
  • When Steam is down, play anyway. If Steam's servers are out, Exo says so and can start Steam in its own offline mode for the account it signs in as, so games that run offline still start. Exo writes only Steam's two offline values, keeps a copy of the file, and puts exactly those values back when Steam is up again.
  • Recomps in Library. Native PC ports from the decompilation projects installed through their own tools show up in Library with their covers, and their saves are never treated as leftovers.
  • Music opens in one piece. The first time the room opens it no longer shows squares, then a frame, then the music. Its own frame stands in, the library pane, Home's shelves and the player, each in the place and size of what comes, until your library and Home are both in, then it fades once.
  • Waits that fit where they are. A bar only where Exo knows how far along it is (downloads, installs, compiles); a button that's working spins its own glyph; cards and rows that are loading shine in place; and a quiet refresh shows nothing. Library's Refresh spins and shines the covers again instead of drawing a bar on the button.
  • One way to ask, one way to tell. Every question in Exo, onboarding and the installer included, is the same dialog: what will happen in its title, Cancel first and ready for Enter, the action named and red when it deletes. Notices stack at the top right in one style, with their actions in them.
  • Liquid Glass, sharper. Glass edges follow Apple's latest: a darkened edge that holds over bright art, a light hairline inside it and a brighter line along the top. The bend at a capsule's edge is drawn at twice the screen's resolution and no longer steps or looks pixelated, a game's page keeps its rim over its art, and opening a menu inside Settings or Discord's settings no longer drops the glass behind it.
  • No scrollbars. Everything still scrolls by wheel, trackpad, keys and touch; a long list fades into its pane's edge instead, as Music's library, Discord's lists and PC › Apps do. Windows' contrast themes keep their scrollbars.
  • Exo Shaders rests until you ask. Shaders compile only when you press Compile; with nothing to do, the service uses no CPU or memory to speak of, and background work waits until nobody is using your PC, not just until a game closes: no keyboard or mouse for a while, nothing full screen, no sound playing or call going, and other apps quiet.
  • Exo Mouse checks your CPU core. Every mouse's page has CPU core: Measure reads the moving mouse for ten seconds and says whether its input waits on a busy CPU, and can give it a quiet core of its own.
  • Smaller fixes. Removing a game's DLC no longer marks the game as Playing. Select all in Library works while a refresh runs. The Store opens faster and without a bar across its top. In Library's right-click menu, New folder sits with the other actions.

3.15.4 — October 26, 2026

  • Only my voice cuts off without a click. When it started holding back a voice that was going out, it stopped the sound mid-wave, and the call heard a click each time. It now fades that last moment out, as the gate does at the end of a sentence.
  • A hum or buzz near your mic stays out of the call. A steady buzz with overtones, like a ground loop or a power supply at 60, 120 and 180 Hz, sounded like a voice to noise suppression. It kept your mic open for as long as it lasted, whether Only my voice was on or off. Exo now knows a sound that holds one level for two seconds isn't a voice: the buzz goes out only as it starts, and your voice over it still goes out in full. Knocks, clicks, low rumble and fans were already kept out, and still are.
  • Only my voice stays on through noise. Its safety stop, which turns it off when it seems to be holding back your own voice, counted noise it held back too. A hum or a vibration held back for 20 seconds turned it off for the rest of the call, and the noise then went out. Only voices count toward the stop now.
  • RTX Video is set the way NVIDIA Control Panel sets it. Exo now takes the same steps as the panel and the NVIDIA app before changing Super Resolution or HDR, waits out a busy driver, and reads Off the way the panel does. The message when the driver doesn't keep a choice no longer blames the NVIDIA app, which never changes RTX Video by itself, and Exo logs what the driver keeps so a PC that shows Set elsewhere can be explained.

3.15.3 — October 26, 2026

  • The voice meter and Teach Exo my voice work again. 3.15.2 kept 20 seconds of the meter for teaching, but only eight seconds of which moments were speech: past the eighth second every meter frame failed, so the meter froze and teaching your voice failed.
  • Only my voice off holds nobody back, not even on paper. With it off, Exo still listens to learn your voice, but it said it was holding voices back and could show the panel's "kept holding back your own voice" note; it doesn't now.

3.15.2 — October 26, 2026

  • Only my voice never keeps you muted. A voiceprint taught from a few seconds could judge your own voice in a game not yours and hold you back for the rest of the call: Discord stopped showing you talking and nothing was sent. If it holds back more than half of 20 seconds of your speech, it stops holding back for that call and the voice panel says so. Teaching now takes at least eight seconds of speech (three before), out of the last 20 seconds the meter heard.
  • Only my voice keeps learning your voice. Teach Exo my voice used to learn once, from as little as three seconds, and never again. Now that take is where it starts: in calls, every stretch that is clearly you is kept, and each 30 seconds of it is folded into your voiceprint, so it fills in another microphone position, a tired or a loud voice. It stays anchored to what you taught: anything less than clearly like it is never folded in, so a TV or someone in the room can't become you, not even a little at a time. Discord settings › Voice & video says how much it has learned from, with Teach again and Forget, and it learns whether or not Only my voice is on. It stays on this PC, encrypted.
  • A purge clears at once. When a moderator's bot purges messages (or Discord removes a banned member's), they disappear from the chat right away, as in Discord, instead of staying until the channel was opened again.
  • Mentions name the person. A message that mentions someone it doesn't carry (a bot posting with pings off, as Exo Bot files bugs) showed @unknown-user; Exo looks the person up once, as Discord does, and shows their name.
  • New forum posts show up while you look. A forum open on screen took in a new post only once it was opened again; new and changed posts come in as they happen.
  • Razer receivers connect on their own. A Razer Viper V3 Pro on its receiver showed "Exo could not reach your mouse" with a browser's advice (pick the other interface, allow Chrome on macOS): Exo Mouse took the receiver's first interface, which has no settings reports. It now starts with the interface that has them and tries the others before giving up, and if Razer Synapse holds the mouse it says to quit Synapse in Exo's own words.
  • Refresh leaves hidden games alone. Library's Refresh fetched achievements and covers for games you hid, which nothing shows; it skips them now (the stores are still read whole, to know what you own).
  • RTX Video Super Resolution says more when the driver won't take it. On some PCs the row read Set elsewhere and no choice stuck. Exo now logs what the driver reports, so it can be decoded, and points to the NVIDIA app, which can own RTX Video.
  • Teaching your voice says what to do. A take too short asked you to talk in Voice Check, which is gone; it asks you to talk on the meter.

3.15.1 — October 26, 2026

  • Exo Voice is Exo's own. Noise suppression in Discord settings › Voice & video runs Exo's tuned DeepFilterNet chain end to end: nothing of Krisp's ships or runs anymore, and a saved Krisp choice becomes Exo Voice. The choices are Exo Voice, Standard and None. The first seconds of a call no longer let the room through while the model warms up — the gate holds until suppression is on, the way Discord's waits for its model — and a kept Voice Tune is applied again, so a quiet microphone reaches the gate at the level it was tuned to. Voice Check is gone; Hear your voice, a live playback of exactly what the call sends (the same model and settings as the call, where Voice Tune's had run stronger ones), takes its place, and Teach Exo my voice learns from the live meter instead of a recording.
  • The Store is made for you. Discover was Steam's charts as everyone sees them, so it led with Dota 2 and Counter-Strike for someone who has played both for years. It now starts with Picked for you, games closest to what you play and wish for, each marked on its cover with why: a bookmark for a game on your wishlist, or Similar to and the icon of the game you play that it is like, and Because you play shelves for the games you've played most lately, close to that game and with enough reviews to know they're good. The banner puts your wishlist and the big games nearest your taste first, with the same marks, and games you own (from any store) leave every shelf. Your hours come from every store: a game from Epic or Battle.net is found on Steam by its name, and VALORANT, League, Fortnite and other games not on Steam count by their kind. The picks turn a little each day. Featured games now light the black around the banner. Games Steam marks as adult only or with frequent sexual content are left out of every Store list, as Steam's own store does unless you opt in. Epic's free games no longer take a shelf, and a card is its cover and name, with only a coming game's date or a sale's discount under it: price and score are on the game's page. Nothing leaves your PC and no sign-in is needed; with nothing played and an empty wishlist, Discover is Steam's charts without what you own. The Calendar uses the same taste, so games from other stores count there too.
  • Look into a voice channel before joining. Clicking a voice channel opens it, as Discord's preview does: who's in it, who's live and whose camera is on, with Join voice; a double click joins at once. A channel you can't connect to still opens, so you can see who's there. Out of the call, the bar under it is Join voice itself, one Liquid Glass capsule instead of a green button inside a glass ring. Green in Discord is now only for what is on (online, in a call, live, allowed): an incoming call's Join and Join with video are glass like the rest of the call's controls, and Accept and Add Friend no longer turn green.
  • Exo Shaders starts on every NVIDIA PC. A driver whose registry names its file without a folder stopped the engine at start with "The path is empty" (reported to SCSKiller on a GTX 1070); that entry is skipped and one unreadable display key no longer stops it.
  • A mouse Exo can only read says so. A mouse OpenMouse can see but not set up yet (an Asus receiver with nothing paired, some Corsair, Attack Shark and AJAZZ models) showed settings with made-up values that every change failed on; it now shows the mouse, its battery and connection, and why its settings aren't there.
  • A calendar in the Store. A new Calendar view lays out what came out in the last two weeks and what is dated in the next six, a day at a time: games on your wishlist first, then games like the ones you play most, each with the reason under it. Hide games you own, show only your wishlist, or leave out Early Access. Coming up leads, from today, then Out recently, newest first; days sit side by side, each as wide as its games, and a day leads with its biggest releases. Coming games far down Steam's list are left out unless they are on your wishlist or close to what you play, so a day is not filled with tiny games. It needs no Steam sign-in: it reads Steam's public lists and uses your wishlist and playtime on this PC only, and a game known only as "Q1 2027" never gets a day.
  • Exo Mouse knows more mice. It moves to OpenMouse's 0.47.0 protocol library, which adds Scyrox and Hitscan mice (Hitscan wired, on its receiver or on its 8K receiver: DPI stages and colours, polling rate, lift-off, debounce, motion sync, angle snapping, ripple control and button mapping) and updates its Lamzu Atlantis and Pulsar drivers; Exo's Starlight X fixes carry over unchanged.
  • Laptops with two graphics chips can run games on the fast one. On a PC where Windows can pick between a built-in chip and a GeForce or Radeon, System › This PC offers Graphics for games: Exo asks Windows (in its own Graphics settings, for your user only) to run each game's programs on the fast chip, as a game starts and for the games you have now. Undo puts every program back as it was. It never touches a Riot game, an uninstaller, a crash reporter or an anti-cheat's program, and no game file changes.
  • Pausing a GOG download keeps what it has. Pause, and Pause downloads while I play, used to delete the half-downloaded game, so Resume began at zero. The unfinished folder is kept and carried on from; only Cancel throws it away.
  • Big libraries keep their cover thumbnails. A library whose covers alone passed the cache's file limit had every thumbnail deleted on each pass and drawn again on the next scroll. The limit now counts only what can be dropped.
  • A friend's game reads as the game. A clip recorder or overlay that reports a friend's game adds itself to the name, so Friends are playing showed "VALORANT with Medal" as a game of its own, apart from your VALORANT. The recorder's part (Medal, Overwolf, Outplayed, Blitz and the like) is left off, so it is VALORANT, with Play.
  • Friends are playing shows more. Games your friends are in that you don't have now show too, with a Store button that searches for it in Exo's Store, and the shelf holds eight games instead of four.
  • Stream volume while you watch. A person's menu had only User volume, their voice, so while you watched their Go Live the slider seemed to do nothing. As in Discord, it now has Stream volume and Mute stream for a stream you watch, kept in step with the stream's own controls.
  • Music fits a portrait screen. On a portrait monitor or a narrow window the player's buttons squeezed over each other and song names went down to one letter. The player now spans the room under the library with its buttons whole, a narrow page drops the album column and shows the song by its art, and Jam, Friend activity and Devices fold into More.
  • Portrait screens keep their words. At 720 and 540 px wide (a portrait 1080 screen at 150% and 200%), Library's Continue banners go two a row, Discord hides the member list and lays it over the chat when shown, its search folds to a magnifier, and System's panels go one column, instead of names a few pixels wide and buttons over their rows' words.
  • Reactions on Discord messages are bigger. The chip is 30 px tall with a 20 px emoji and a larger count.
  • Safer between the page and Exo. The Shaders page can only ask its engine for what it uses (it could ask it to add a program by path, delete caches or stop); the capture folder must be one you picked; removing a replaced app asks in Exo's own window; a request far over its size is refused before it is read; a store id is checked for length and the wishlist is capped at 1,000; a request reusing the id of one still running no longer answers the wrong one; install, update and resume answers no longer carry a path.
  • Updates, settings and downloads fail less.
