A healthy update is no longer rolled back because its first start receipt couldn't be written; settings read again when a scanner holds the file for a moment; the cover title map and a driver download no longer end half-written; a driver download that goes quiet stops after a minute; the DLSS write and its marker land together or not at all.
  • Smaller fixes. Riot Add account and Switch leave the signed-in account alone when it couldn't be saved; Battle.net's Diablo II: Resurrected and Diablo Immortal had swapped names in the owned list; Clean up in first-run setup no longer returns to Welcome after the last removal; Discord banners dismiss again after one is closed under the pointer; Update all no longer repeats a game already updating; Escape closes an open dropdown in Properties before the sheet; clicking the current room while another is opening cancels it; Discord menus take Up, Down, Home and End; a failed forward no longer re-sends to people already reached; a refused GIF favourite is put back; folder and dialog focus stays inside until closed; the music player no longer says Lossless with nothing playing.
  • Recordings take a third of the space. While you aren't playing and a laptop is plugged in, Exo Capture re-saves your recordings, older ones too and the biggest first, in the graphics card's careful encoder at a third of the size, one at a time. Before the smaller file replaces anything it is checked: audio byte for byte, length, frame count, HDR settings and a picture comparison against the original on 24 frames. The original goes to the Recycle Bin only after that; a file that doesn't pass stays as it was. A game starting stops it at once. Settings › Capture turns it off, and Make smaller on a recording's page does one right away. Measured on this PC: 28.6 MB to 9.2 MB for a test clip and 9.1 MB to 2.9 MB for a real recording, picture match 0.994 to 0.999, and the add-on's own 163-check test passes on an RTX 3070.
  • VALORANT and League hours correct themselves. A Riot ID's hours from tracker.gg were only ever raised, so one over-count from an earlier version stayed on screen for good. tracker.gg's lifetime total now replaces the old figure on each refresh; a partial reading (queues added up, one season) still only raises it.
  • An anti-cheat folder that is a link still counts. A folder named EasyAntiCheat, BattlEye or another anti-cheat that is a junction or link was skipped before its name was read, so a game could be treated as having none.
  • One press for the best settings. System › This PC leads with a row that names what is left to set for this PC (Exo's power plan for your processor, the driver profile for your card, HDR for HDR games) and sets them together. Each stays in its own section with its own Undo, and display modes, BIOS and Windows settings still ask first.
  • Buying in the Discord Shop asks in Exo's own window. A dialog from Exo names the item, the price and the card before anything is charged, so nothing but your click can start a purchase.
  • Add a game only takes what you picked. A game added from Library › Add a game must come from the file or folder you just chose in Windows' picker. Windows' error text for a link, folder or picker that wouldn't open no longer reaches the page (it is in the log).
  • Epic's settings are saved safely. Changing a game's DLC and Epic's notification switch write beside the file and move it into place, so a crash can't leave Epic's config cut off.
  • System › This PC tells you when GPU scheduling is off. On a GeForce RTX 40 or 50 (DLSS frame generation) or a Radeon RX 7000 or newer (FSR frame generation), a row says hardware-accelerated GPU scheduling is turned off and opens Windows' Graphics settings. A PC that never set it, a laptop's shared graphics and a workstation card show nothing.
  • Green and red buttons are readable in every theme. Voice join and hang up, Go Live stop, the unread badge and the permission toggles took white or black text that was too faint on one theme's green or red; they follow the theme now.
  • HDR is on before the game starts. A game you start from Exo that is set to Always, or that the wiki already lists with HDR, gets HDR a moment before its store launches it, not once it is running, so games that only look for HDR when they first start now offer it. HDR still goes back off when the game closes, and if the game never starts it goes back off after two minutes.
  • A long run of emoji no longer freezes a chat. A message of two dozen emoji and a word took a second or more to draw, again on every redraw; it is instant now, and a timestamp far in the future shows as written instead of failing the room.
  • Save Image only saves pictures. A link on Discord's servers named like a program (payload.exe) is saved with a picture's extension or not at all, never as a program in Downloads.
  • The colour profile under HDR stays put. Exo's SDR gamma 2.2 profile no longer gets a new name every second, so it is installed once instead of again on every look during an HDR game.
  • Setup works from the one-line install. The bootstrap script's empty app folder is no longer taken for another program's files, and a failed rollback after an update puts the new version back instead of leaving no app folder.
  • Big pictures send themselves smaller. An image over a quarter of a megabyte added to a Discord message is quietly remade as WebP before it goes up: it looks the same on Discord, takes a fraction of the room, and moves under the size limit that would have stopped the original. Animations and already-small files are left as they are, and a picture that would not shrink keeps its own file.
  • Snips save smaller. A snip's PNG now stores opaque shots in 24 bits and picks its filter row by row, about a third less space for the same pixels — nothing about the picture changes.
  • Any role color, not twenty. A role's color picker is a color field over a hue strip with a hex box and Default (no color), where the fixed palette once picked from Discord's twenty. The palette's custom swatch drew jagged beside the rest; the field replaces it and every other preset.

3.15.0 — October 23, 2026

Exo's Discord grows into the whole of Discord's: every page of Server Settings, group DMs and video calls in DMs, bookmarks and reminders, message requests, quick reactions, link previews that play and much more. Shaders tells ready games apart and is honest about compile times, snips start at once, and the Devices room thanks snekxs, who made OpenMouse.

  • Snip starts at once. Snip in the Capture menu waited for the menu to fade before freezing the screen; the menu now goes at once, and the snip overlay shows in about 65 ms instead of 240.
  • The snip editor stays light. It drew every snip at full size, a snip across several screens taking hundreds of MB in Exo's page; it now draws at the size it is shown (Save still keeps every pixel) and lets go of the picture as it closes. Five snips in a row leave Exo's page where it was.
  • When Windows runs out of memory, the log says who took it. Exo's page once restarted after a snip because Windows itself had run out of memory (Windows said so too); the snip was not the cause. If the page ever runs out of memory again, app.log records how full Windows' memory was and the programs holding the most.
  • A message's time fits beside it. Pointing at a message in a run shows its time in the avatar's column, as Discord does, a clear gap before the words and never cut off, at every text and chat size.
  • Channel topics, as Discord shows them. A channel's topic in its header shows its mentions, emoji and links instead of codes like <#2114…>, and pressing it (anywhere but a mention or link) opens Channel topic with all of it.
  • A Server Guide resource opens beside the guide with Discord's side header: the channel's name, its notifications and Close, instead of a copy of the channel's whole header that squeezed the name to one letter. A call's chat beside it shows the channel's own icon.
  • Forums load lighter. A forum's list asks Discord for each post's picture at the size it shows instead of the full photo (often 4K and several MB each), and a post's first line shows its emoji and mentions, with the author's picture beside their name instead of above it.
  • The Server Guide's welcome names you where the server's message says [@username], as Discord's does.
  • Mark Unread brings back New messages. After Mark Unread on a message, the bar counting what's new since then shows again while that message is above you, until you mark it read, as in Discord.
  • Shaders tells ready games apart. Teamfight Tactics read Not supported though Exo Shaders compiles it (all but a few encrypted files); it reads Ready · some files encrypted now, and only games whose shaders stay locked are left out. A game says what it waits for: Marvel Rivals says Start it once from Steam (its launcher clears the shader cache at a new season) instead of asking for a recording its anti-cheat would block, and a game whose ray tracing alone needs a recording reads ready, its poster saying Compile without ray tracing (partial); that last case is checked in code only.
  • Honest compile times. Shaders' times come from your PC's own compiles of each game, with reading and preparing its files counted. Until a game has compiled on your graphics driver its time is a range and the queue says rough estimate instead of promising a finish time, where it said 5 min for Counter-Strike 2 (its one cold compile here took 1 h 22 min) and 10 min for Beast of Reincarnation (about 1 h 40 min). A quick recompile of a game whose cache still held it no longer counts as how long it takes: VALORANT read under a minute, where a compile from scratch takes about 4.
  • Access, as Discord's. Server Settings › Access picks how people join: Invite only (with the rules members agree to), Apply to join (an application of up to five questions, short answer, paragraph, multiple choice or your rules, with Discord's examples and Apply from profile; leaving it asks to approve or reject the applications waiting) or Discoverable (Discord's checklist while the server doesn't meet it, then its description, category, tags, emoji information and language). Age-restricted server is there too. Seen on a real server; saving was tried only in the dev mock.
  • Server Insights. Server Settings › Server insights shows this week's visitors, communicators, new members and new member retention against last week's, as Discord's page does, and opens the full insights on Discord's website; a server under 500 members gets Discord's note instead. The note was seen on a real server, the numbers only in the dev mock.
  • Server tags in Discord's art. Server Settings › Server tag shows each of Discord's badges as Discord draws it, in the colors you pick, with the badge before the tag's name. Exo reads the art from Discord's own web client when you open the page; nothing of it ships with Exo, and if Discord's client changes shape the picker lists the badges by name. A server without Server Tags can try badges and colors, as in Discord, but can't set a name.
  • Linked roles. A role's Links tab, as Discord's: the accounts members must connect to get the role (Steam, X, Reddit, eBay and the rest, or the server's apps that link roles) and, where Discord reads them, how much of each, such as an account at least 30 days old or 10 Steam games, with all of them or any one needed. As in Discord, requirements start only on a role with no members. Seen on a real server; saving was tried only in the dev mock.
  • Onboarding, as Discord's. Server Settings › Onboarding turns it on or off with Discord's requirement shown, and Advanced server setup, and edits each part: the default channels new members start with (whole categories too), the questions asked before or after they join (up to 4 before, 15 in all, each answer giving channels and roles, with Discord's checks), and the Server Guide's welcome sign, 3 to 5 tasks and resource pages. Seen on a real server; saving was tried only in the dev mock.
  • Quick reactions. A message's hover bar starts with the three reactions you use most, and its menu with a row of four, as in Discord: a press reacts, and a second takes it back. Only emoji you can use there are offered, with Discord's own picks while you've used few.
  • Who's who in voice and the member list. People in a voice channel show LIVE while they stream, a camera while it's on, and their mute or deafen mark, the server's own in red and yours from their menu apart, as Discord shows them; a channel with a user limit shows how full it is (2/10). The member list shows the server owner's crown and a booster's gem, and the picture someone set for that server. The member list's marks were seen on a real server, the voice marks only in the dev mock.
  • Custom status. Discord settings › Account sets your custom status as Discord's Set your status does: an emoji, up to 128 characters, and when it clears (24 hours, 4 hours, 1 hour, 30 minutes or never), with Clear status; it shows under your name in the panel. Exo now always tells Discord your custom status while it's your active app, so using Exo no longer hides one you set elsewhere. Saving was tried only in the dev mock, to leave the account as it was.
  • Server pictures and voice channel statuses. Messages show the picture someone set for that server, as Discord does, instead of their own. A voice channel's status (what the call is about) shows under its name, with its emoji; in the channel you can set it (Set channel status, 500 characters) and a channel manager can clear it, as in Discord. Server pictures were seen on real servers, statuses only in the dev mock.
  • Message requests. DMs from people you aren't friends with, and the ones Discord files as spam, wait under Message requests (with a count) instead of among your DMs, as in Discord: each shows its latest message with Accept and Ignore, and opened, a request shows Discord's Accept the request line with Ignore and Accept DM where the message box goes. Seen only in the dev mock: the test account has no requests.
  • Calls know when they're recorded. While Exo Capture keeps a replay or records, Exo tells your calls it records them, as Discord's clips do, so everyone in the call is told their voice may be recorded; and when someone else in a call has clips on, the voice panel says so. A voice channel's status line no longer reshapes the voice panel.
  • Publish, Remove embeds, Remove all reactions. A message's menu has Discord's Publish in an announcement channel (to every server following it), Remove embeds on your message (or anyone's for a moderator), asked first unless you hold Shift, and Remove all reactions for moderators. Remove embeds is covered end-to-end, Publish and Remove all reactions only in code.
  • @silent messages. Start a message with @silent and it goes without notifying anyone, as in Discord, the word left out. A silent message shows Discord's crossed-out bell after its time and starts its own group, and a forward's note starting with @silent sends the forward silently too.
  • Modify attachment. A file waiting to be sent has Modify beside Mark as spoiler, as in Discord: rename it, give a picture or video a description (alt text, read out to people using screen readers), or mark it as a spoiler.
  • Previews of your own pictures show. A picture added to a message had an empty preview, and so did a new avatar or banner before you saved it: Exo's page blocked pictures made on your PC. They show now.
  • Long messages. Near Discord's limit (2,000 characters, 4,000 with Nitro) the message box counts down what's left, in red past it, and sending past it offers Upload as file, which sends the message as message.txt; the + menu's Upload your message as a file does the same. A paste longer than a message can be goes in as message.txt, as in Discord, where Exo used to cut it off at 4,000.
  • #channels, @roles, @everyone and @here in the message box. Typing # suggests the server's channels you can see, with their icon and category, and @ now suggests the server's roles in their colors and Discord's @everyone and @here after people; each goes into the message as Discord sends it.
  • The image viewer goes through a message's pictures. Opening one of several pictures in a message shows Previous and Next (the arrow keys too) and which one it is, and the viewer has Discord's Zoom (or a click on the picture) to see it at its own size, Save image, Open in browser and Close.
  • Video calls in DMs. A DM's header has Discord's Start Video Call beside Start Voice Call: the call rings as usual and your camera comes on as it connects. An incoming call has Join with video too.
  • Ignore & Block. Discord settings › Messaging permissions lists the people you blocked and ignored, as Discord's Ignore & Block does, each with Unblock or Unignore.
  • Invite friends, as Discord does. Invite people (the server's header, its menu, a channel's or a server's right-click) opens Discord's Invite friends: your friends with Invite, which sends them the link, the link with Copy, and Edit invite link to choose how long it lasts, how many uses and temporary membership. It used to copy a 7-day link and nothing else.
  • The server menu, as Discord's. It has Create channel, Create category and Create event for those who may, Notification settings (as on a server's right-click), and Hide muted channels, which leaves muted channels out of the list, and a category with nothing left under it, as in Discord.
  • Link previews that play. A YouTube, Twitch, Vimeo, TikTok or SoundCloud link's preview has Play, which plays the video right there, as in Discord; Spotify and Apple Music links show their own players. A preview's author and footer show their icons, and the footer says when the post is from.
  • A category's menu, and New unreads. Right-click a category for Discord's Mark as read, Collapse category, Collapse all categories, Mute category, its Notification settings, Edit category (its name and permissions) and Delete category. When unread channels are scrolled out of sight, a New unreads bar (or how many new mentions) shows at the list's top or bottom and goes to them.
  • Group DMs, as Discord's. Create DM picks up to nine friends (one opens your DM with them, more make a group), as Discord's Select friends does. A group DM has its member list beside it (Show member list), with the owner's crown; anyone in it can Edit group (its name and icon) from its right-click, and the owner can Remove from group or Make group owner on someone's right-click. People joining or leaving show at once.
  • Bookmarks and reminders. A message's menu has Bookmark message and Remind me (in 30 minutes, 1 hour or 4 hours, the next 9:00 in the morning, or Monday at 9:00), as in Discord, and the Inbox has Bookmarks and Reminders tabs, each with Jump and Remove bookmark or Mark complete. They are Discord's own, so they match Discord on your other devices, and when a reminder comes due Windows says so.
  • A post's pictures together. A link whose preview carries several pictures, such as a post on X with four, shows them in one preview in Discord's grid (two side by side, one beside two, or two by two) instead of a preview per picture, and the viewer goes through them with Previous and Next.
  • Everything someone is doing, on their profile. A profile shows each of their activities, not just the first: a song on Spotify with its album, how far in (ticking) and Play on Spotify, a stream with Watch, and the voice channel they're in on a server you share, with Join voice.
  • Speak message. A message's menu has Discord's Speak message, which reads it aloud as "Name said …", mentions, emoji and links read as words; pressed again while it speaks, it stops.
  • Role icons and In voice. In a server with role icons, a name in chat and in the member list shows its highest role's icon (picture or emoji), as in Discord, and someone in a voice channel shows In voice in place of what they're doing.
  • A server's Privacy settings. The server menu has Discord's Privacy settings: direct messages and message requests from its members, and sharing your activity status and activity joining there.
  • Thanks to snekxs. Under your mouse, the Devices room says Built on OpenMouse by snekxs, with a heart: Exo Mouse is built on OpenMouse, which snekxs made, and is in Exo with their permission.
  • The readout under your mouse reaches across. In a narrow window, the hairline over the Devices room's battery, signal and polling figures stopped short above an empty column; it runs the whole width now.

3.14.1 — October 23, 2026

  • Exo Shaders on SCSKiller's newest engine. Exo Shaders now runs SCSKiller 1.4.0-alpha.3, which carries every fix from its stable 1.3.2 too, with Exo's own changes kept. Exo sets its new options at their best by itself: games on a hard disk are read as they compile (only those drives), a game never compiled compiles while your PC is idle, and no game has a compile size limit.
  • Built on stable releases. Exo now runs on the stable .NET 10, Windows App SDK 2.5 and WebView2 releases instead of previews, so their preview bugs never reach your PC.
  • Exo starts lighter and sooner. Store, Discord's page, Music and System now load the first time you open them instead of at every start, and Music's Spotify starts with Music (or is picked up if it's already running). On this PC Exo starts with 923 MB in use instead of 1.4 GB and 6 processes instead of 13, the Library is ready in 1.6 s instead of 2.1 s, and the opening splash is shorter to match. A room opened the first time shows once it's whole, its pictures included, with its tab pulsing meanwhile (a fraction of a second; Music about two while Spotify starts), and at once after that. Discord stays connected the whole time, for calls, messages and the Bar's count.
  • Lighter between calls. Noise suppression's model loaded when Discord signed in and stayed until Exo closed, about 80 MB with no call. It now loads as you join a call (half a second, inside the time the call takes to connect) and is freed a minute after the call ends.
  • Select games to act on together. Select, beside the Library's sort menu, turns a press on a cover into a pick: Shift picks every game from the last one you pressed, Ctrl+A picks them all, and the keyboard's arrows, Enter and Space work as well. A glass bar at the foot of the Library counts them and hides them (Unhide in Hidden games), adds them to a folder or a new one, or picks all or none; Done or Escape ends it.
  • Uninstall several games at once. When every picked game is installed and none is running, the bar has Uninstall (Remove for games added from a folder): a second press confirms, and Exo removes them one after another, as each game's own Uninstall does.
  • A folder with your picture matches the others. Its name sat on a flat box in the folder's color over the picture; now it has the same glass front as a folder of covers, as tall, with the same edge and type, taking its color from the picture under it, and its name reads in white (Light's ink on Light) whatever the folder's color.
  • Your stream's sound is your games and music, never your voice. Sharing a screen sent everything but Exo, so it left out what Exo itself started too: the Music room's Spotify or YouTube Music, and games launched from Exo. And an app playing your mic back to you (Voicemeeter, Sonar, Wave Link, Windows' Listen to this device) went into the stream, so friends heard you twice. Now every app that plays sound is taken on its own and mixed: games, music and browsers are in; the call, Discord, mixers and voice changers are out.
  • Exo viewers keep a stream in H.265. Exo said it plays H.265 before Discord had signed in, so the server never heard it: each time someone on Exo started watching, the stream dropped to H.264 and started over, which is when it sat loading or didn't start. Exo now says so whenever it loads.
  • A stream that won't open tries again. Discord at times ends a watch the moment it starts, and Try Again then worked; Exo now asks once more by itself a second later.
  • Glass stays glass when a page slides in. Switching servers in Discord, Settings sections, server and channel settings, or Music's pages turned every glass name and button on the page into a flat dark plate that only became glass again as the slide ended, the glass you saw fade in. Glass now stays glass the whole way.
  • A stream's controls fade cleanly. When a watched stream's controls hid or came back, their glass drew clear for the fade and its blur popped in after; each control now fades on its own.
  • Move folders by dragging. Drag a folder in All games onto another and it moves before or after it, with a bar in the gap showing where; a folder's menu has Move earlier and Move later.
  • H.265 streams show whole. A friend's H.265 stream was drawn cropped to its top-left corner, big or small: Edge's decoder called every picture 1280×720. Exo now reads each stream's real size from the stream itself, so a 1080p or 1440p stream fills its frame.
  • Watch several streams at once. As in Discord, Watch Stream on another person keeps the first: the newest is big above the call and the others are tiles among the people, each with its own sound. Click one to make it big; Stop Watching on each.
  • Your stream's tile fits when it's small. In a full call or under a watched stream, its buttons sat over LIVE and its numbers were cut off: small, it shows LIVE and its name, the buttons when you point at it, and the numbers in the Share menu. A long name on any small tile ends in "…".

3.14.0 — October 23, 2026

  • Friends can watch your Go Live, smoothly. Viewers on Discord's app saw your stream load forever: Discord's server threw every frame away, because Exo labeled its video packets differently from Discord's own. They match now, and the stream plays smoothly: it starts at 8 Mbit/s and follows what Discord's server says it can take (never more than its limit, down at once when packets go missing, slowly back up), packets go out evenly instead of in bursts, a lost one is sent again once, and a whole picture goes out when someone starts watching or loses one, not every five seconds. Your own preview plays live while Exo is on screen, even with a game in front on another screen.
  • Go Live in H.265. When everyone watching can play it, as Discord's app decides, your stream goes out in H.265, sharper at the same bit rate, and changes to H.264 by itself if someone joins who can't. Friends' H.265 streams and cameras play in Exo too where your graphics card decodes it.
  • The call's controls are glass. The call's bar, a stream's buttons and name, the names on people's tiles, New messages, an incoming call, unsaved changes and Discord's notices are liquid glass like the rest of Exo, and a stream's volume slider reads in Light.
  • Hide Members, as Discord draws it. Watching a stream, Hide Members is a down arrow and Show Members an up arrow, and with members hidden the stream ends above the call's bar instead of under it.
  • Lighter in a call with clips on. With instant replay or a recording running during a call, Exo cleaned your microphone twice, once for Discord and once for the recording; the recording now takes the call's cleaned voice. In a call with League running, Exo went from 70% of a CPU core to 32%.
  • Streams cost less to watch. Video in calls and streams is drawn at the size it shows instead of the stream's own (a 1440p stream shown 1500 wide drew almost three times the pixels), and reaches Exo's page as raw bytes instead of text.
  • Quicker in small ways. The Library is usable 0.8 s sooner at startup, the music volume reads at once instead of waiting on retries, System reads your graphics driver once instead of twice, and a store rewriting its files unchanged no longer makes the Library look again.
  • A saved clip says so, out of the way. Saving a clip or a recording shows one small line at the top middle of your screen for a moment (Clip saved · 30 s) instead of a card in the corner of your game, where a shooter's minimap or kill feed is.
  • Starting… stays solid. While a game starts, its Play button in Continue keeps its look instead of turning see-through over the cover.
  • No stray windows from Riot Client. Bringing a store's window back for a sign-in or a launch also showed windows the store keeps hidden: a blank "TrayIconClass" window and a "Default IME (Not Responding)" one. Exo brings back only what it hid and the store's main window.
  • What's new, when Exo updates. The first time Exo opens after an update, a sheet says what's new in that version, each change in a line on what it does for you, then Continue (Escape or a press outside closes it too). It shows once per version, never on a new install, and never over setup, a game's page or another dialog. Settings › About › What's new opens it again.
  • Your voice gets through, clear and on time, without your fan or desk. Noise suppression ran at full strength, which took words out with the noise: your voice sounded robotic, and the gate, judging what was left, held whole words shut. It now takes noise down by about 40 dB (a fan nearly gone) and keeps your voice, and Automatic sensitivity opens on your voice and stays shut on what suppression strips (a fan, a key). A low cut at 80 Hz takes out your desk's rumble before anything hears them, and a knock or slam on the desk no longer opens Automatic sensitivity (a voice already going out isn't cut by one) (the high-pass Exo's tips mentioned wasn't actually there). Noise Suppression for Others steps aside while any game runs, even one started outside Exo, so it never starves your microphone, and if cleaning someone's voice fails, their voice keeps playing instead of going silent for the rest of the call.
  • Noise Suppression like Discord's. Voice & Video › Noise Suppression is one choice, as Discord's: Exo AI (the default: Exo's DeepFilterNet run the way Krisp runs its own model, your voice brought to the level the model knows and back again, so a quiet or a hot mic is cleaned as well as any), Standard (Google's WebRTC, Discord's Standard) or None.
  • Your voice starts and ends as on Discord, at its own level. Automatic sensitivity keeps the 100 ms before a word and holds 500 ms after it, as Discord's does, and the last moment fades out instead of ending on a click. Automatic Gain Control is gone: your voice goes out at its own level, as TeamSpeak sends it, and if your mic sends nothing for 15 s in a call the voice panel says so. Recordings get your voice whole. Exo's call audio gets Windows' real-time audio scheduling, so a game using all of your CPU can't make your voice or theirs stutter.
  • Recordings without your fan. Before your first word and between sentences a recording carried your fan (and turned it up); now pauses sit 30 dB down behind a gentle gate that looks 100 ms ahead, so every word is whole from its first sound. Captures also open at half volume in the player.
  • Everyone at one volume. Voice & Video › Even Out Voices (on by default) brings each person in a call to about the same loudness, so one friend's hot mic isn't louder than the rest; your volume for each person still applies on top.
  • Only My Voice. Exo can learn your voice from a Voice Check (Use as My Voice) and, with Only My Voice on, hold back voices that aren't yours in calls: someone else in the room, a TV or a stream on your speakers. It listens for about a second before it decides, never holds back on one uncertain moment, and lets your voice straight back out. The voiceprint stays on your PC, encrypted, and Forget deletes it.
  • DLSS settings checked in the game. While a game runs, Exo asks the driver whether its DLSS override actually took (the preset, the mode, frame generation) and notes it in its log.
  • Discord's checkout page never gets Exo's access. A copy of the page that holds Stripe's card field was also served from Exo's own address; messages from it are refused now, as Exo Mouse's are (from a security audit).
  • Discord stays fast after you switch windows. Every time Exo left the front it throttled and trimmed itself at once, and Discord then took 4 to 10 seconds to load messages and profiles (and seconds before your voice went out in a call). Exo now waits a minute out of focus before stepping back (10 seconds when minimized), and a quietly dead connection to Discord is noticed within seconds instead of holding everything for 30.
  • Every channel you can see opens. A channel without Read Message History (rules, welcome) was grayed out and couldn't be pressed; it opens now, as in Discord, showing what arrives while it's open.
  • Chats open at the newest message. A conversation you had read could open a little above the bottom and stay there.
  • Profiles open at once, smoothly. A profile card settles in on a spring with what is known, and grows to its full size when the rest arrives instead of jumping; hovering someone reads their profile ahead.
  • Voice channels show a speaker, as in Discord, instead of a headset.
  • System, as you had it. Display is back to its full set of settings; Exo's profile says Applied on its line, beside its sync and Undo; Shaders has Refresh again.
  • Games move to the middle of the screen. Opening a game from the Library, its cover flies to the middle and its place in the grid stays empty until it flies back on close; a Continue banner opens out into the whole page and shrinks back into its place.
  • Gifts in your Library. Games someone gave you on Steam or Epic show in the Library under Continue, each with its art, who sent it and their message: Accept adds the game to your library, and it shows up there a moment later; Decline asks once, since the sender gets a refund. Exo reads each store's own gifts page in the background. Steam's website needs one sign-in, with Sign in on Steam's row in Settings › Game stores or in setup (Exo never holds the Steam app's sign-in); Epic needs nothing more.
  • Folders look like folders. A Library folder is exactly a cover's size, its name and how many games it holds on its glass front, without the tab that stuck out behind it. It opens out of its place, which stays empty, and when it closes back in its front swings shut and the covers drop back in.
  • Sort a folder your way. An open folder has its own sort menu beside its name: Folder order (the order you put games in, as before), or Name, Installed first, Last launched, Time played, Size and Store, as the Library has. Each folder keeps its choice, and the covers on its card follow it.
  • A game in more than one folder. A game's menu lists every folder under Folders, with a check on each one it's in; pick one to put it in or take it out, and its other folders keep it. Editing a folder can tick games that are in other folders too (each says which). A game in a folder still shows only inside its folders, not loose in the grid.
  • Your own picture on a folder. Edit folder › Add picture (or drop a picture on the sheet) takes a PNG, JPEG or WebP under 15 MB and opens it in a cropper the folder's exact shape, with the folder's glass and name where they'll sit: drag to move it, scroll or use the slider to zoom, or use the arrow keys and + and −. The folder's card then shows your picture with its name and count on a shorter glass front; Remove picture goes back to the games' covers. Exo checks the picture itself, writes it again as its own JPEG in its cover cache, and deletes it with its folder or when you replace or remove it.
  • Hide games. Right-click a game and choose Hide to keep it out of the Library: the grid, Continue, Friends are playing and its folders. The Library's sort menu has a Show section, All games or Hidden games, which lists only the games you hid (the heading says Hidden games), each with Unhide. Ctrl+K still finds a hidden game, so you can open and play it.
  • A press outside closes it, the first time. Every sheet, dialog, popover and menu (a game's page, the Store's, Settings, Properties, a folder, What's New, Ctrl+K, Discord's settings, dialogs, profiles and menus) closes on the first press anywhere outside it, the dimmed space, the edges and the Bar over it included, and that press doesn't also open what was under it. With a menu open over a page, one press closes the menu and the next the page; a press inside never closes it, nor does selecting text that ends outside. Some Discord menus needed two or three presses and a few never closed; they close now.
  • Exo's profile on Radeon. System › Driver and profile now tunes an AMD Radeon in one press, as it does a GeForce, through AMD's own driver settings: Radeon Chill, Boost, Super Resolution and Enhanced Sync off, FreeSync on for every screen that has it, and with FreeSync the frame rate target just under your refresh rate (the same cap as Exo's G-SYNC profile, and it follows a new refresh rate). Every value it replaces is saved first, so Undo puts back exactly what you had; a driver update that moves a setting shows with Re-apply, and Exo applies it again by itself. Fluid Motion Frames stays yours. If AMD's driver doesn't answer, the page lists what to set in AMD Software, as before.
  • Anti-Lag while you play. On a Radeon, Exo's profile turns Anti-Lag on just before a game Exo launches starts, and off once no game runs: left on everywhere, AMD's driver also runs it in Windows' own apps and costs CPU. Always and Off are beside Use.
  • FSR 4 for FSR 3.1 games. On a Radeon RX 9000 or 7000, and an RX 6000 from AMD Software 26.10.1, Exo's profile turns on the driver's FSR upgrade, so games with FSR 3.1 get FSR 4's sharper image with no change to the game.
  • Fluid Motion Frames per game. On a Radeon, a game's page has Fluid Motion Frames: on, Exo turns AMD's frame generation on just before that game starts and off once no game runs (AMD's switch is for every game at once). It needs AMD Software running, and the page says so when it isn't. If Exo closes mid-game, it switches it back the next time it starts.
  • Your Radeon, right now. Under Exo's profile, a line shows the GPU's temperature and hotspot, power, clock, load, VRAM in use and fan speed, every three seconds.
  • Sharper, steadier Radeon. Exo's Radeon profile also turns Radeon Image Sharpening off (it sharpened every game on top of the game's own) and leaves V-Sync to each game (Wait for Vertical Refresh: Off, unless application specifies), so Fluid Motion Frames keeps working. On a Radeon, System › This PC asks AMD's driver whether Smart Access Memory is on before it says Resizable BAR is off.
  • Exo's profile on Intel. System › Driver and profile now tunes an Intel Arc or Iris Xe in one press, through Intel's own driver settings: Endurance Gaming and the sharpening filter off, Low latency on (On with boost and Off are beside Use), Adaptive Sync on and, on a screen that can run it, frames capped just under your refresh rate (the same cap as Exo's G-SYNC profile, and it follows a new refresh rate), and Intel's prebuilt shaders downloaded, so games don't compile them while you play. Every value it replaces is saved first, so Undo puts back exactly what you had; a setting your driver doesn't take is left out instead of stopping the rest, and Exo applies the profile again by itself after a driver update moves it. If Intel's driver doesn't answer, the page lists what to set in Intel Graphics Software.
  • XeSS frame generation per game. On an Intel GPU, a game's page sets XeSS frame generation for that game: the game's own choice, or 2x, 3x or 4x. Exo sets it in Intel's driver just before the game starts and puts back what you had once no game runs, even if Exo closed mid-game.
  • One-click driver updates on Radeon and Intel. System › Driver and profile shows your AMD or Intel graphics driver beside the newest one for your card, as it does on a GeForce, read from AMD's and Intel's own download pages (graphics built into your processor get the processor's driver). Update asks first, downloads from AMD or Intel, checks their signature (and the checksum Intel lists), waits while a game runs or you're in a Discord call, then installs: on a Radeon, AMD Software: Adrenalin Edition with its driver, since Fluid Motion Frames needs AMD Software; on Intel, the driver alone, without Intel Graphics Software, the Driver & Support Assistant or Intel's data collection. Exo never restarts your PC; when the new driver needs a restart, the row says so. On a Radeon, Exo's profile is put back afterwards.
  • NVIDIA driver updates wait for your call. Installing a driver ends a Discord call, so Update now waits for the call to end, as it waits for a game. Exo's profile is put back after the install again: it was skipped, read while the driver still counted as installing.
  • Interrupts in one press (MSI mode). System › This PC › Interrupts says whether every device that can use MSI mode already does. Optimize interrupts switches a graphics card, network adapter, audio controller or USB controller still on a shared interrupt line to MSI mode, so it signals the processor on its own instead of waiting behind a busy neighbor. Windows asks permission once, and the change takes effect when you restart; Exo never restarts for you. Storage, everything between the processor and the drive Windows starts from, bridges, Sound Blaster cards and any device without a signed, running driver are left alone, and interrupt priority and affinity stay untouched (no measured gain). Each value is saved first: Undo all, or Revert on one device, puts it back. After the restart it says what switched and what Windows or a driver update put back, and Devices lists each one, its interrupt now and what Exo does to it.
  • Windows' precompiled shaders show in System. Under Shaders, one line says whether this PC gets games' shaders precompiled from Windows (Advanced Shader Delivery) and how many games came with theirs; those are left out of Compile. Exo Shaders with this check is now the one Exo runs.
  • System, tighter. Game Ready or Studio sits beside the driver's Update, and G-SYNC or not beside Exo's profile. Display has one row each for refresh and color, scaling, color (its sliders in a popover beside Adjust) and RTX Video, instead of a row per setting behind Show.
  • This PC, checked. System has a This PC part that shows only what holds your PC back, in plain words: RAM running below its rated speed (turn on EXPO/XMP in the BIOS), Resizable BAR off, an AMD or Intel chipset driver behind its maker's newest, and Game Mode off. A power plan that holds your processor back says why: Power saver, a capped processor, boost off, Best power efficiency, or on a dual-CCD X3D or a Ryzen 9 9900X or 9950X a plan that keeps every core awake, which stops AMD keeping a game on one CCD (the V-Cache one on X3D). When all is well it says All good.
  • Exo's power plan, tuned for your processor. Exo makes one Windows plan named Exo, a copy of Balanced (so AMD's and Intel's own tuning of Balanced carries over), with settings for your kind of processor: single- and dual-CCD Ryzen and Intel up to 11th gen get full clocks between frames, energy preference 0, aggressive boost, no core parking and no power saving on PCIe, USB, NVMe and SATA, with idle states left on so boost isn't hurt; a dual-CCD X3D, and a Ryzen 9 9900X or 9950X, whose second CCD AMD's driver also parks in games, keep Balanced's parking and minimum state so it can; Intel's 12th gen and newer keep their P-cores awake at energy preference 0, with the E-cores and Windows' Thread Director policies as Balanced and Intel have them. Idle states stay on everywhere: the gaming plans that turn them off placed last in a 39-plan Fortnite test. A laptop gets it plugged in only, and keeps Balanced on battery. On Windows' own plans Exo switches to it by itself; on your own or a third party's plan, This PC offers Use Exo's plan for your processor. Undo puts your plan back and Exo won't switch again; the settings it sets show in Control Panel's power options, and uninstalling Exo puts your plan back and deletes Exo's.
  • AMD's chipset driver, kept up to date. On a Ryzen, when AMD puts out a newer chipset driver, Exo installs it by itself while you're away: after ten idle minutes, with no game running, no Discord call and the PC plugged in, since the install can drop USB and sound for a moment. It downloads AMD's own installer, checks AMD's signature, its version and its size, and runs it silently. If Windows needs a restart, This PC says Restart to finish the AMD chipset update; Exo never restarts your PC. Where Windows would ask for permission, it waits for you to press Update. If an install fails, the row says why and Update opens AMD's page. Settings › Downloads › Keep the AMD chipset driver up to date turns it off.
  • Intel's chipset driver, kept up to date. On an Intel processor with Intel's Chipset Device Software installed, Exo keeps it on Intel's newest the same way: while you're away (ten idle minutes, no game, no Discord call, the PC plugged in), it downloads Intel's own installer from Intel's download page, checks Intel's signature, its version and the SHA-256 Intel lists, and runs it silently with no restart. System › This PC shows the Intel chipset driver, what's installed and what's next, with Update to install it now; Settings › Downloads › Keep the Intel chipset driver up to date turns it off. Where Intel's package isn't installed (Windows Update or your PC's maker brought the chipset's files), Exo leaves it so, and it never installs Intel Management Engine, which Intel says comes from your PC's maker.
  • A speaker on servers with a call going. A server in Discord's rail shows a small speaker while people are in its voice channels you can see.
  • Discord's settings open at once. They stay ready with the room instead of being built when you open them.
  • Lists fade into their edges. Discord's channel, message and member lists, its server rail and Music's library fade out where they scroll on under a panel's edge, instead of being cut off by its corner.
  • Every edge the same. Text fields (Discord's message box and searches) have the same thin edge as panels; theirs was brighter. The mouse in the top bar shows its profile and battery without an icon.
  • A long settings page stays in its sheet. Changing to a long page in Discord's settings no longer draws past the sheet's bottom edge for a moment.
  • Glass or Solid. Settings › General › Material is now two choices. Glass is Liquid Glass even with Windows' transparency effects off; Solid has nothing showing through. Tinted is gone (a saved Tinted opens as Glass). Solid used to leave Settings and the menus opened in it see-through; they are solid now.
  • No more glass flicker. On a game page, in Settings, in Discord and in lists, glass buttons and panels showed a flat tint for a moment and then popped into glass as a page, room or sheet came in, because the whole thing faded in and glass can't see through a fading parent. Nothing that holds glass fades in whole anymore: rows rise in, sheets settle by size, tabs are there at once.
  • The dim covers the whole window. Behind Settings, a game's sheet, a folder or a Discord dialog, the top bar now dims with everything else instead of staying bright.
  • Glass that looks like glass. Every glass surface in Exo, Exo Mouse included, is now clear liquid glass: the colors behind it show through softly, its edges catch the light (a bright top line, a fainter bottom one, a soft glow inside), and there is no gray tint or wash across it that read as metal. Words on it keep a soft shadow and at least 4.5:1 over the brightest art, in Void, Dark and Light.
  • Exo opens in your Appearance. If the page's own copy of your Appearance was out of date, Exo could open in Light for a moment before switching to Void (a white flash). Exo now hands the page your saved Appearance before it draws.
  • System is one page. Driver and profile, HDR, Display, Upscalers and Shaders fit on one screen without tabs. Display shows Best for this screen, with each setting by hand behind Show. Shaders lists only the games that still need compiling, and finds new games by itself (no Scan games button).
  • Every Discord member is there. A big server's member list is its full length from the start, with placeholder rows, and loads the members wherever you scroll (it used to grow only 100 at a time from the top). A late update for the list you just left no longer replaces the new one with a single row.
  • Discord's bottom bar stays put. Switching servers no longer slides your profile bar away and back, and the channel list ends above it instead of scrolling under it (its edges no longer doubled up, and profile effects no longer showed past it).
  • Light, Dark and Void. Settings › General › Appearance: Match Windows (Light or Dark, as your Windows apps are), Light (soft gray and white), Dark (charcoal) or Void (pure black, for OLED screens). Every room, menu, sheet and dialog follows it, Discord and the Devices room too, and Exo opens in it from the first frame.
  • Words you can read on every background. Every word in Exo was measured against what is really behind it, art and glass included, in Light, Dark and Void, and brought to at least 4.5:1: a game's sheet and a Store page sit on thicker glass, badges over cover art dim the art more, a Store banner fades under its side column, and a game without a cover shows its name brighter.
  • The whole picture on big games. Continue's cards and the Store's banners show their whole art, with the name and buttons on a small glass plate, instead of fading the art away under them.
  • Ctrl+K brings back your last search, selected. Typing replaces it, as in Spotlight; it used to be added to the end.
  • Escape closes a game's sheet however soon you press it. A sheet opened and closed quickly could stay open.
  • Discord and Music open smoothly the first time. Exo already loaded every room behind the opening animation, but kept them folded away, so the first visit to Discord still had to lay out its whole chat (a 109 ms hitch; later visits 30 ms). Now each room is laid out out of sight while Exo opens, and the first visit is as quick as any other.
  • Light looks finished. Buttons and panels keep their edge on Light's page (they had almost none), Discord's home button shows (it was white on white), styled display names in Discord read on the light page, a glass button keeps its frost under the pointer instead of going clear, and a game's stats lose a dark smudge.
  • Devices matches the rest of Exo. Exo Mouse draws its panels and controls in Exo's own colors in Void and Dark, and sits in the same frame as every other room, so switching rooms no longer moves everything a few pixels.
  • More mice in Devices. Exo Mouse now sets up 40 more SteelSeries mice: the Rival 100, 105, 106, 110, 300, 300S, 5 and 600, the Rival 3 Gen 2 and Rival 3 Wireless Gen 2, the Aerox 3 Wireless Gen 2, the Prime, Prime Wireless and Prime Mini, the Sensei 310, Sensei [RAW] and Sensei Pro / Rival Pro, and their special editions. Endgame Gear's OT mice start their session as Endgame's own app does, a Logitech mouse with an older onboard profile format saves its buttons instead of refusing over lift-off, and a mouse whose connection died while the PC slept is opened again by itself. A Lamzu that stores X and Y per DPI stage shows both, each set without moving the other, with Separate X and Y beside them.
  • Tidier rooms. Music's home tiles fill their row, its covers line up with their titles, and search shows the top result beside the first songs. System's panels end level, with Check again on its title line, and Display's two columns come out even. The Store's Free on Epic shelf fills its row and drops two arrows that did nothing, and the banner's button matches Continue's Play. Discord's Account puts your card beside your details, Active Now no longer repeats a game's name and time, Add Friend lines up with the tabs beside it, and a profile opens at once with what Exo already knows.
  • Small things that were off. A menu opened with the mouse no longer lights its first row. A new folder's picked games show their check, and its search shows its magnifier. A game's store is a label, not a button that did nothing. Ctrl+K and the Store say "Nothing matches" the same way, in the middle, with Clear search. Devices › Profiles has real Apply, Games, Rename and Delete buttons (Delete in red) instead of gray words, and the profile on the mouse no longer shows a dead Apply. A playlist, album or artist page has a title as large as a game's, and every title plate over art has the same corners.
  • The same feel everywhere. Discord's buttons give way when pressed like every other button, a few transitions that ran on their own timings take Exo's, and pressing the tab you're already on no longer slides the page over itself.
  • Captures have your call in them. With Exo's own Discord client, Exo Capture looked for the Discord app to record a call's voices, found nothing, and a game's capture had the game and your mic but not your friends. The voice track now follows Exo's own client when the Discord app isn't running.
  • Downloads checked for revoked signatures. NVIDIA's driver, Exo's runtimes and upscaler files were checked for a valid signature from the right publisher, but not whether that publisher had since revoked the certificate (as after a leaked key). Now they are; with no connection to check, the signature alone decides, as before.
  • Menus and sheets read over bright art. Large glass (menus, Ctrl+K, popovers, sheets) now dims what is behind it as small glass does, so its gray words keep 4.5:1 even over white art (they fell to 2.6:1 on Dark).
  • YouTube Music's ad blocker keeps working. It is now uBlock Origin Lite. uBlock Origin is an older kind of extension that Edge's engine, which Exo's music players run on, is retiring this year, so it would have stopped blocking.
  • Continue's titles stay dark glass. Over bright art (VALORANT's) a title and its More turned white; they now stay the same dark glass on every card, as do a sheet's close buttons.
  • Changing Appearance fades. Switching between Light, Dark and Void (or Windows switching for Match Windows) crossfades the whole window over a third of a second instead of snapping.
  • Rooms come in in one color. As a room or page changed, its panels drew the page's own color and turned into glass only when the move ended, a visible second step on every room. They are glass the whole way now.
  • Dark stays Dark behind Settings and sheets. The dimming behind Settings, a game's sheet and Discord's settings took Dark's charcoal page down to Void's black; on Dark it now dims half as much, as on Light.
  • Glass never turns white. The Bar and Music's player no longer turn into white glass over bright art; they stay the same glass everywhere. Labels over videos and pictures (a call's names, Watch, a GIF's star) keep white words in Light too.
  • Smoother room changes. Changing rooms worked out the style of the whole page twice, as the move began and as it ended (15 to 30 ms each, a dropped frame or two on a fast screen). Now only what moves is touched.
  • New glass. Exo's glass is now Glassy UI's: edges lit from the top left with a softer light opposite, a sheen at the top, frosted menus and sheets, and a soft two-layer shadow.
  • Folders are glass. The front of a Library folder is Liquid Glass tinted in its color, bending the covers behind it, drawn at your screen's own resolution while it tips open.
  • Exo Shaders skips games Windows already compiled. On a PC that gets precompiled shaders from Windows (Advanced Shader Delivery: Windows 11 24H2, Gaming Services, and on NVIDIA an RTX card with driver 621.01 or newer), a game installed with its shaders already built for your card shows Precompiled and isn't compiled again. Exo Shaders' Cache page says whether your PC takes them, or the one thing it still needs.
  • Threads look like Discord's. The thread mark in a channel's header, a message's thread, Create Thread and the Inbox is now Discord's own (from its web client), not a reply arrow.
  • Server invites show as Discord's invite card. A discord.gg or discord.com/invite link in a message gets Discord's card under it: the server's icon, name and verified badge, how many are online and in it, and Join, or Joined when you're already in, which opens it. An expired or unknown invite says Invalid Invite, and a message that is only invite links shows just the cards.
  • Discord settings open at once. Each section read its data again every time you opened it, so Account, Voice & Video, Data & Privacy and the others showed Loading or empty controls first. A section you've seen now opens with what it showed and refreshes quietly behind it.
  • The selection glides without flicker. In Settings, Discord's settings and every tab group, the hover light could sit under the sliding selection as it passed, the row you clicked lost its light before the selection reached it, and the chosen row could show two fills for a moment as a sheet opened. Now no other row lights while the selection moves, the row you clicked keeps its light until the selection arrives, and the chosen row only ever has the one fill.
  • Settings starts with its sections. The small gray "Settings" label over General is gone, so the list starts level with the page's first title, as Discord's settings and Apple's System Settings do.

3.13.4 — October 21, 2026

This update fixes Exo on 4K and 1440p screens.

  • Exo fills big screens again. On a 4K screen at 150% (or a 1440p one), 3.13.3 drew the page larger as intended but showed only its top-left three quarters, cut off at the right and bottom with black around it. The whole page now fills the window.
  • New NVIDIA drivers show up the same day. Exo looks for a newer NVIDIA driver at most once an hour instead of once a day, so a driver NVIDIA puts out today is offered today.
  • Discord invite links join in Exo. Clicking a discord.gg or discord.com/invite link in a message joins the server right in the Discord room and opens the channel it invites to, as Discord's app does, instead of opening your browser. If you're already in, it just opens it; an invite Discord refuses opens Join a Server with the reason.

3.13.3 — October 21, 2026

This update makes voice calls work the same on every PC: noise suppression that never drops a word, any microphone picked up properly, and nothing cut when the PC is busy.

  • Noise suppression that keeps up on every PC. Exo now runs DeepFilterNet directly, built by Exo from the project's own source: every 20 ms of your voice is cleaned right away, in about a millisecond. The plugin Exo used handed each frame to a thread of its own that woke up on a timer. On a busy PC, or one where Windows slows timers, it fell behind and filled the gaps with silence, so some people went through silent, choppy or garbled. Measured on a real voice over a fan, 94 to 100% of the words now go out, none cut mid-word, with the voice's clarity kept. Each voice cleaned, yours and each one you hear, has its own copy of the model.
  • The gate hears what noise suppression hears. DeepFilterNet judges how much of each moment is voice, and that judgment now opens the automatic gate, as Discord's does with Krisp. The quiet start and end of a word go out too.
  • Your first words are cleaned too. The model loads when Discord starts in Exo, not when a call begins, so the first two seconds of a call no longer go out uncleaned.
  • Windows' noise suppression stands in. If Exo's isn't ready yet, Windows' own cleans your voice instead of nothing.
  • Your default microphone, as Discord picks it. Default means the microphone and speakers chosen in Windows' Sound settings, as in Discord. Exo used Windows' communications device, which on some PCs is a webcam's mic or a monitor's speakers.
  • Bluetooth and USB mics with a deep buffer go through. Exo drops sound only when it's 40 ms later than that microphone usually is. Every packet from a mic that always runs over 40 ms behind used to be dropped, so nothing went out.
  • A microphone that comes back keeps the call. When a mic or headset goes away mid-call, Exo keeps looking for one for as long as the call lasts. It used to stop after 10 seconds.
  • NVIDIA Broadcast and AMD mics are cleaned too. Exo stepped its noise suppression aside for any mic named like NVIDIA Broadcast's or AMD's, even with that app's own cleaning off, and then nothing cleaned the voice.
  • Spotify's own window stays out of sight. Spotify's window could open on your screen while Exo started it: Exo asked it a second time to show when it was slow, and Spotify answered by raising its window. A start that handed off to a Spotify already running wasn't hidden either. Now every window Spotify opens is hidden until Exo holds it, and Spotify stays hidden if hosting it fails, until it can be hosted.
  • Display offers only what your screen takes. Refresh rates and color depths in System > Display now come from what the screen itself says it takes, not only from Windows' and the graphics driver's lists: a 60 Hz screen no longer shows 144 Hz, and an 8-bit screen no longer shows 12-bit. On HDMI, a color that doesn't fit the cable's bandwidth at the current mode isn't offered either.
  • SDR looks right in HDR, every time. When HDR comes on, the screen can take a few seconds to settle, and Exo's gamma 2.2 for the desktop could miss it and never come on, leaving everything washed out. Exo now tries again for about 30 seconds, and if Windows still refuses, app.log says why. An SDR screen with Auto color management is no longer taken for an HDR one on Windows 10 and 11 before 24H2.
  • HDR goes back off after a crash. If Exo closed while it had HDR on for a game (a crash, an update, the PC shutting down), HDR stayed on, washed out, until you turned it off in Windows. Exo now remembers, and turns it off once no HDR game runs.
  • Steam stays out of sight when Exo didn't start it. Exo hid the windows of a store app only when Exo had started it, so a Steam that Windows started at sign-in could still pop up its web helper, update and sale windows. Now Steam, Epic, GOG, the EA app and Ubisoft Connect running in the background are kept hidden and out of the tray the same way, within 30 seconds. One you open yourself stays open.
  • Turn off every store notification and sound at once. Settings > Game stores > Notifications and sounds turns off Steam's and Epic's own pop-ups, toasts and sounds with one press, for every Steam account on this PC: toasts and their sound, achievement and controller toasts, friends coming online, messages and invites, taskbar flashing, the screenshot sound and new-release notices, and Epic's notifications and What's New page. Exo keeps them off. Steam closes and starts again hidden to take it, unless a game is running or Steam is downloading.
  • Folders in your Library. Library > + > New folder: name it, pick its color and tick its games from your whole Library (search finds one fast). Create folds the sheet into the folder's card at the front of All games: a real folder in its color, the size of a cover, holding its first three covers like papers. Hover it and it opens a little, the front tipping toward you as the covers rise and fan out. It opens out of that card into a panel of its games and folds back. Right-click a game to add it to a folder, start a new one with it, or take it out; right-click a folder or press its pencil to edit it, or delete it (its games go back to the grid). A folder is also how you keep favorites.
  • Add any game. Library > + > Add a game picks the game's own .exe, wherever it is, and Exo plays it from there: the title comes from the game itself, and an engine's build folder (Unreal's Binaries\Win64) isn't taken for the game's. Add a game folder still finds the .exe for you.
  • Uninstall says what it does. A store game's page says Uninstall instead of Remove. A game added from a folder still says Remove, since Exo leaves its files where they are.
  • The same layout on every screen. On a 1440p or 4K screen Exo fit more in, so the Library, the Store and Devices looked different from a 1080p screen. Exo now lays its page out as on a 1080p screen and draws it larger to fill a bigger one, so every screen gets the same layout, only sharper. A 1080p screen, or a smaller one, is unchanged.
  • Go Live joins the way Discord's app does. Your stream's connection now names its channel and says it carries a screen, and it uses the stream channel Discord gives instead of working one out. Viewers could stay on "joining" forever. app.log now records each step of a stream: whether Discord holds it paused, who watches, what viewers ask for, and how its encryption settles.
  • Voices you hear never drop on a busy PC. When cleaning other people's voices falls more than 100 ms behind, the waiting sound plays as it came until it catches up, instead of being cut.

3.13.2 — October 21, 2026

This update makes your voice clear in calls with noise suppression on, lets Go Live start on any PC, shows Epic, Steam and Xbox games as they really are, and lets you arrange your Discord servers.

  • Your voice at a normal volume. Automatic Gain Control now works before noise suppression and brings a quiet microphone up to a normal speaking level within a second or two of talking. A quiet mic could reach a call 25 dB under everyone else's voice, so noise suppression and the gate held most of it back and people couldn't hear you. Gain control used to work only after the gate, rising 1 dB a second. It never turns a voice down, and a loud word never clips. The gain it learns for your mic is where your next call starts.
  • Go Live when your graphics card's encoder won't start. Exo Capture tries your graphics card first, at your settings and then at lower frame rates and 1080p. If the card still won't encode, your stream now goes out on Windows' own H.264 encoder, on the CPU, at up to 720p 30, instead of not at all.
  • Epic games that aren't installed stop showing as installed. A game Epic Games Launcher started installing but never finished, Unreal Engine, Epic's tools, and a game whose folder was left behind after it was removed no longer show up as installed games. Games show their real names instead of Epic's internal code names.
  • Remove works for Epic games. Remove on a game Epic Games Launcher installed opens Epic's own uninstall confirmation. Exo used to say a game was removed when nothing had been removed; now it tells you when no store has the game installed.
  • Remove works with every store. Remove kept Steam's, GOG Galaxy's, Riot Client's, Ubisoft Connect's and Battle.net's windows hidden, so a removal that asked you to confirm waited on a question you couldn't see; the store's window now shows so you can answer it. Xbox games, and any game Windows has no uninstaller for, open their store's app so you can remove them there, instead of stopping with a message. When you finish a removal in a store's app, Exo notices and updates the game's card. A game the Amazon Games app installed is removed through that app; Exo used to say it was removed when nothing had been. Exo finds a game's uninstaller even when Windows lists its name with ™ or ®, and a GOG game from GOG's offline installer is removed with its own uninstaller when GOG Galaxy isn't installed.
  • Epic updates show up. While you're signed in to Epic in Exo, Exo compares each Epic game with the newest version Epic has, so Update shows before Epic Games Launcher starts the update itself. Epic games installed with Heroic show up too.
  • Steam games keep their Update. A game whose update Steam had started, paused or couldn't finish showed as not installed, or not at all; it now shows Update. Update also stays when Steam is closed and Exo can't check which games you own.
  • Xbox games in the folder you chose. Exo finds games the Xbox app installs to a folder you picked, not only in XboxGames.
  • Covers stay covers when you switch Steam accounts. After a switch, a game whose cover wasn't ready yet showed Steam's wide banner in its tile, and kept it for as long as Exo was open, even once the cover had arrived. The cover now replaces the banner as soon as it's ready, and a tile still showing a banner tries for its cover again each time you come back to it.
  • Arrange your Discord servers. Drag a server or folder up or down the server list and it moves there at once, with a white bar where it will land. Drop a server on another to make a folder of both, or on a folder to add it. Exo saves the order to your Discord account the way Discord does, so Discord's own apps show it too, and a change made in Discord's app shows in Exo. A folder with one server stays a folder, as in Discord.
  • The right NVIDIA driver for GTX 10 series cards. NVIDIA's Game Ready drivers no longer support GTX 10 and 900 series cards, older ones, or TITAN V, and Exo offered them a newer hotfix driver that wouldn't install. These cards now get NVIDIA's newest driver for them, its security update driver, and show as up to date once they have it. RTX 20 series and newer cards still get NVIDIA's newest Game Ready or hotfix driver.

3.13.1 — October 21, 2026

This update fixes calls with noise suppression and makes Go Live work on more graphics cards.

  • Noise suppression never silences a call. With Noise Suppression for Others on, you heard no one and your own voice stopped going out after a minute, and stayed off until Exo restarted. Every voice cleaned at once shared one copy of DeepFilterNet's model, and they wrote over each other. Now each voice gets its own copy, so your microphone, each person you hear, and Voice & Video's meter run side by side. Three voices cleaned at once now come out exactly as each does alone.
  • Go Live on more graphics cards. When a graphics card's video encoder refuses a stream's settings, Exo Capture tries again at 60, then 30 fps, then at 1080p, instead of failing. When it can't start at all, Exo now says why (the window, the screen, or your graphics card's encoder) instead of always blaming Windows.
  • Streams play on any graphics card. A stream that a graphics card can't decode now plays through software decoding instead of staying black.
  • A clearer streaming icon. Share Your Screen, LIVE in voice and friends who are streaming now show a screen with an arrow going up.

3.13.0 — October 21, 2026

This update gives Exo a new look: Liquid Glass on everything but the black behind it, white words, Inter as it was drawn, Tabler icons with one for every meaning, a new app icon, and motion that answers at once and settles.

  • Liquid Glass you can always read. The top bar's capsules, menus, popovers, Ctrl+K, notices, Music's player and the buttons over game art are glass again, Apple's regular kind: they take on the color of what passes behind them and bend it at their edge, and keep it in a band that leaves every word clear, even over white art (the glass in 3.10 let text fade there). Like Apple's small glass, the top bar and Music's player turn light with dark words over bright art and dark again over dark art; menus stay dark gray. The Library scrolls under the top bar now and stays sharp, and a press lights the glass where you click. Settings > General > Glass picks Clear, Tinted (frosted, so less shows through) or Solid, and only that, or contrast turned up in Windows, changes it: not a running game, not Exo in the background, not Windows' Transparency effects switch. Still glass costs nothing, and scrolling under it keeps your screen's full frame rate.
  • White, done for AMOLED. Words and accents are white instead of cream: primary buttons, switches, sliders, focus rings and the glass's light. The grays under it are neutral and keep exactly the contrast the warm ones had, and every word on glass reads better for it (6.6:1 at the worst spot on the top bar's glass, 10.4:1 in menus). White buttons dim a shade under the pointer, since white can't get brighter. Exo Mouse matches.
  • Inter as it was drawn. Exo now uses Inter's own release: open 6 and 9, an l with a tail so l, I and 1 never look alike, square periods and colons that match the icons' dots, real italics in Discord, and Cyrillic, Greek and Vietnamese in the same font instead of a fallback mid-line. Titles use Inter's tighter Display cut without the extra squeeze, and small words on glass stay a touch heavier.
  • A new app icon. Exo's mark as Liquid Glass: black glass lit from above with a bright rim, and the three bars as slabs of white glass with soft corners. It is sharp at every size, from the taskbar to the desktop, and setup, the uninstaller, shortcuts and Apps & features all use it. Setup's screens are white instead of cream too.
  • Icons, one family, one for every meaning. Every icon in Exo, Exo Mouse and setup is Tabler's now, on a fine line that matches the words beside it, and no two things that mean different things share one: the Devices room is a mouse and the top bar's mouse capsule shows the profile it is on; Exo Capture's button is a viewfinder, Save replay rewinds and Open captures is a gallery; a server's members and your friends, a voice channel and the volume, Browse Channels and Music's queue, a song you like and a game on your wishlist, a poll and a file upload, Copy ID and Copy each have their own. Ctrl+K shows its games as games, and the Library's game menu has its icons. Play, pause and skip are solid, as players draw them; a toggle that is on (a liked song, a starred capture) and the room you're in fill in; the checks that draw themselves in use the same line. Exo's mark in the top bar and on the opening screen is the app icon's, same lean, same squircle, rim all the way round.
  • No more fades by words. Lists, chats, pages and the Store's shelves end at their edge instead of fading the words or rows near it.
  • Glass on everything but the background. Only the black behind everything is AMOLED now. Sidebars and panels, Settings, Discord's settings, Properties, the game and Store pages, cards, menus and every button, field and chip are Liquid Glass: black over black, never a flat gray, and the color of the art behind it over art. Scrolling stays as smooth as it was.
  • Black stays black. Glass over the black page is black now, drawn by its hairline: Discord's channel and member lists, Music's panels, Devices' cards, the message box and every control had a faint gray from a 3% floor. Over art it still takes the art's color. Tinted keeps its frost. A panel's edge is drawn on its glass, so it shows over black, and Friends are playing's capsules take the Bar's brighter glass rim.
  • Clear is clearer. Clear glass shows more of what is behind it while every word keeps its contrast, and the gray words on a hovered row turn white.
  • Buttons and fields with all four edges. Every button, search and text field, the Discord message box too, has one even edge all around; the old shading made its top line disappear on black.
  • A smooth opening. The opening screen shows the app icon itself, sharp at any size, and settles in smoothly instead of stuttering; setup and first run show the same mark.
  • Discord's bar floats across. Your picture, mute, deafen and settings, with a call in progress above them, float as one glass bar from the server icons to the channel list, your picture in line with the server icons, and the lists scroll under it.
  • Requirements at a glance. A Store page lists the system requirements a row per part, your PC's part beside its name with a check or a cross and Steam's Minimum and Recommended quieter under it (side by side when the page is wide), with one small verdict at the top. DirectX and Network are judged too: the DirectX your graphics card runs, and whether Windows is online.
  • One button for emoji, GIFs and stickers. Discord's message box has a single emoji button; its picker has Emoji, GIFs and Stickers as tabs.
  • Motion that answers and settles. Every move in Exo now follows one set of rules: things that appear ease out, things that travel move on a calm spring that never bounces, and nothing you watch lasts longer than a third of a second. Buttons give way the moment you press and come back smoothly (most used to snap). Menus grow from the button that opened them and fade away when they close, instead of popping in and vanishing; dialogs and sheets settle in from the middle, Properties and a playlist's details too. Switching rooms is quicker and shorter, a shelf lands in under half a second, and the top bar's live capsules drop in without the old overshoot and blur. Game covers in the Library, the Store and Shaders all lift toward the pointer the same way and take a little light from their own brightness instead of a gray film. A liked song's or starred capture's icon fills in from the middle as Apple's do, buttons that appear with a row or card fade the same everywhere, and someone speaking gets the same green ring everywhere. What you do with the keyboard happens at once. The top bar no longer turns the wrong shade for a moment after you switch rooms over bright art, Music's player stays on top while its pages slide under it, Discord's banners leave instead of replaying their entrance, and a jump to a message lights at once and fades slowly. With Reduce motion on, nothing slides or grows, but menus, sheets and rooms still fade in instead of snapping. Exo Mouse moves the same way.
  • Noise suppression keeps up on a busy PC. When a game leaves too little CPU for DeepFilterNet, Exo's Discord runs its lighter first stage alone, about 30% less work, instead of letting your voice fall 10 ms further behind each time it can't keep up, and goes back to the full model once the PC has had room for 20 seconds (longer each time it has to go light again in a call, so a busy PC doesn't swap back and forth). Your mic, live, in Voice & Video says which one runs.
  • Echo cancellation on speakers. When your speakers aren't on your head, Exo's Discord takes the call's own sound out of your mic with WebRTC's echo canceller (AEC3) before noise suppression, so nobody hears themselves back: it finds how far away your speakers are by itself, follows a new device, and costs almost nothing. On headphones or a headset it doesn't run, as there is no echo to take out. Voice & Video has the switch, on by default, and Your mic, live says whether it runs.
  • Noise suppression for others. Turn on Noise Suppression for Others in Voice & Video and Exo's Discord takes the background noise out of everyone else's voice too, for you: each person gets a DeepFilterNet of their own, on a thread of their own, so one noisy friend never slows the rest, and the end of each sentence plays when they stop, not at their next word. A Go Live stream's sound is never touched. Off by default: each person talking costs about what your own mic's noise suppression does.
  • Discord Shop. Checkout shows the item, its price and the card field again (the top of the dialog went blank), the card form is half as tall, and Stripe's Autofill button, which did nothing in Exo, is gone. The Shop shows eight items to a row at 1080p, each on Discord's own colors for it, with search in the header.
  • Close buttons over art are glass. A game's and a Store page's close button were drawn solid by mistake; they're glass now and, like Continue's More, turn light over bright art. With Reduce motion on, glass appears at once instead of fading in.
