  • Dialogs and switches. Dialogs no longer show a white ring around their edge when they open, and the knob of a switch that's on sits inside its track.
  • Profiles near the edge. A profile opened low in a server's member list slides up to fit beside the name instead of jumping to the top of the window.
  • Discord menus fit and read. A submenu (Roles, Timeout, Mute, Notification Settings, Move to, Add to Calendar) opens beside its row as real glass, flips to the other side at the window's edge and keeps its row lit; a hovered row lifts the glass instead of glaring white or red, so a role's name no longer disappears; a menu taller than the window scrolls.
  • Sharp covers on hover. A game's cover in the Library, the Store and Shaders lifts without growing, so it stays sharp instead of blurring for a moment.
  • Fans no longer open your mic. With automatic input sensitivity, Exo's Discord now sends your voice only when it rises over the room after noise suppression, so the steady sound of fans and the room never opens it, and a word still opens it from its first syllable. The Input Sensitivity meter shows the sound as it's judged, after noise suppression.
  • Stream buttons say what they do. While watching a stream, Hide Members shows people instead of a smiley, and Full Screen shows the window's own maximize instead of an arrow.
  • Sound Volume for Discord's sounds. Notifications › Sounds has one volume for all of Discord's sounds: messages, rings and the call's sound effects.
  • Settings, Discord's settings and the Roles page are glass like every other dialog, and a sheet opened from the keyboard no longer draws a ring around itself.
  • Settings and Properties float. They're a panel of glass over the dimmed room again, instead of glass across the whole window.
  • Optional keys line up. Save and Get one (or Remove) sit beside the field instead of wrapping under it.
  • Discord's menus line up. A menu row's icon sits at its end, as Discord draws it, so every label starts on one line, and every Copy ID has its ID icon. GIFs and Stickers are the same size as Emoji, so switching tabs never resizes the picker.
  • A stream you watch stays on. Leave for another server and come back, and the stream you were watching is still there; its controls and LIVE show while you move the pointer over it and hide when it rests.
  • New upscalers without an Exo update. When Exo opens it asks NVIDIA, AMD and Intel on GitHub for their newest DLSS, FSR SDK (FSR 4) and XeSS release, and a new one reaches your games without waiting for Exo: Exo fetches the files your games use from the release itself and accepts each only when it matches GitHub's listing and carries the vendor's signature. FSR 4 had no other way in.
  • FSR 3.1 updates found. A game shipping FSR 3.1.2 (Call of Duty) was taken for newer than Exo's 3.1.4, because 3.1.2's file is numbered higher, and never offered the update; FSR 3.1 is now compared, and shown, by its release.
  • Your phone's calls show at once. Joining or leaving voice on another device shows on the PC right away; the PC no longer takes it for its own call and waits for a connection that never comes.
  • No flicker on glass. Glass buttons over a game's page, the Library's cards and the Bar no longer flash clear and pop back as a page opens or a room switches; a page rises from black instead of fading, so its glass reads the art from the first frame.
  • The Store's banner. The next game fades in only once its art has loaded and the last one leaves when the fade ends, so two games never show through each other, and the list beside it loads its art at once.
  • Every Store page laid out the same. A game's page has one layout now, whatever the game: the trailer and screenshots with Details under them on the left, and the logo, buying, About and the same seven requirement rows on the right, in the same places and sizes on every page. Each part holds its place from the first frame while the details load, so nothing moves or snaps when they land; it was arranged anew for every game, and again as its details arrived. Opening and closing start at once: beginning any view transition made the whole page work out its styles again (25 to 70 ms before anything moved). Closing, the tile's picture appears only as the page lands on it, not blown up across the window.
  • Spotify opens in Music every time. When an earlier Spotify left its crash reporter running, Exo took it for Spotify itself, so Music stayed on Opening Spotify and restarted Spotify each time you opened it. Exo now starts Spotify properly whatever was left behind.
  • Epic games update in Epic, out of sight. A game Epic's new launcher installed now updates in that launcher: Exo starts it hidden, asks it to update the game and follows its own record until the update lands. Exo had patched the files itself, which left Epic's launcher saying the update never finished, so the game showed Update forever. Games Epic's launcher doesn't own still update through Exo directly.
  • Tall profiles fit. A profile with many roles stays under the top bar instead of losing its banner beneath it.
  • Your mouse stays connected. Devices no longer drops a mouse that is still there while you use it. Exo reads a device's name and serial once instead of every time any device on your PC comes or goes, so a busy receiver can't make the mouse look like a different one; when several of those checks overlap, a late answer from an older one no longer disconnects it; and a check that fails leaves the mouse connected.
  • Finalmouse on its cable. A Finalmouse plugged in by its cable shows up in Devices and is set directly, with your saved profiles and its battery charging; unplug it and plug the receiver back in, and Devices goes back to the receiver by itself. With both in, Exo uses the cable.
  • No 255 % battery. Back on its receiver straight from the cable, before the mouse has linked again, a Finalmouse keeps its last battery reading instead of showing 255 %.

3.12.0 — October 20, 2026

This update gives Exo a new installer and a new first-run setup in Exo's own look, and lets Exo clear out the apps it replaces.

  • A new installer in Exo's look. Install, Update and Uninstall open in one small window with Exo's buttons, switch, colors and motion, and Exo's own minimize and close. Options holds the desktop shortcut, and a bar shows real progress while Exo copies.
  • The installer cleans up after itself. Every install and update removes copies left by earlier updates, old logs and downloaded installers, so only the app remains.
  • A new first-run setup. It opens with a welcome, then walks through game stores, music, Discord, captures and this PC in Settings' own rows and buttons, each step sliding in from the side it comes from. A step with nothing to set up on your PC is left out.
  • Captures in setup. The keys for saving a replay, recording and snipping, and Print Screen, ready to change before your first game.
  • Remove the apps Exo replaces. Setup and Settings > Clean up list Discord, Snipping Tool, Photos, Xbox Game Bar, Clipchamp and the NVIDIA App when your PC has them. Remove runs the app's own uninstaller, then deletes every folder, registry key, startup entry and shortcut it leaves, as Revo Uninstaller does. Anything in Program Files asks for Windows' permission once. Spotify, the Xbox app and the NVIDIA driver are never offered: Exo needs them.
  • After a removal, nothing nags. Without Snipping Tool, Print Screen opens Exo's snip; without Game Bar, Windows stops asking to install it again.
  • Leftovers cleared for you. What apps you already uninstalled left in your folders is cleared when setup or Settings > Clean up opens.

3.11.0 — October 20, 2026

This update cleans up what a second tester found: the Store page, game pages and Properties, Discord, accounts, and the polish everywhere.

  • Game pages without Captures: your clips and snips stay in Captures, not on each game's page.
  • A calmer game page. A longer Play with Remove and Properties beside it; Open folder, Verify files and the cover tools live in Properties now, and Upscalers and Shaders are one row each that opens its System page, where Update and Compile live. Allowing upscaler changes for a game with anti-cheat moved to its Properties.
  • Properties fits. A wide panel with its sections on the left, like Settings, with nothing to scroll.
  • Game art lifts on hover instead of drawing a ring around it, and games in Continue aren't repeated in All games.
  • Properties fit the store: Riot, Epic and other games show only what their store has, never Steam's overlay, cloud saves or launch options, and a section with nothing in it is left out.
  • Every DLC. Properties lists every DLC a Steam game has, outfits and upgrades included, marked Owned or Not owned; a switch shows only where Steam has one.
  • Content packs you can remove: packs Steam keeps only in a game's downloads, such as Call of Duty's Multiplayer and Zombies (over 200 GB together), show in Properties with a switch, like Marvel Rivals' HD pack.
  • Steam overlay off. It costs frames, so it is off by default for your Steam games; turn it on for one game in Properties or for all in Settings > Game stores. Exo changes it while Steam is closed, never while you play or download. Exo writes the switch where Steam reads it (it used to write a copy Steam ignores, so games still got the overlay).
  • Steam EULAs accepted for you: each installed game's EULA is accepted the way Steam's Accept button saves it, before launch if one is new, so no game stops at a EULA dialog or a blank Steam window.
  • Roblox's logo on its game page, as every other store's.
  • Game pages fill any window, from a half-screen window to a wide monitor, with nothing to scroll and spare room going to the trailer and the text, not to gaps.
  • Cards and your PC. About, Details and System requirements are cards; requirements read short, mark what your PC meets with its own part beside it, and say where you stand at the top.
  • Whole hero art across the top of the page.
  • Game pages open cleanly: the card grows into the page's window and shrinks back into it on close, as a card opens in the App Store, and a trailer shows its picture until it really plays.
  • Sharp banner art: a game without Steam's wide banner shows its full-size header instead of a small capsule stretched across the banner.
  • Other stores' prices come back: Exo asks CheapShark only when a page opens, not for every card you point at, which had it refusing Exo, and gives it a ten-minute break if it ever says to slow down.
  • Graphics asked for by memory. A game that asks only for video memory ("1 GB VRAM") is checked against your card's memory; it showed no check mark.
  • Collectibles, as Discord shows them: avatar decorations, nameplates, display name styles (in Discord's own fonts and effects), profile effects and profile colors show on profile cards, full profiles and the DM profile panel, and in the member list, DMs, Friends, call tiles and messages. They move only while Exo is in front (in lists and messages while pointed at, and with Performance mode only while pointed at) and stay still with Reduce motion.
  • Discord's Shop: Shop sits under Friends, as in Discord, with Discord's catalog by category, Avatar Decorations, Profile Effects, Nameplates and Your Collection, each item shown on your own profile card, picture or name with the price you pay and the Nitro price. Use and Remove put on and take off decorations, nameplates and profile effects you own, here and in Discord settings > Profiles.
  • Buying in Exo: Buy checks out with a card already saved on your Discord account, showing Discord's exact price with tax before you confirm; Exo never sees card numbers, and a payment your bank must confirm (or PayPal and other methods) finishes in Discord's own shop, as does an account with no saved card.
  • Profiles show what you wear: Discord settings > Profiles has your real profile card beside the fields, with your decoration, effect, colors and unsaved changes, plus a counter in About Me and a proper banner color swatch; Account and the panel at the bottom show your collectibles too.
  • Role channels show at once and stay: Exo asks Discord for your own roles in each server as soon as it connects, so channels only your roles open (moderator channels and the like) show right away and don't drop out after a reconnect.
  • Raw mic, no Windows processing: Exo takes the mic as it is, without Windows' or the driver's enhancements or anything an Equalizer APO chain adds, and Windows' echo cancellation, gain control and noise suppression stay off; the Echo Cancellation switch is gone.
  • The noise gate hears speech: with noise suppression on, it opens for sound that suppression keeps (speech, down to -65 dBFS) and stays shut for what it cuts (noise), so a quiet voice gets through and leftover noise never does.
  • Clip Guard: a limiter keeps your voice under -1 dBFS when gain control lifts it or you shout, adding no delay.
  • Noise suppression at full strength: it removes noise all the way, as DeepFilterNet is built to, with its post-filter off; about 5% of one core and 20 ms.
  • A quiet mic gets through: with noise suppression on, the automatic noise gate opens for a voice down to -70 dBFS instead of -55, which held a quiet mic shut.
  • Voice settings, plain switches: Noise Suppression, the noise gate (Input Sensitivity), Echo Cancellation and Automatic Gain Control, each yours to turn on or off, with Voice Check to hear yourself before and after. No profiles, Voice Focus or Voice Tune. A mic that cleans itself, such as NVIDIA Broadcast's or AMD's, gets no second pass from Exo.
  • AMD Noise Suppression's mic recognized: pick AMD Software's "AMD Streaming Audio Device" as your mic and Exo's noise suppression steps aside for AMD's, so your voice isn't cleaned twice.
  • Mutes are kept: muting a person or a stream now saves to your Discord account; a mute without a volume change used to fail to save.
  • Typing stays out of calls: noise suppression takes noise down 30 dB (36 in a noisy room) instead of 18, enough to remove a keyboard, without the post-filter that muffled voices. Voice Tune no longer raises Microphone Volume past 100%, since gain control already lifts a quiet voice.
  • Chats stay at the newest message when you come back to Discord or pictures load in.
  • Background noise stays out: the voice gate opens for sound that lasts at least 4 ms, so steady noise can't open it, and still adds no delay.
  • Sharp streams and cameras: Exo now tells Discord what arrives and how much it can take, as Discord's own app does, so a stream rises to the quality it is sent at instead of staying at its starting bitrate (a 1440p stream came in at 1.5 Mbit/s and looked blocky).
  • Watching a stream: Hide Members lets the stream take the room, and Full Screen (or a double-click) puts it on the whole screen; Esc leaves. Stream Volume and Mute Stream sit beside them, kept on your account as Discord keeps them.
  • Soundboard volume and mute: Discord settings > Voice has Mute Soundboards and a Soundboard Volume slider for the sounds others play in a call.
  • Voice channel chat gets Discord's short header with Close Chat, and Show or Hide Chat in the call.
  • The voice bar names the call as channel / server wherever you are; click the name to open the call.
  • The picture viewer is the picture alone; right-click to copy, save or open, videos included.
  • Spotify Jam links in chat show an invite card with Join, which joins in Exo's Music room.
  • Server emoji you can use send as emoji, never as a link; ones you can't are locked in the picker.
  • Sticker favorites, synced with your Discord account, with right-click Favorite.
  • Instant profile panel in DMs, and the bar above the DM doesn't repeat the person while it's open.
  • Who's in voice: pointing at a server shows who is in its voice channels and who is streaming.
  • Lock icons on private channels, as in Discord.
  • One field style for every search, with the text centered.
  • Lighter calls: noise suppression reuses one engine instead of leaving a thread running after every call and Voice Check, received sound allocates nothing per packet, the microphone is cut into frames without per-sample work, and app.log says each minute how much CPU the call takes.
  • Your voice reaches the call sooner, and sounds like you: with a game in front, Exo no longer slows itself down, lowers its priority or pages noise suppression out of memory during a call, and the microphone never waits on the rest of the voice's processing. Voice Focus is now a plain gate, low cut and limiter that adds no delay (it held every word back 20 ms). Noise suppression turns noise down by at most 24 dB, with its post-filter off; unlimited, it took parts of your voice with the noise and muffled it. Voice Check's "after" is what the other person hears, Opus included, and app.log says each minute how old your voice is when it leaves Exo and where that time went.
  • Unread dots clear when you're caught up. Reaching the bottom reads a channel to its newest message, as Discord does, so a deleted last message, a jump that lands at the bottom or a channel too short to scroll no longer leaves the dot lit. Forums read when you open them and light up for new posts. Mark As Read (a channel, a server, the Inbox) and Mark Unread change the dots at once. Channels you never opened count as read up to when you joined, and servers using Channels & Roles light up only for channels in your list.
  • Friend, Ignore and Block in a person's menu. Right-click someone or use their profile's More for Start a Call, Add Friend (Accept Friend Request, Remove Friend), Ignore and Block, with Discord's questions. The profile card has Discord's Add Friend button beside More, and people you ignore no longer notify you.
  • Channel menu: Mute Channel for 15 minutes up to until you turn it back on, Notification Settings, Mark As Read only when there is something to read, and Invite People says the link was copied.
  • Server menu: Show All Channels in community servers, and no Leave Server for the server's owner.
  • Messages: Add Reaction opens the message menu, Discord links open in the room at the message, Pin and Unpin ask first (Shift skips), the pins list has Unpin, Esc marks the channel read, and typing reads "A and B are typing…".
  • Friends: right-click a friend or request for the person menu.
  • Discord settings: "Use data to personalize my Discord experience", Deauthorize asks first, "On servers I moderate" shows spoilers where you can manage messages, and AutoMod, Roles, Profiles, Members and Engagement use Discord's words.
  • Submenus stay open on the way. In a person's menu, crossing another row on the way into Roles, Move to or Timeout no longer swaps the open submenu out from under the pointer.
  • Mod-only channels show at once and stay. Exo took the first member Discord listed for a server as you, which could be someone else, so role-only channels appeared late or vanished after a reconnect; it now picks you by id and asks for your roles once after connecting.
  • Friend requests from strangers. Accepting a request from someone you share no server with asks first, as Discord does, then goes through; before, Discord refused it silently.
  • "You accepted X's friend request." The line Discord puts at the top of a new friend's DM shows as Discord's, not as a blank message from you.
  • Server right-click menu. Mark As Read, Invite to Server, Mute Server for a while, Notification Settings with Suppress @everyone and @here and Suppress All Role @mentions, Leave Server, Copy Server ID.
  • Unread DMs on the rail. Each DM with unread messages sits under Home with its count, as in Discord.
  • DM menus. A DM's right-click adds Mark As Read, Close DM, Mute @user and Copy Channel ID; a group DM has Mark As Read, Mute Conversation, Leave Group and Copy Channel ID.
  • Failed messages stay. A message Discord didn't take stays in the chat in red with why, Retry and Delete.
  • Blocked and ignored messages fold. "3 blocked messages — Show messages", as Discord groups them.
  • Discord's tab has its own icon. A chat icon in the top bar, so it no longer matches Friends inside Discord.
  • Profile effects put on and come off from the Shop and Profiles, as Discord's own Profiles saves them.
  • Bio box in Profiles no longer clips its first line under the rounded corner.
  • Go Live at the right bit rate, and at 120 fps. Each size and frame rate streams at H.264's usual best (8 Mbit/s at 1080p 30, 12 at 1080p 60, 24 at 1440p 60, 50 at 4K 60), and 120 fps is offered for screens that fast, up to 1440p; Source on a 4K screen streams 4K.
  • Opening some DMs no longer breaks the room. When Discord refused someone's profile, Exo passed its refusal on as the profile and the DM's side panel crashed; it now shows their name and picture.
  • GIF pictures hold still with Reduced Motion. GIFs sent as pictures (not just GIF videos) stay on their first frame until you point at them when Reduced Motion or Performance mode is on, or Play GIFs is off.
  • Unread DMs on the rail always show their count, as Discord's do.
  • Add a card and pay in Exo. With no card on your Discord account, or to use another, the Shop's checkout adds one: your billing address, then the card in Stripe's own field (Discord's payment service), saved to your Discord account. A payment your bank wants to confirm shows its check over Exo, then completes, instead of sending you to Discord's website. Exo never sees the card number.
  • The mic closes between words again. With noise suppression on, the automatic gate also follows what comes out between your words, so a steady sound suppression keeps (music or a game in the mic, a voice processed before Exo) no longer holds it open; it had stayed open almost all the time.
  • Best for this screen means all of it: on NVIDIA it also sets No scaling on the GPU and Override the scaling set by games, and says In use only when everything matches.
  • Compile times right after Scan in System > Shaders.
  • Up to date without asking twice: Upscalers reads your games again after Update all.
  • Every Starlight X shows everything, on any firmware; a setting the mouse didn't report says why.
  • Driver installs report what happened. After NVIDIA's installer finished, Exo read the version from the driver it had loaded at start and called a good install failed; it now reads the driver Windows records, so the install says Done and System shows the new version without a restart.
  • Lucid's sounds and a roomier headphone picker. With Lucid's notifications on, Switch sound and Game sound sliders set its cue volumes (0 is off); the headphone search opens as its own panel with taller rows.
  • Start Lucid with Exo stays on. Saving settings copied each one by name and missed Lucid's, so the switch turned itself back off; every setting now survives a save (a test checks them all).
  • Driver updates clean up after themselves. The unpacked driver (about 3 GB) is removed however an install ends, including when NVIDIA's installer stops or Windows permission is declined; leftovers from before are removed too.
  • Safer links. A Steam link can open the store, a game's page, install or run a game, but never pass launch options or client commands.
  • Equalizer APO's device picker opens in front of Exo when you choose Lucid's devices, instead of behind it.
  • Lucid Voice stays off. Exo Voice already cleans up your mic, and a second chain before it adds delay in calls, so Exo turns Lucid Voice off and keeps it off however it gets switched on, Lucid installed later included. A Voice Tune made through Lucid Voice is let go of then, since its gate was set for Lucid's louder sound. Lucid's headphone EQ is untouched.
  • Spotify, however it was installed: installer, winget, Chocolatey, Scoop or a moved folder. The Microsoft Store edition offers Switch to Spotify's app, keeping your account and library.
  • No start-with-Windows switches. Exo turns store clients, Discord and Spotify off at startup by itself, once, the way Task Manager does, and starts them when needed.
  • Steam Switch works on one click, shows each step, and brings up Steam's sign-in when an account needs it.
  • Riot Client closes with its own quit for Add account and Switch; Vanguard and games are never touched.
  • Lucid's sounds and a roomier headphone picker. With Lucid's notifications on, Switch sound and Game sound sliders set its cue volumes (0 is off); the headphone search opens as its own panel with taller rows.
  • Start Lucid with Exo stays on. Saving settings copied each one by name and missed Lucid's, so the switch turned itself back off; every setting now survives a save (a test checks them all).
  • Snip without a flash, and Esc cancels: starting a snip no longer flashes black over your screens, and a snip started from Exo takes the keyboard, so Esc cancels and Enter takes the whole screen.
  • Rooms stay as you left them: coming back to Music or Discord no longer reloads the page, replays its slide-in or jumps to the top; the open chat keeps its messages and only fetches what is new. The Store keeps its scroll too.
  • Songs without the mood row: the genre and mood chips over your Liked Songs are gone, so the list starts right under its title.
  • True black everywhere: panels and sheets are AMOLED black set apart by one thin line instead of a gray, see-through fill. Properties' section list sits inside its panel, as in Settings, and Devices matches.
  • Black liquid glass: buttons, fields, chips, Discord's composer and search, and Exo Mouse's controls are one solid polished-black material with a light rim along the top, plainly visible on black. Fields sit recessed, a press sinks, and Play, Get on Steam and Confirm stay cream.
  • Setup is Exo's own window: black and borderless, with the Exo mark, one Install button, a thin progress bar and Launch Exo; if setup fails, the reason shows on its last screen. Setup, the uninstaller, shortcuts and Apps & features all show the current Exo mark, sharp at every size, and in-app updates stay silent.
  • Rooms come back loaded: their arrival animation plays only the first time.
  • No flicker: Settings opens with one float-in, and pages never stretch mid-transition.
  • No light rim on the rounded corners of game art.
  • One look for every field, words on true center.
  • Captures favorites: the star turns at once and a Favorites chip shows every starred capture.
  • Clicks never get lost: a click while a page is still moving lands on what you pointed at, opens it and stays open, and a page can never freeze mid-move.
  • Downloads live on the cards: the top-bar download pill and the floating download list are gone; each game card shows its own progress ring and Update tag.
  • Settings panels are evenly inset. Exo's and Discord's floating settings keep 12px all round, and the boxes inside follow the panel's corners.

3.10.0 — October 19, 2026

This update rebuilds the Library around what you actually do, gives Exo a warm cream-on-black look with its own black Liquid Glass, and makes Exo's Discord client match Discord feature for feature in Exo's style.

  • Properties for every installed game. On its page and in its card's right-click menu: launch options, language, cloud saves, its files (size, Browse, Verify, which drive it's on), automatic updates, betas with access codes, and its DLC, such as Marvel Rivals' High-Resolution Pack, switched on or off with the space it takes or frees shown before anything downloads or is deleted. Epic games get their optional content, such as high-resolution textures, the same way.
  • Your Steam Family's games. Every game your Steam Family shares sits in the Library next to your own, installed or not, and Install hands it to Steam; one you are downloading shows up with Steam's progress the moment it starts. Games you own yourself always count as yours, only games make the list, and Steam games you own but haven't installed no longer go missing.
  • Steam accounts in Settings. Every account Steam remembers on this PC is listed under Steam with the signed-in one marked, Switch and Remove on the others, and Add account to sign another in through Steam's own window. Switching restarts Steam hidden as that account, and your library and hours follow it.
  • Riot accounts. Switch between your League and VALORANT accounts in Settings > Game stores, or right-click a Riot game and pick Play as. Exo keeps each account's "Stay signed in" session encrypted on this PC, never switches while a Riot game runs, and never touches Vanguard.
  • Store apps stay out of sight. Steam, the Epic Games Launcher, GOG Galaxy, Riot, the EA app, Ubisoft Connect, Battle.net, Amazon Games and Xbox stay hidden for as long as Exo runs them, not just while a game starts, and their tray icons go away until you open one or close Exo. Sign-in, Steam Guard and agreements still show.
  • Apps start when they're needed. Settings > Game stores and General have a switch for each store app, Discord and Spotify that starts with Windows, and first run turns them off for you unless you say otherwise; turning one back on restores it exactly. The new Epic Games Launcher starts in its tray with its notifications off while Exo starts it.
  • Quiet installs. Visual C++ installs with only a progress bar, WebView2 and Spotify without an installer window, and none of Exo's background tools flash a console window. Windows still asks for permission where it must.
  • Set up for you. On an NVIDIA PC, first run ends with This PC: Exo's driver profile for your card and screen, on by default and applied when you finish (undo it any time in System > Graphics). Everything else Exo tunes by itself, and a new Library sorts installed games first.
  • Steam's own downloads show live progress. When Steam updates a game by itself (its auto-update, or one you started in Steam), Exo now follows it with the same live reader as its own downloads, so Downloads shows real progress and speed instead of a size Steam rewrites only now and then. Nothing is asked of Steam and its window is left alone.
  • The Library stays where you left it. Coming back to the Library no longer scrolls it down on its own (the A to Z strip that did it is gone).
  • Installed first. A sort that puts the games you can play now first, A to Z, then the rest. Games you haven't installed sit darker and drained of color until you point at them.
  • Continue. The games you are playing sit at the top as big landscape art with Play one click away: the one running glows green, one downloading or updating shows a ring with its percent, the rest show playtime and when you last played. The first game's art lights the black around it.
  • Friends are playing. When friends on Exo's Discord client are in a game you own, it shows as one capsule with their faces, the game and Play (or Install).
  • One grid of every game. Installed and not installed live together in the order you sort them; a game you haven't installed carries a small download mark instead of a separate shelf.
  • Clicking fast opens, never plays. Clicking a game again and again opened its page and then pressed Play under the pointer (double-click played on purpose). Only Play plays now: presses in the streak that opened the page do nothing.
  • The Library holds still. Opening a game's page nudged the Library up 5 px (its buttons left the shelf header); they stay now.
  • The store beside the name. A game's page shows its store (or the stores to choose between) as a pill beside its name, and the cover sits alone with the same space above and below it.
  • GOG games Exo installs now start, and Update really updates. Exo recorded the folder above the game, so Play never started it, and Update only re-checked the files it had. It now records the game's own folder, fixes games installed earlier, and Update moves to the newest build.
  • Downloads show what's really happening. GOG shows a percentage; Epic, GOG and Amazon show your network speed instead of disk writes; the status line is Exo's own words instead of the tool's log lines. A failed Epic install says why at once instead of waiting up to two hours.
  • Epic games Exo installs, start and leave cleanly. Play starts a game Exo installed through Epic in about two seconds (Epic's own launcher had no record of it and started nothing), an uninstalled game no longer comes back as installed, Cancel removes the half-finished download while Pause keeps it to resume and says Paused, and a finished install shows as installed at once.
  • Steam downloads show their speed, and Cancel leaves nothing. A Steam download shows how fast it is going beside the gigabytes, worked out from what Steam reports, and a canceled Steam install leaves no empty folder in your Steam library.
  • Nothing stays stuck. Stop clears an EA, Ubisoft or Battle.net launch that never started. A canceled Steam update or a damaged download queue no longer blocks installs, paused downloads no longer turn off auto-update, uninstallers can ask Windows for permission, and the window no longer freezes while a game uninstalls.
  • A menu on every game. Right-click any game, or press More on Continue, for Play or Install and Open folder. Play from Continue or Friends are playing says Starting… and reports a failure instead of doing nothing.
  • The game page says what happens. Shaders compiles that game right there (Get Exo Shaders when it's missing), HDR says what really happens and offers to turn on HDR for HDR games, and failed upscaler reads and library refreshes say why, with Check again or Retry.
  • Uninstall does what it says. An uninstaller whose path has spaces and no quotes starts instead of failing at "C:\Program"; an Amazon or GOG game Exo couldn't remove says so instead of reporting it removed or handing the job to another copy of the game.
  • A store that can't be read is read again. When one store's library can't be read, the next refresh asks it again instead of reusing its older list; Steam playtimes no longer blank out while the Library refreshes.
  • Pins are gone. The Library is one grid in the order you sort it; nothing offers Pin anymore. Settings saved with pins still load.
  • Activate key fills the key in for you. Steam opens its own key page with your key entered and GOG's redeem page arrives filled in; you accept and confirm there. Epic's page can't take a key, so Exo copies it for you to paste, and the popover says which store does what.
  • Game pages fill the window. On a Store game page the trailer starts at the top and is as large as your screen allows, and the game's name, tags, store buttons, description, details and requirements fill the right side top to bottom with no empty band; on smaller windows the details tuck under the screenshots, and nothing ever scrolls. Steam descriptions no longer show stray &amp; codes.
  • The trailer gets its size back. System requirements and This PC sit in the right column under buying again, so a game's trailer and pictures fill the left side.
  • The Store recovers on its own. A search or home page that can't reach the store says so with Retry instead of loading forever, a failed Refresh says it failed, Epic, GOG and Ubisoft prices come back on the next visit after a slow answer, the home page shows refreshes that landed while you were elsewhere, and region-locked games say they aren't sold in your region.
  • Clearer game pages. System requirements sit under the trailer and the tags stay clear of them. Free-to-play games show Free, unreleased wishlist games show their date, and This PC says whether your graphics card is enough for Minimum or Recommended.
  • Reduce motion holds the Store still.
  • Your wishlist never looks empty when it isn't. If Exo can't read the wishlist for a moment, the Store keeps it on screen and says so. Adding or removing a game that can't be saved goes back, with the reason.
  • Epic's free games stay current. A saved Store page no longer shows a free game whose free days have ended.
  • Game pages fit narrow windows. Below 900 px a game page scrolls as one column, and long developer and publisher lists wrap.
  • System requirements are back. Minimum and Recommended show under the trailer again, ending with This PC (your Windows, processor, graphics card and memory against the game's); they had slipped out of view below the media.
  • Tags in the same place on every page. Short logos, tall logos and games with no logo all put the tags at the same height.
  • Lucid, set up from Exo. Settings > Lucid starts Lucid with Exo (out of sight), opens it or its first setup, picks the outputs it plays through, and chooses your headphones from Lucid's own list, with search and favorites. Without Lucid, the same place offers Get Lucid.
  • Lucid's game and night settings in Exo. Game Mode on or off, Keep the game's EQ on Alt+Tab and Only while gaming, Night Shift hours or Windows Night light, its notifications and Discord status. Each game's own EQ stays as Lucid tunes it. Lucid closes the way Windows closes it at sign-out and comes back out of sight with the change; Lucid Voice is never touched, since Exo Voice handles the mic.
  • Lucid's updates in Settings. Settings > Lucid shows the installed version and says when a newer Lucid is out, with Update to open Lucid, which installs it itself. While Exo runs it, Lucid stays out of sight with no tray icon, which comes back when Exo closes.
  • First run offers Lucid. This PC adds Start Lucid with Exo (off until you turn it on) and Set up Lucid, or Get Lucid when this PC doesn't have it.
  • Exo moves like one app. Rooms slide toward the tab you pick, pages inside a room push in from the side you chose, and the art you open flies to where it lands and back: a game's cover into its page, a Store tile into its game page, an album into its page, the song into Now playing, a Discord picture into the profile or the viewer, a capture into its viewer. A press mid-move ends it and lands where you pointed, and Reduce motion turns it all off.
  • Highlights follow rounded corners. Discord's server name, hover bar, folders and user panel, Music's sidebar rows and recent covers, Ctrl+K rows, and the focus rings on switches, sliders and text buttons now take the shape of what you point at instead of showing a square. Spacing is even too: Discord's settings, user panel and thread list, System's header, first run's music tiles and the downloads panel sit at the same insets as everything around them.
  • Settings float. Exo's Settings and Discord's settings open as one panel over the room you were in, as Discord's own settings do now: up to 1200 by 880, centered over the dimmed room, and a press around them closes them.
  • A System room. Your graphics card and screen get their own room between Music and Devices: a slim header with your card, driver and screen, and three pages that fit the window without scrolling: Graphics (driver and Exo's profile, HDR, upscalers in your games), Display, and Shaders. The Library no longer has a GPU button.
  • Shaders, simply. Scan games, see every game as its poster with its state, press Compile, and watch the queue play out live on the posters: the one compiling first with its progress, the rest in order. Click a poster to compile just that game.
  • The right NVIDIA driver. Choose Game Ready or Studio. On Game Ready, a newer GeForce hotfix driver from NVIDIA's support site is offered when there is one. Workstation cards (NVIDIA RTX, Quadro) get NVIDIA's RTX Enterprise driver instead of the data center one, and laptops their notebook driver.
  • Upscalers line up. Update all and Check again end where the panel's line above them ends, instead of 20 px short.
  • Pages fade at their edges. A page that scrolls fades softly into the black at its top and bottom instead of being cut off, and Store shelves fade at a side with more beyond it. The see-through band under the top bar is gone.
  • The Store shows its rhythm. A thin line fills under the featured game until the next one, and holds while you point at the banner.
  • This PC under the requirements. A Store game's System requirements end with your Windows, processor, graphics card and memory, and whether your memory covers the minimum or recommended. The Steam Deck rating is gone (Exo plays on Windows).
  • Things glide. The rooms slide to make room when the song comes and goes in the bar instead of jumping.
  • A quieter top bar. Search is a smaller capsule without the Ctrl K label, and the light that swept under the bar while a game started is gone (Play's own animation shows it).
  • No duplicates. Each thing has one home: Discord's account, voice and notification settings live in Discord's settings (the gear in your Discord bar opens them), and in the Music room the song is in the room's own player, not also in the top bar.
  • A cleaner search. Ctrl+K lists each game, room, DM and server on one line, with no notes under them.
  • Pure black. Every page's background is black again: no colored glow behind art, and no cut-off color under the top bar.
  • Menus match. Every menu and dropdown (Exo's, Discord's and the mouse's) has the same rows, the same hover and a check beside the current choice.
  • Smoother art corners. Art no longer has a hairline under it, which showed as a ragged ring around rounded corners.
  • Cream on black. Text is a warm cream with warm grays, the same in every room; section titles are one size everywhere, Store included.
  • Black Liquid Glass. Buttons over game art are a dark glass that bends the art at its edge, with a light rim. It draws solid while a game runs and when Windows' transparency effects are off or contrast is up.
  • One design across the app. Every room now shares one Apple-style system: controls are filled capsules instead of outlines, one gray marks what is selected everywhere, sidebars and side panels float as rounded panels (Settings, Music, Discord, Devices), Music's player is a floating bar, menus and popovers are one raised panel, sheets have wider corners, and switches are full-size.
  • One bar. Search on the left (the Exo mark; Ctrl+K), the rooms in the middle, the song beside them, and Capture and Settings on the right. A room's own controls stay in the room. The bar shares its width and compacts in steps on smaller windows, so nothing overlaps.
  • Live, beside the rooms. The song playing, a Discord call, the game you're playing or a download sits in one small capsule next to the rooms; hover the song and it grows in place into the full player (skip, seek, time and volume) without moving anything. _Verified: on screen._ Downloads
  • Every press gives. Buttons and capsules give way a little under the press.
  • The bar's controls hold still. The mini player's seek line and volume could drag the window instead of moving: the bar's clickable spots were measured mid-glide and never again. They now follow every glide to where it lands.
  • Songs change in place. The mini player kept vanishing for a moment between two songs, so it shrank and grew back; the last song now stays until the next one arrives.
  • Window controls in the Tools capsule. Minimize, maximize and close sit in the same floating capsule as Capture and Settings, after a hairline, so the top right is one piece like the rest of the bar.
  • Every room is ready when the splash ends. Rooms loaded behind the splash didn't actually ask for their data until you opened them (a hidden room runs nothing). Now System (card, driver, screen, Display, Shaders), the Store, Discord and Music load during the splash, so they open filled.
  • Covers at full quality. The mini player's cover is the picture the music app gives, untouched, instead of a 256 px recompressed copy; Spotify's comes at 640 px and Apple Music's at 600. When Spotify's page has no cover yet, Windows' copy of the same song's cover stands in.
  • Settings uses the window. On a wide window each panel lays its rows in two columns, and the sidebar lines up with the Search capsule.
  • Settings follows you down the page. Scrolling marks every section in the sidebar in turn; short sections near the end were skipped.
  • Settings like System Settings. Each section has its icon in the sidebar and the settings sit right beside it.
  • Cream text everywhere. A theme setting was painting text plain white over Exo's cream; every room now shows the warm cream and grays.
  • Centered labels. Text in buttons and tabs sat 1-1.5px above center in places; every label now sits on its middle.
  • New icons. Every icon in Exo is now Lucide: one clean stroke, the same weight everywhere.
  • The Exo Island. A small capsule in the top bar while something is live that the room you're in isn't showing: a Discord call (mute and leave from anywhere, the dot rings when someone speaks) or the game you're playing (click to open it). Nothing live, nothing shown.
  • Ctrl+K finds everything. Besides games and actions it now finds your Discord DMs and servers (Enter opens them) and plays, pauses or skips music.
  • The top bar never overlaps. The song and the call compact inside their own slots as the window narrows, the download capsule opens the Library's queue from any room, and Ctrl+K keeps Tab inside, walks rows in the order shown and shows Check for updates as a toast.
  • Errors where they happened. Settings and first run put every error in red under the row that caused it (update installs, store sign-ins, folders, Capture and Music), keep first-run errors until you act, and cap Settings at a 1080 px reading column.
  • Covers with clean corners. Game art, posters, avatars and thumbnails no longer show a thin gray rim or jagged pixels at their rounded corners; hover and selection draw a ring just outside the picture.
  • Every button the same in every room. Primary, regular, quiet and danger buttons each have one fill, with one hover and one pressed shade, across every room and Exo Mouse.
  • One slider everywhere. Discord's settings, Capture, Display, Music, the mini player and Exo Mouse all use the same slider with a visible knob.
  • The song stays in a small window. When the window narrows, the mini player keeps the cover, title and volume, letting the room names, artist and skip buttons go first.
  • An emoji picker you can see. The picker is Discord's size, nine 32px emoji to a row, server emoji and Unicode alike. Favorites and Frequently Used from your Discord account sit at the top, and right-clicking an emoji favorites it everywhere you use Discord. Reacting with ©, ®, ™, ⚧ or 👁‍🗨 no longer fails with "Unknown Emoji", and without Nitro another server's emoji shows locked instead of failing.
  • Discord feels instant. Channels and DMs you have opened show their messages the moment you go back, a channel or server you rest the pointer on loads before you click, switching servers keeps the channel and member lists on screen, and the member list no longer empties when you change channel or the connection drops for a moment.
  • Notifications behave like Discord's. No pings for @silent messages, blocked people, message requests, muted categories and their threads, or during Do Not Disturb, and never twice for one message. While Exo is in front they arrive as Exo's own banner with the person's picture, where it was said and two lines of the message; clicking one opens the chat. Opening or reading a chat, here or in any Discord app, clears its notifications, Windows' included.
  • Discord's image menu. Right-click any picture, including one open full size, for Copy Image (the picture itself), Save Image (to Downloads under Discord's file name), Copy Link and Open Link; the viewer has a Save button.
  • Pictures load like Discord's. Pictures are fetched at the size they are shown instead of as multi-megabyte originals that sat half-drawn in busy channels like Finalmouse's announcements; single pictures show up to Discord's 550 by 350, and YouTube previews lose their black bars.
  • Menus open where there's room. Every menu, picker, profile card and submenu flips up or left near an edge and stays inside the window, and a right-click opens the message menu at the pointer, so the last message's menu opens upward.
  • Attachments you can manage. Attached files show a picture preview and a shortened name, so Remove always shows, and Enter sends pictures with no text.
  • The unread dot clears at the bottom. Reaching the newest message clears the channel's dot and mention count at once.
  • The Inbox has For You, Unreads and Mentions. For You shows Discord's notifications and opens what each one points to; Unreads lists every unread channel across your servers with its newest messages, a jump, and marking a channel or server read; Mentions can show just this server and leave out @everyone, @here or role mentions.
  • Search works like Discord's search bar. from:, mentions:, has:, in:, before:, during:, after:, pinned: and authorType: with suggestions that become chips; results show their count, sort by Newest, Oldest or Most Relevant, and come 25 to a page.
  • Reactions show who reacted. Point at one for the names or right-click for the full list (remove someone's with Manage Messages); super reactions show in their colors and, with Nitro, can be added.
  • Files look like Discord's. Several pictures share one grid, described pictures get an ALT tag, text and code files show their first lines with Expand, and other files show as a card with size and download.
  • Slash commands and app buttons. Type / to run an app's commands with each option filled in, see "is thinking…" and dismiss answers only you can see; app buttons, menus and cards work in both chat styles, app forms open as Exo dialogs, and right-click a person or a message for its Apps.
  • Profiles you can act on. Every connection type shows its own mark and opens like Discord's; where you can manage roles, give or take a role right from a profile; full profiles have the friend buttons and your own has Edit Profile; Settings > Profiles gains Server Profiles for per-server nicknames.
  • First run includes Discord. Setup gains a Discord step: sign in with Discord's phone QR code (or see that you already are), and a pointer to Voice Tune. It can be skipped; the Discord room offers the same code later.
  • Go Live says what it really sends. A resolution bigger than the screen or window you picked says so, for example "Your screen is 1080p, so this streams at 1080p." Exo never enlarges your picture the way Discord does, which adds no detail.
  • Chat reads at Discord's size. Messages and names are 15px with Discord's line and group spacing, and the last message has room above the message box instead of sitting on it.
  • In Exo's look. Black surfaces with hairlines, cream text and cream primary buttons, Exo's red and green for states, and a compact server rail with 40px icons. The selection glides between channels, DMs and Friends tabs.
  • Servers like Discord. Server Guide with its resources, Events (create, edit, start, end, cancel, interested, add to calendar), Browse Channels, Members with join method and roles, Create Channel, Invite, mute and mark as read. Tested on a real server.
  • DMs and Friends like Discord. Active Now with the game or song and time elapsed, a profile beside each DM with mutual servers and friends, full profiles, and call lines in the chat.
  • Quiet while playing. While a game runs, Discord messages wait in the unread badge instead of popping up. On by default; turn it off in Discord's settings > Notifications.
  • Discord's settings, all of them. The gear opens Discord's own User Settings, laid out as Discord's: Profiles (display name, pronouns, avatar, banner color, About Me), Account (username, your email and phone number masked, two-factor status, Logged-in Devices with Log Out; the password and closing the account open on Discord's site), Data & Privacy, Messaging Permissions (sensitive media filters, the spam filter, DMs and message requests from server members, who can send friend requests), Notifications, Voice & Video, Appearance, Accessibility, System, Language & Time, Activity Privacy, Connected Apps (connections, and authorized apps with Deauthorize) and Developer. Account settings are your Discord account's own, saved the way Discord's apps save them, so a change here shows in Discord everywhere.
  • Voice like Discord's. Input Profile is Voice Isolation, Studio or Custom; in Custom, Input Sensitivity (automatic, or a threshold over a live meter of your microphone), Noise Suppression, Echo Cancellation (Windows' own, on microphones that have it) and Automatic Gain Control. Push to Talk takes a key or a side mouse button and a release delay; Global Attenuation lowers other apps while someone speaks; Keybinds (Push to Talk, Push to Mute, Toggle Mute, Toggle Deafen, Toggle Voice Activity Mode, Toggle Streamer Mode, Disconnect from Voice) work anywhere in Windows during a call, even in a game. Pick the camera calls use and test it.
  • Discord's sounds. New Message, Incoming and Outgoing Ring, Mute, Unmute, Deafen, Undeafen, PTT, User Join and Leave, Voice Disconnected and Camera On and Off play as in Discord, each one switchable with a preview, and Disable All Notification Sounds silences them all. Windows notifications, taskbar flashing and the taskbar's unread badge can each be turned off.
  • Chat as you set it. Compact message display, text size in chat, space between message groups, zoom, saturation, role colors in names, beside them or not at all, links always underlined, spoilers always shown, pictures and link previews off, emoji reactions hidden, emoticons sent as emoji, stickers in autocomplete, 12- or 24-hour times, /tts messages read out at your rate, Copy IDs with Developer Mode, and Streamer Mode hiding your email, connections and invite links and silencing sounds and notifications.
  • A smaller home mark. The Discord mark at the top of the server rail is a small glyph in its tile, not a full white tile.
  • Only Exo's client. The Discord room is always Exo's own client; Exo no longer hosts, installs or patches the Discord app, and the switch for it in Settings is gone.
  • Your bar floats. Your name, mute, deafen and settings sit in their own floating bar under the sidebar; devices and status are set in Discord's settings, not from small arrows beside the buttons. A call floats above it and opens the call when clicked.
  • Direct messages shows a count. No selection pill beside it, just a number when something is waiting.
  • Buttons close what they opened. Pressing Pinned messages, Threads, Inbox, GIFs, Stickers or Emoji again closes it.
  • Profiles and pictures open. Click someone's picture in chat or in Friends for their full profile; images and GIFs from links open full size like uploads do.
  • Easier to read chat. Names are a step larger than the messages under them, with Discord's spacing between groups.
  • Performance mode. In Discord's settings > Accessibility, Enable Reduced Motion: GIFs hold still until you point at them, and nothing in Discord animates or moves on hover.
  • GIFs: Trending and Favorites. Favorites are your Discord account's own list, the same as on every device: the star adds or removes one. GIFs show in two columns at their own heights, and load again: Discord now serves them from a new host Exo had blocked.
  • Voice channels have a view. Opening a voice channel shows everyone in it as a tile with a ring while they speak, and the call's controls (mute, deafen, disconnect, or Join Voice) in one bar under them.
  • Video in calls: Go Live, screen share and cameras. Share Your Screen in a call goes out end-to-end encrypted like Discord's own, its sound as music-quality stereo that never echoes the call's voices. Turn On Camera does the same with your camera, encoded by the graphics card (H.264). People streaming show LIVE with Watch Stream, which opens their stream big above the call with its sound; cameras show in the tiles.
  • Go Live like Discord, with Exo's own picker. Share Your Screen opens Exo's picker instead of Windows': Applications and Screens, each a fresh picture with its title and app icon, and the game you're playing first. Then Resolution (720p, 1080p, 1440p or Source) and Frame rate (15, 30 or 60), each with one line saying what it's for, and Stream sound, beside a live preview of what you'll share; Exo remembers your choice. A window sends only its app's sound, as Discord does; a screen sends everything your PC plays except Exo and the call. While live, the button shows your stream with its real resolution, frame rate and bit rate, and Stop Streaming. Exo Capture captures it and the graphics card encodes it (no yellow border, cursor shown, a steady bit rate for Discord), and the picture goes straight to Discord without passing through Exo's page, so it is sharper and lighter than before.
  • No lost video. A lost video packet is asked for again and Discord resends it; only when it can't be filled does Exo ask for a fresh keyframe, so a stream doesn't freeze or smear on a shaky connection. Your own stream resends what Discord missed and makes a keyframe when someone starts watching.
  • Better call sound. Your voice goes out at the channel's own bitrate (up to 384 kbps on boosted servers) instead of a fixed 64 kbps, and a lost voice packet is rebuilt from the next one (Opus FEC) or smoothly concealed, instead of a click.
  • Friends without the extra Message. The More menu on a friend no longer repeats the Message button beside it.
  • Server Settings, the whole of it. Where you have the permission, the server menu opens Discord's Server Settings in its own groups: Server Profile (name, icon, banner on boosted servers, description), Engagement (system messages and their switches, the inactive channel and time-out, default notifications), Emoji and Stickers (upload, rename or edit, delete, slots left), Members, Roles, Invites (who made each, uses, expiry, revoke), Integrations (webhooks and apps, remove), Safety Setup (verification level, sensitive content filter, the 2FA requirement shown), AutoMod (Discord's five rule kinds: custom words, mention spam, suspected spam, commonly flagged words and member names, each with its actions and exceptions, on or off), the Audit Log (filter by person or action, what changed and why, load older) and Bans (search, the reason, Revoke Ban). Each section shows only with the permission Discord asks for, and changes wait for Save Changes as in Discord.
  • The role editor. Create Role, drag roles to reorder them, and edit one: name, color (Discord's swatches or your own), Display separately, Allow anyone to @mention, every permission in Discord's groups with Discord's descriptions (searchable, Clear permissions), and Manage Members (who has it, remove, Add Members). Each role shows its member count; roles above yours stay locked, as in Discord.
  • Edit Channel. A channel's right-click menu has Edit Channel: name, topic, slowmode, age-restricted, a voice channel's bitrate and user limit, and Permissions per role or member, each one allowed, left to the roles, or denied, as Discord shows them; Delete Channel asks first.
  • Right-click anyone. A name or picture in chat, the member list, a call, the Members page, Direct messages or the Audit Log opens Discord's person menu: Profile, Mention, Message, and on a server what you may do to them: Change Nickname, Server Mute, Server Deafen, Disconnect and Move to while they're in voice, Timeout (60 seconds to a week, or remove it), Kick and Ban (with a reason, and how much of their message history to delete), and their Roles to tick on or off; then Copy Username and Copy User ID. Only what your roles allow, and only for people below you. Clicking someone in the member list opens their profile card.
  • Moderating messages. With Manage Messages you can delete anyone's message; Pin Message shows only where you may pin. One message's menu is open at a time: pressing elsewhere closes it.
  • A Discord crash fixed. The build tool turned a number in Discord's date math into nothing, which crashed the room; that math now lives in one place that builds right, and a check stops it coming back.
  • Discord remembers where you were. The channel you had open in each server, and the server or DMs you were in, come back when Exo starts again, as in Discord.
  • Pictures at full quality. Profile pictures, server icons and banners load lossless at twice the size they show (they were small compressed copies).
  • Emoji stay sharp. The Apple emoji style is gone: its pictures topped out at 64 px and looked soft. Windows' own emoji draw at any size.
  • Stickers like Discord's tab. Every sticker shows its name, under its server's name and icon: this server's first, then Discord's own packs (Wumpus and friends), then your other servers' (locked without Nitro, as in Discord). Animated stickers play, in the picker and in chat, at full quality; a sticker in chat shows its name under the pointer. Stickers work in DMs too.
  • Polls. Polls in chat show as Discord's: pick an answer (or several), Vote, see each answer's share fill in with your vote marked, Remove Vote, and End Poll on your own. The + beside the message box has Create Poll (a question, up to ten answers, how long it runs, one answer or several). When a poll ends, its line says who won.
  • Voice messages play. A voice message plays with its waveform filling as it goes (click it to jump) and the time left; other sound files get a small player.
  • Soundboard. In a call, the Soundboard holds Discord's sounds and your server's: press one and everyone in the channel hears it; sounds others play are heard here too (not while deafened).
  • Replies read right. The quote over a reply shows mentions as names and emoji as emoji, instead of codes like <@768…>.
  • Every name readable. A role color too dark for Exo's black (a near-black bot role) is lifted just enough to read; other colors are the role's own.
  • Delete asks first. Delete Message shows the message and asks before it goes, as Discord does; hold Shift to skip the question.
  • Full profiles one click away. On a profile card, click the picture or the name for the full profile; the View Full Profile link is gone.
  • Smooth GIFs. The GIF picker played every GIF at once (each one a video); only the ones in view load and play now, and held-still ones still show their first frame.
  • The message box lines up. It is one of the room's floating boxes now: as wide as the chat, its foot and top on the same lines as your user bar beside it, with who is typing floating above it.
  • Profiles fit. A profile's banner keeps Discord's shape, or is a short strip of their color when they have none; the full profile fits its content instead of a fixed tall box; the profile beside a DM floats like the member list. Click someone's picture or name there for their full profile (no more View Full Profile button).
  • Escaped text reads right. Text Discord escapes, like Kunai\. in a link preview, shows as written, without the backslash.
  • Calls look like FaceTime. People in a call are 16:9 tiles as large as fits, each lit by their own picture blurred behind them, with a ring while they speak; the controls float in one capsule on the same line as your user bar. A call alone no longer fills the whole room with one huge tile.
  • One place for Discord's settings. Exo's Settings no longer has a Discord row; Discord's settings open from the gear in your Discord bar. The section is now just Music.
  • Exo Voice. Two input profiles now: Exo Voice, everything Exo has tuned together (DeepFilterNet noise suppression with its post-filter for the noise left under speech, echo cancellation, Voice Focus, automatic sensitivity), and Custom for every dial yourself. Voice Isolation and Studio become Exo Voice. Sound that reaches Exo late after a stall (a heavy game) is dropped instead of sent late, so the call stays live and noise suppression never falls behind.
  • Calls no longer turn your game down. Exo's Discord calls opened the microphone the way Windows treats as a call, which let Windows turn every other sound down by 80% while you talked. Exo now opts out of that; Discord's own attenuation in Voice & Video, your choice, is the only one.
  • Voice Tune. In Voice & Video (Exo Voice), about 45 seconds, guided step by step: a moment of quiet, a passage read normally, a few words loud and a few soft, then typing and clicking while quiet. Exo hears your room (its noise and any mains hum), your voice (its pitch and level, soft to loud) and your mic (its level, whether it clips, whether it only carries up to 8 kHz like a Bluetooth headset), then tunes Exo Voice for that voice on that mic: the low cut (lower for a deep voice, so it keeps its weight), the gate's floor just under your soft voice, how hard noise suppression cuts (lighter in a quiet room, where it sounds more natural), a filter for any hum, and the level, set at the mic itself when Windows' input level is the mic's own hardware gain. Play your reading back before and after first, then keep it; it's saved for that mic. It also knows your microphone (about 90 popular mics, XLR interfaces, mixers and headsets by their USB id or name; Bluetooth and built-in mics by how they connect) and says exactly what to set in its own app: what the device does better (its gain knob, clip protection, a hardware low cut) and what to turn off so it doesn't work twice (built-in noise cancelling, auto gain). Only where a device's noise removal is as good as Exo's (NVIDIA Broadcast, on the GPU) does Exo Voice's own step aside. In a call you're muted while it listens. It also says when Windows has the mic muted, and can stop Windows turning your game down during calls. It never changes the tone of your voice: a natural voice differs from the average as much as a coloured mic does, so a correction would change good voices too.
  • Voice Tune spots double processing. When another app already processes your microphone before Exo hears it (Lucid Voice, or Equalizer APO on its own), Voice & Video says so under Voice Tune and asks you to turn it off for that mic, since Exo Voice would gate, compress and de-ess your voice a second time.
  • Sharp "s" sounds softened. Exo Voice turns down only a sustained, sharp "s" by 3 to 4 dB; "t", "k", "ch", "sh" and vowels are never touched, and it adds no delay.
  • Voice Check. In Voice & Video, record a few seconds of yourself and play it back before and after, exactly as the call sends you, with your mic's peak, the room's noise before and after, and your voice's level. A mic that clips or is too quiet is named, and one press sets Microphone Volume right. Nothing is kept.
  • Steadier voice on a bad connection. Discord reports how much of your voice reaches it; Exo now follows that, adding more recovery data to each packet the moment packets go missing and giving those bits back to your voice when the line is clean (instead of a fixed 5%).
  • Voice Focus: only your voice gets through. After noise suppression, Exo's own voice stage cuts what suppression lets by: a high-pass for desk bumps, rumble and hum; a gate that listens only where voice lives and opens only for sound that lasts like speech, so keyboard and mouse clicks and knocks never open it, with a look-ahead so no word's start is cut; clicks between words ducked; a gentle leveler and a −1 dB limiter so you sound the same loud or soft. On speech mixed with typing, clicks, knocks, a fan and hum it kept the voice within about 1 dB, cut clicks in silence by about 70 dB (suppression alone: about 20), clicks between words by about 34 dB, and fan and hum in pauses by about 26 dB; three seconds of typing alone sent nothing. It costs about 0.02 ms per 20 ms of sound. Part of Exo Voice. The microphone is also coded as mono at Opus's best complexity, so every bit goes to your voice.
  • Your stream beside the call, not over it. While you Go Live, your stream is a tile among the people in the call, with Hide Preview and Stop Streaming on it; the Share button's menu shows or hides that preview.
  • Go Live keeps its frame rate, exactly. The stream runs on its own clock: a frame exactly every 1/30 or 1/60 s, each the newest picture, whether the screen is still or the monitor runs at 144 Hz, instead of drifting between 48 and 59.
  • The message box is centered. "Message #chat" and what you type sit in the middle of the box instead of a little high.
  • Settings use the whole window. Discord's settings pages fill the width, as Exo's Settings do.
  • Your preview plays smoothly. Your stream's tile plays the very frames your viewers get, at the stream's own rate, instead of a still picture twice a second.
  • More room for a sharp stream. Go Live may now use about 14 Mbit/s at 1080p 60 (Discord's own reference is 9) and up to 25 at higher sizes, and the encoder no longer bursts to four times that when a still screen starts moving and then starves to pay it back. Your tile shows that ceiling ("up to 14.4 Mbps"): a still picture is already perfect at almost nothing, so what it sends then says nothing about quality.
  • Threads and search behave like Discord. A thread opens inside its channel with a breadcrumb back, notifications land on the thread, and Search, Pinned and Inbox have Jump, which opens the message in its conversation with Jump to Present. Search covers the whole server on Enter.
  • Every emoji, Discord's way. The emoji picker has every emoji by category with search and each server by its icon, : suggests them as you type, and :smile: is sent as the emoji. Mentions read as names in the box and open profiles in messages, and role mentions show the role's name and color.
  • Sending feels instant and safe. Files show with upload progress the moment you send, drafts stay with each conversation, edits happen in place, and leaving a server or group, removing a friend or deleting a channel now asks first. Sensitive media follows your Content & Social filters.
  • No more reconnect loops. Exo stops asking Discord about people who aren't in a server (webhooks, people who left), keeps under Discord's rate limit, and waits a minute after Discord closes the connection for asking too fast.
  • Server Settings keep unsaved changes the way Discord does. Changing section, closing or Escape shakes the save bar red instead of throwing your changes away, and Safety Setup waits for Save Changes. A server name must be 2 to 100 characters, said under Name.
  • The person menu, fixed. Roles, Move to and Timeout open on a click and stay open on the way across. Arrow keys move through the menu, quick role ticks no longer undo each other, and Remove Timeout shows only for someone timed out. Bot and Server Booster roles save their color, display and permissions.
  • Members on every server. Members is in Server Settings everywhere and searches and pages through all members. Prune needs Kick Members and Manage Server, Bans need Ban Members and go past 1,000, and stage events are created as stage events. Errors show under the control that failed.
  • DM calls like Discord. A DM's call shows its stage above the conversation, with the call bar, camera, Share Your Screen and Soundboard; Decline tells Discord to stop ringing in your other apps too, and ringing follows Discord's own updates.
  • Voice that says why. Voice Check and Voice Tune play back exactly what the call sends; Voice Tune can be canceled and can forget a mic's tuning; a full channel, a join that never connects, a stream that doesn't start and a camera that won't open each say why.
  • Discord rests in the background. Video, previews and the mic meter rest while Exo isn't in use; a headset unplugged mid-call switches to Windows' device; a dropped or moved voice server no longer ends the call or leaves it hanging.
  • Notifications follow your settings. Exo now notifies as each server and channel is set in Discord (all messages, only mentions or nothing), and respects a server's choice to ignore @everyone and role mentions.
  • Lighter on Discord. Reading a busy channel tells Discord once, a few seconds later, instead of once a message; a server's Interested events are one request; voice changes in a busy server no longer reload your server list or friends.
  • Pins, events and files. Pins load from Discord's current list, Download ICS file never overwrites a file of the same name, and a file too big for your account says so.
  • Try Again when Discord drops. If the connection to Discord stops, the room offers Try Again instead of only saying so.
  • Server Settings hold up. A page that fails to reload keeps what it had and offers Try Again; Delete, Create Role, Add Members, Revoke Ban and the like can't fire twice; Remove from a role takes only that role; Add Members waits for you to stop typing and stops at 30; the Audit Log never mixes two filters; AutoMod's mention limit can be cleared and retyped.
  • Settings that stick. A profile you just saved no longer comes back as unsaved changes, two account switches flipped quickly no longer undo each other, two quick favorite GIFs are both kept, and a privacy change Discord refuses says so.
  • Calls hold steady under load. Exo's voice no longer allocates on its audio threads, closes a call, Voice Check or the mic meter at once, and lets go of your microphone and speakers when it's done. Noise suppression loads in the background.
  • No more silent calls. A speaker who reconnects keeps being heard, a call whose voice link never finished connecting tries again, and a speaker leaving can no longer crash the call. Microphone Volume above 100% no longer clips.
  • Video recovers by itself. A camera or stream that loses a frame asks for a fresh picture until one comes, without flooding the sender, and your own camera no longer smears for viewers when Exo falls behind.
  • Deafen means everything. Deafen silences a Go Live you're watching, a new speaker choice moves the stream's sound too, and a call that rang before you opened Discord shows when you do.
  • Private channels stay listed. In servers that list only the channels you follow, private channels you can see (moderator chats) show again, as in Discord.
  • Discord remembers where you were. A dropped connection no longer sends you back to the server Exo started on; the server, channel and open thread stay. Ctrl+K takes you to a DM or channel even with Discord's settings open, and finds channels too.
  • Every channel opens. Voice and stage channels have their text chat beside the call, and forums list every post, archived ones included, with tags, previews and New Post.
  • More of Discord's chat. Forward, Mark Unread, Create Thread and Copy Message Link in the message menu; forwarded messages, command replies and threads under messages show as in Discord; replies can skip the ping; a new-messages bar and slowmode; the bell mutes for a while and sets what a channel notifies you of, and timed mutes end on time. Chat spacing follows one even rhythm.
  • Moderator menus on everyone. Timeout, Kick, Ban and nickname show on the first open even for people Exo hadn't seen yet, and sit under More on their profile card and full profile.
  • Full profiles. The profile card, the panel beside a DM and the full profile show everything Discord does: banner, decoration, badges, activity, About Me, Member Since, roles, connections, your note, and mutual friends and servers you can press. Badges say what they are on hover.
  • Members and roles stay loaded. A server update or a reconnect no longer leaves a server's member list empty; deleted roles no longer linger.
  • Change your banner. Discord settings > Profiles has Change Banner and Remove Banner with Nitro; without it, your banner color is what people see.
  • Voice Check plays back. Play Before and Play After work again in Voice Check and Voice Tune; the page blocked the take.
  • Lucid Voice turned off is off. Voice & Video no longer says Lucid Voice processes your mic once you turn it off in Lucid, and checks again when you come back to Exo.
  • See Exo Voice work. Voice & Video shows Exo Voice live: your mic's level, what is sent, and every stage running, with the tuning in use. Voice Tune's gate now fits a quiet mic; run it again to apply.
  • Spotify links just work. Paste any Jam invite (short spotify.link links included) into Jam and you're in, and a Spotify link sent in Discord opens in Exo's Music room: a Jam joins, a song plays, an album, playlist, artist or show opens.
  • Spotify, the whole app. Create playlists and folders, pin, rename, move and delete from Your library; Edit details with a new cover, public or private, collaborative and Download; song and artist radio, credits and copy link from any song.
  • Spotify pages like Spotify's. An artist's full discography, tour dates, Featuring, Appears on, Discovered on and About; album release facts; episode dates, minutes left and Mark as played; Liked Songs' genre and mood chips; audiobooks; playback speed; Spotify's keyboard shortcuts; Jam by link or QR with the host's switches. Sign-in finishes in your browser and Spotify's window never shows.
  • YouTube Music, the whole app. Explore with New releases, Charts and Moods & genres, search filters, full artist pages, Start mix and Shuffle play, Play next and Add to queue, an editable Up next with Autoplay, thumbs down, the Song/Video switch, Related, and its keyboard keys.
  • Your playlists everywhere. One Edit details sheet for every service (name, description, cover on Spotify, privacy on YouTube Music), Move up and down, and Delete with a confirm; the library adds YouTube's Subscriptions, Podcasts, Uploads and History.
  • One way each service is set up. Audio quality, Autoplay, keyboard shortcuts and the needs-a-subscription screen work the same in Spotify, Apple Music and YouTube Music, each at that service's best.
  • Apple Music, all of it. New and Radio (live Apple Music 1, Hits and Country, stations and top charts), search by kind, and a library with Recently added, Made for you, Music videos and Apple's own sort orders.
  • Your Apple Music playlists in Exo. Make, rename, describe, reorder and delete them; from any song, Suggest less, Add to library, Create station, View credits and Copy link.
  • Apple Music pages and Up Next. Artist pages carry the station, every shelf and About; album pages show their formats, release facts and more from the artist; Up Next has AutoPlay, Playing next and History; lyrics light word by word. It plays at the web player's best, AAC 256 kbps.
  • The player holds still. Music's floating player keeps one size and place: opening Now playing, Queue or Sound no longer squeezes and moves it, the panels float above it at a roomier width, and Lossless sits under the artist instead of eating the title. Lyrics are bigger and bolder.
  • Sound settings checked. AutoMix and the rest are Spotify's own settings, read back after each change (AutoMix only applies to playlists Spotify allows it on).
  • Ready when you are. The music service you chose starts with Exo, hidden, instead of when Music first opens: Spotify was ready about 5 seconds after launch instead of up to a minute. An idle player is frozen, so it costs memory, not processor time. While the first page loads, Music says so instead of showing black.
  • Sound. A new panel in the player bar.
