On Spotify: audio quality up to Lossless (a Lossless badge shows by the song), exclusive mode for bit-perfect sound, crossfade and its length, gapless, AutoMix, Smart Shuffle, volume normalization, mono audio and Spotify's six-band equalizer, all Spotify's own settings. For every service: a sleep timer.
  • Your queue, editable. Play next in every song's menu, and the songs you queued can be moved up or down, removed, or cleared.
  • Apple Music stays on the web. The Windows Apple Music app can't be controlled from outside (no way to reach its library, queue or quality), so lossless on Apple Music isn't possible in Exo yet; the web player stays.
  • Cider is no longer a music choice. Apple Music plays in Exo's own room through Apple's web player, so Cider isn't needed; if you had chosen Cider, Exo moves you to Apple Music.
  • The top bar's cover stays put, and is always there. The mini player keeps the song's cover the whole song, and the next song's cover fades in over the last only once it has loaded, never over black. Covers no longer go missing: Spotify's 640 px picture was downloaded but then dropped while being prepared, and a song restored paused now finds its cover from Spotify too.
  • The song keeps its size. Skipping songs no longer makes the mini player grow and shrink with each title.
  • The top bar holds still. One row of evenly spaced capsules: Search, the call (or a download), the rooms, the song, the mouse, then Tools. The room tabs never move; each live capsule slides down into its own place when it starts and back up when it ends. The song is shorter: its line alone, no numbers.
  • The call, from anywhere. Outside Discord, the call's capsule shows the server's icon, the channel over the server, the faces of who is in it (a green ring while someone speaks), LIVE while you stream, how long the call has run, and mute, deafen and leave.
  • AutoMix everywhere, and Smart Shuffle that works. Spotify's AutoMix only mixes playlists it picks; turning it on in Exo now also crossfades every other song, so every change is smooth. The Smart Shuffle switch now really turns Smart Shuffle on or off for what is playing (it was only Spotify's "list it as a play mode" setting); where Spotify doesn't offer it (playlists you don't own), it says so.
  • Discord's server list lines up. The Direct messages logo's top meets the top of the channel panel beside it.
  • Your mouse's profile in the top bar. A capsule right of the song shows the mouse profile on now (from Exo Mouse; the mouse's name when no saved profile matches) and its battery, red when low. It opens Devices.
  • Settings look like Discord's. A plain list of sections, and each section a title over rows on the black, split by hairlines and using the window's width, as in Discord's settings.
  • Lyrics keep time, and Music keeps your place. Lyrics follow the song, a page that fails says so instead of showing the last one, coming back to Music keeps the page you were on, and song menus say when something didn't work.
  • More of each service. YouTube Music can save an album and follow an artist; on Apple Music, clicking a song in the queue plays it and Play next works.
  • Spotify when it was open first. The room works when Spotify was already running before Exo, Home loads after Exo's theme rewrite, and hidden players rest instead of working in the background.
  • Music says when it didn't work. A play, skip, shuffle, repeat or like the service turns down reads in red on the player strip, and so do failed queue edits and Ctrl+K's music commands.
  • Apple Music wakes on the way in. Pointing at the mini player or the Music tab wakes a sleeping Apple Music page, as it already did for YouTube Music. Spotify covers that couldn't be found are tried again a minute later.
  • Reduce motion means Reduce motion. Exo's own Reduce motion setting now also stops the top bar capsules' slide, the Ctrl+K palette's scale and the loading spinners. Pressing Ctrl+K again closes the palette with its fade.
  • Music comes back by itself. When Spotify was closed or lost Exo's connection (it handed itself to an older Spotify still closing), Music said to close Spotify and Open again did nothing; Exo now starts it again on its own.
  • One size for every tab bar and toolbar. The Store, System and Discord's Friends and Shop headers use the same filled tab group (28px tabs in a 32px pill), and the Store's search, refresh and arrows match Exo's 32px controls.
  • NVIDIA updates wait for the game. A driver update waits until you stop playing, and Exo checks for one at most once a day.
  • Display settings that tell the truth. GPU > Display no longer hangs, Best only reports a color the screen took, Default color resets, and a scaling error says why.
  • Display changes you can trust. A refresh rate on trial is saved only once you keep it, a revert puts things back in the right order, and HDR and your calibration profile come back after one.
  • System never waits on Windows. Shader status, the DLSS indicator's administrator prompt and the upscaler folder check run off Exo's main thread, and Exo Shaders and Exo Capture start once even when two parts of Exo ask for them together.
  • Shaders tells you how long. Before you press Compile, the whole queue's time and finish show, from how fast this PC compiled each game before; while compiling, the time left follows the live rate.
  • Drag games into the order you want. Drag a poster to the front, or move it with Ctrl and the arrow keys, and Shaders compiles it first. The compiling poster is never cut off, and compiling keeps full speed in other rooms.
  • Hotfix drivers are found again. System > Graphics offers NVIDIA's newest GeForce hotfix when it's newer than Game Ready, including ones NVIDIA announces only on its forums.
  • Captures holds still. The page stays where it is, shows whether the add-on and instant replay are on, Esc keeps your snip edits, and trims work from the keyboard.
  • Captures, even when empty. With nothing saved yet, Captures shows the three ways to capture (Save replay, Record, Snip) with their keys, one press away, instead of one line of text.
  • Snip says how. The snip screen says how it works at the top of the screen you're on (drag, click a window, Enter for the whole screen, Esc to cancel), and the selection's edge stays visible over bright pictures.
  • Exo Capture stays installed. Updating Exo no longer removes the Capture add-on.
  • Tests on the page. The polling test and the click test sit beside your mouse's settings, ready to use: click the pad and move the mouse in circles for five seconds to see the average, peak, lowest 5%, stability and dropouts against what is set on the mouse; click the other pad to count each button's clicks and catch a switch that bounces. Nothing on the page moves while a test runs.
  • No empty space. Settings panels keep their own height and a wide window uses another column instead of an outsized picture, so the room fills the window.
  • Steam stops asking which account. Add account in Settings clears Steam's automatic sign-in so it can show its own; if no other account signs in, Steam now goes back to signing you in as before instead of asking at its next start.
  • Your Discord roles stick. When Discord sent a server after sign-in, Exo didn't know your roles there, so the moderator menu (Timeout, Kick, Ban, Roles) and role-only channels disappeared. Exo now takes your roles from your member wherever Discord sends it. And Roles, Timeout and Apps stay open while you move into them: crossing the menu's edge or another row on the way no longer closes them at once.
  • Driver installs finish cleanly. After a new NVIDIA driver goes in, Exo connects to it again before putting your profile and color back (they failed against the removed driver), System says the driver is being installed instead of showing read errors, and Exo no longer promises a Windows permission prompt that may not come.
  • Exo opens lighter. Discord's sticker player, its settings, Server Settings and Edit Channel load when first used, and the top bar no longer pulls the whole Discord client into startup: the script Exo needs before its first paint fell from about 1.6 MB to under 700 KB.
  • A crashed window says so. If the WebView2 browser behind Exo's window stops, Exo says its window stopped and offers Restart, instead of a message about installing WebView2.
  • Notices when you're away. A download or update that finishes, fails or is canceled while Exo is in the background shows as a Windows notification; never while a game runs. The downloads queue floats in every room again.
  • Music's floating player. Music's player floats over the page as one capsule, and Your library's filters and sort are one menu.
  • Pages open while Exo is in the background. A room opened while another window had focus could stay blank until you clicked Exo; only spinners and other loops pause in the background now.
  • Music opens without an error. Right after Exo starts, Spotify could answer Home with an error. Exo now remembers the query Spotify's Home needs for your Spotify version, finds it faster when it must, and asks again for a few seconds before saying anything.
  • Music opens without a sign-in flash. If you were signed in last time, Music says Opening while the service starts instead of briefly asking you to sign in.
  • Room shortcuts work everywhere. Ctrl+1 to Ctrl+5 and Ctrl+, now switch rooms while you're typing too, so they work from Discord's message box.

3.9.0 — October 17, 2026

This update makes Exo's own Discord client look and work like Discord, with end-to-end encrypted voice calls, noise suppression, friends, notifications, files, GIFs, emoji and reactions.

  • Laid out like Discord. Servers down the far left with their names on hover, Direct messages on top and Add a Server at the bottom; a server's channels with folding categories, channel icons, unread marks and who is in each voice channel; your DMs with a search and Friends; you at the bottom with mute, deafen, settings and your status. The conversation has Discord's header (call, pinned messages, member list, search, Inbox), the member list down the right grouped by role in each role's color, names in their role color, date lines, Discord's red NEW line, "Today at" times, and the hover bar (react, reply, edit, More), also on right-click. In Exo's darker palette and Inter.
  • Voice calls. Click a voice channel to join it, or Call in a DM (it rings the other person); an incoming call shows a Join bar and a Windows notification. Calls are end-to-end encrypted with Discord's own DAVE library and connect in under a second; the sound goes straight between Windows and Discord's voice server. Video and screen share stay in the Discord app for now. Joining, the encryption and leaving _Verified: on screen._ Hearing and being heard
  • Noise suppression in calls. DeepFilterNet 3 cleans keyboards, fans and room noise out of your microphone on this PC before anything is sent, faster than real time. It downloads once, checked against its pinned fingerprint, the first time the client starts; turn it off in Settings > Music and Discord.
  • Friends and status. Friends shows who is online and what they're doing, all your friends and pending requests with Accept and Ignore, and Add Friend by username; a profile card opens from any name or avatar with Message on it. Pick Online, Idle, Do Not Disturb or Invisible from your name.
  • Everything you do in a chat. Send, reply (the quote jumps to the original), edit, delete, react, pin, copy text or a message link; attach files with +, paste a screenshot or drop files (up to 25 MB, 10 at a time); Discord's GIF picker; stickers; every server's emoji, with ones Discord would only allow with Nitro sent as their picture; :name: and @name suggestions; search; pinned messages; pictures open full size. Forum channels list their posts, and a post opens like a channel. Join a server with an invite and leave one from its menu.
  • Notifications and the unread badge. A DM or a mention while you're not looking at that conversation shows as a Windows notification; clicking it opens the conversation. Muted servers and channels stay quiet. Your unread count shows on the Discord tab and Exo's taskbar button.

3.8.0 — October 17, 2026

This update adds Exo's own Discord client as a preview, NVIDIA's newest driver installed clean and driver only, NVIDIA Control Panel's display settings in GPU > Display, SDR that looks right while HDR is on, and a new opening that has everything of Exo's own ready before the library shows.

  • A new opening. Exo opens on black, its mark settles in at full size and its three bars sweep in one after another, then it holds while Exo loads Store, Devices, Settings, the GPU sheet with every tab and Exo Shaders' page, and the first screen of covers, and sinks back as the library comes up. It always plays in full, once, at your screen's own frame rate; no text or flicker before it, and no loading line in the top bar. Nothing of Exo's own builds on first open: the GPU sheet opens in a frame. Discord and music still start with their rooms.
  • Exo Shaders is half the size. Its engine and command-line tools share one copy of .NET instead of two: about 119 MB on disk from 234 MB.
  • Exo Shaders is ready when you are. It starts with Exo again, like Exo Capture and Exo Mouse, so its page opens at once and it sees every game you play.
  • Exo's menus show over Discord and music apps. The Capture menu (and every other menu) opened over Discord's or a music app's window was hidden behind it; that window now gives way under any open menu.
  • Store pages open whole. Games on screen in the Store are read ahead in the background, a few at a time, with their art and other stores, so a page opens with its tags, description and buttons already there.
  • The NVIDIA driver, driver only. GPU > Profile shows your driver and NVIDIA's newest Game Ready driver for your card. Update (or Reinstall) downloads it from NVIDIA, checks NVIDIA's signature, and clean-installs the driver alone: no NVIDIA app, telemetry, ShadowPlay, PhysX or HDMI/DisplayPort audio. Exo puts your GPU profile and desktop color back afterwards and waits while a game runs. Your display settings (scaling, override, output color) stay as they were.
  • Display has NVIDIA Control Panel's settings. Output color is Default (the driver picks) or NVIDIA settings with format, depth and range; Scaling is Aspect ratio, Full-screen, No scaling or Integer scaling, done on the GPU or the display, with Override the scaling set by games; brightness, contrast, gamma, digital vibrance and hue are sliders (moving one hands color to NVIDIA, Restore hands it back); RTX Video Super Resolution (Off, Auto, Quality 1-4) and RTX Video HDR where the card has them. Each change to the picture still waits 15 seconds for you to keep it. The GPU sheet is larger and Display is in two columns, so nothing scrolls.
  • Exo's own Discord client (preview, off by default). Turn it on in Settings > Music and Discord and the Discord room becomes Exo's own client: your servers and DMs down the left, unread and mention marks, channels in Discord's order, and messages with formatting, emoji, images, videos, link previews and stickers. Send, reply, edit and delete your own messages; Exo marks what you read and shows who is typing. Sign in by scanning a QR code with Discord's phone app, so Exo never sees your password; the sign-in stays on this PC, encrypted for your Windows account, and Sign out ends it. Only channels your roles let you see are shown, and nothing is saved. Calls, video and screen share stay in the Discord app for now. Discord's terms don't allow other clients, so it's at your own risk.
  • SDR looks right in HDR. While a screen is in HDR, Exo gives it a color profile that draws the desktop, overlays and SDR windows with gamma 2.2 like an SDR screen, so blacks stay black instead of gray; HDR content above SDR white is untouched. The screen's own profile comes back when HDR goes off. On by default in GPU > HDR.
  • One kind of control. Every setting with choices is a dropdown, however many it has; on and off is a switch, an amount is a slider.
  • Everything on one line. Settings' section headings and their buttons line up with the rows under them, and every row is the same height.
  • About lights up in Settings' list when you reach the bottom.
  • Exo Shaders' info window opens whole, never on a spinner first.
  • No scrollbars anywhere, including the GPU and Captures sheets, where one flashed while switching tabs. Everything still scrolls.
  • Exo Shaders' cache and about are behind an "i" button in its toolbar, in one window; the gray line at the bottom of the page is gone.
  • "Color" everywhere, in US spelling.
  • Time played is just the time. No "From tracker.gg" or other source note under it.

3.7.0 — October 17, 2026

This update adds the GPU room with Exo's own NVIDIA profile for your card, HDR only for games that really use it, a lighter Exo that starts nothing it doesn't need, and Inter as Exo's typeface.

  • One GPU button. Library's Upscalers button is now GPU, with Profile, Display, HDR, Upscalers and Shaders in one place. Settings > Graphics is gone.
  • Exo's profile for your card. Use Exo's profile writes the best NVIDIA settings for your card's series (GTX 10 and 16, RTX 20 to 50, and workstation cards by generation) for the lowest latency and most performance: maximum performance power, low latency on, High performance texture filtering, the newest DLSS files for every game, and the best DLSS model for your card (Recommended on RTX 40 and 50, K on RTX 20 and 30, frame generation C on all). Pick G-SYNC or No G-SYNC; with G-SYNC the frame cap follows your refresh rate, even when it changes. Frame generation switches stay yours. Undo puts your old settings back.
  • Import a Profile Inspector file from the Profile tab, as before.
  • Display shows only what your screen takes. Color format, depth and range offered only where the screen supports them, the best one picked for you, and every change kept only if you confirm within 15 seconds. NVIDIA color settings (brightness, contrast, gamma, digital vibrance and hue) sit beside them.
  • Exo Shaders on one page, with its shader cache and queue in their own dialogs.
  • HDR only for games that use it. Exo checks your screen first: how bright it really gets and whether Windows HDR is calibrated. Then it turns HDR on only while a game that supports HDR runs (from PCGamingWiki), and puts your desktop back after. Each game page has Auto, Always or Never.
  • Snips open the editor straight away, and you can delete captures from the grid.
  • Game, Discord voice and microphone are always kept on their own tracks, with the mix as the first track, so any player plays it.
  • Smarter encoder choice on NVIDIA, AMD and Intel cards, and HDR snips stay HDR.
  • Nothing starts with Exo just to be ready. Steam is no longer started to check what you own: games Exo confirmed before stay yours, and Steam starts when you play, install, update, verify or switch accounts. Discord starts when you open its room, Music when you open Music, and Exo Shaders when you open its page or a driver update leaves games to rebuild. A Discord or music app you already have open is still picked up at once. On this PC, with Steam closed, Exo and what it starts went from about 1.4 GB of memory at rest to about 0.6 GB. Discord, Music and Exo Shaders _Verified: on screen._ Steam staying closed
  • Idle music lets go. A player that has slept for 15 minutes with nothing playing is closed (the web player, or a music app Exo started; never one you opened), and opens again with Music. Exo Shaders stops once it has nothing to do and its page is closed.
  • Trailers stop off screen. A Store trailer stops and lets go of its video when you leave the Store or minimize Exo, and starts again when you come back.
  • Smaller and quieter. The installer is about a quarter smaller (64.6 MB from 87.6 MB), Exo uses about 30 MB less memory sitting on Library, and it does about a quarter of the background work while idle: one check for games started outside Exo instead of three every five seconds.
  • Inter everywhere. Exo, Exo Mouse and Exo Capture's windows use Inter in place of Geist.
  • tracker.gg is only for Riot games. Rainbow Six Siege and Destiny 2 hours come from Steam.
  • Optimize is always on. The Optimize rows are gone; the fixes they applied stay applied.

3.6.0 — October 17, 2026

This update adds Exo Capture for instant replay, recording and snips, your game's NVIDIA graphics settings, more than one account per store, and hours only from where they are really kept.

  • Exo Capture. The Capture button in the top bar saves an instant replay, starts or stops a recording, or takes a snip: drag a rectangle, then copy or save it from the card that appears. Hotkeys do the same. Instant replay runs only while a game is running, by default, and holds the last minutes in memory until you save.
  • Captures sheet. Every replay, recording and snip, by game, with a player, trim, favorites, Send to Discord (copies the file for you to paste), and a storage limit that never deletes favorites. Trim _Checked in code only._ The rest
  • Settings > Capture, beside Graphics: frame rate (60 fps, or 120 where your screen runs that fast), quality, length of the replay, which sounds to keep (game, Discord voice, microphone, each on its own track), the folder and hotkeys. Video is encoded on the graphics card (HEVC, or AV1 where the card has it; HDR games stay HDR). HDR and AV1
  • Replay follows the game however you start it. A game started from Steam, a desktop shortcut or anywhere else turns replay on, not only games started from Exo.
  • No outline in your clips. Exo asks Windows to capture without the yellow border, where Windows allows it.
  • Light on games. Exo Capture reads Windows' capture frames directly instead of copying each one, takes no more frames than the clip keeps, and gives the game's frames first claim on the graphics card; the game keeps its own fast path to the screen. In Hell is Us at 1080p on an RTX 3070, with the card at full load, replay at 60 fps (the default) cost about 3% of average frame rate (213 to 207), and at 120 fps about 5%, down from 7%; Exo Capture used under 1% of the processor and about 150 MB of memory.
  • Games Windows cannot capture. A game in exclusive full screen gives Windows capture no picture; after five seconds without one, Exo Capture takes the frames from inside the game with OBS Studio's own signed game capture hook, injected the way OBS's anti-cheat compatible mode does. Never for Riot games, while FACEIT or ESEA runs, or when another app like OBS already captures that game. The hook itself _Verified: end-to-end._ The switch to it
  • Your game's NVIDIA profile on its page. A game's page shows the settings the NVIDIA driver holds for it (low latency, frame rate limit, V-Sync, texture filtering, DLSS overrides and more), each with Driver default to put NVIDIA's value back. Settings > Graphics holds the same for every game.
  • DLSS rows only where they apply. A DLSS row shows only on a game that ships that DLSS file.
  • Import a Profile Inspector file. Import profile reads a .nip file and lists what it would change before anything is written; Undo import puts back what it replaced.
  • AMD and Intel cards show what their driver reports, read-only.
  • Who you are signed in as. Settings > Game stores shows the Steam, Epic, GOG and Amazon accounts on this PC.
  • Switch Steam accounts in one click. Pick another account Steam remembers and Exo restarts Steam signed in as it.
  • More than one Epic account. Add an account, switch to it, or bring in one Epic already knows.
  • Hours from all your accounts. Turn on "Count hours from all my accounts" and a game you play on two accounts, like Rocket League on an alt, shows the hours from both. Rocket League's Epic hours _Verified: on screen._ Adding up two accounts
  • Only real hours. Hours come from the store or the game itself, never from Exo's own timing; a game with no real source shows none, rather than a guess. Hours can now also go down when the store says so, so a stale figure no longer sticks.
  • More sources. Steam and GOG first, then GOG Galaxy 2.0's database for games on platforms you connected to it, then tracker.gg, now for Rainbow Six Siege and Destiny 2 too, and Xbox with an OpenXBL key you paste. Each figure says where it came from.
  • Battle.net last played. Battle.net games show when you last played them.
  • Instant replay survives alt-tab. Minimizing a game no longer throws away the replay it was holding.
  • The capture storage limit only removes Exo's own captures, and sends them to the Recycle Bin. With the save folder set to Videos, it could permanently delete your other videos.
  • Saving an edited snip works for full-size screenshots. Pictures over about 750 KB never saved.
  • The capture quality slider shows where it is set, and has a focus ring.
  • Exo Mouse runs on its own address, so the Devices add-on can only reach the mouse functions Exo gives it, never the rest of Exo.
  • Mouse profiles are never wiped by a failed read, and a game profile the mouse only partly takes says so.
  • Accounts stay the account you chose. After an Epic or GOG switch or sign-out, the next sign-in no longer brings back the old account. Signing out of GOG no longer falls back to Heroic's account. Amazon shows only the signed-in account's games. Exo no longer spends Epic's sign-in tokens itself, which could sign you out of Legendary or the Epic Games Launcher.
  • Hours are counted once. A game on two stores no longer counts the same tracker.gg or GOG Galaxy figure twice after a background refresh. Hours from a mistyped Riot ID go away when you correct it. Steam hours stay when Steam is closed. GOG games get their last played date from GOG Galaxy.
  • Stop leaves your other programs alone. Stopping a game only ends processes from the game's own folder, never a browser or Discord it opened. EA's anti-cheat launcher and GameGuard are never ended.
  • Upscaler and Discord changes are safe against interruptions. A DLSS backup is verified before the game's file is replaced, and linked FSR files are put back together if one fails. DLSS changes wait while the game's store is updating it. Discord can no longer be left without its app files.
  • Smaller fixes. Queued badges show on cards owned on two stores, Store shows ™ titles as in your library, Ctrl+K no longer lists hidden extras, the Store home no longer hangs after a network drop, Riot banner art loads, and a broken download-queue file no longer stops Exo from starting.

3.5.5 — October 17, 2026

This update shows Fortnite as one game.

  • Fortnite is one card. The new Epic Games Launcher lists Fortnite's modes, like LEGO Fortnite Odyssey and Save the World, as their own installs with no name, so each showed as an extra card titled with a code. They now fold into Fortnite's card, and their hours count toward it. The same goes for any Epic game whose modes install this way.

3.5.4 — October 17, 2026

This update counts hours for Minecraft and Roblox, keeps Fortnite's hours moving, finds more of your owned Steam and Epic games, and lets Update all in Upscalers include anti-cheat games at your risk.

  • Every owned Steam game shows up. On a Steam install that has seen many store pages, Exo asked Steam about only the first 20,000 apps it knew of and gave up after four seconds, so owned games past that point never reached "owned, not installed". Exo now asks about every app in batches, keeps the answers it got if Steam slows down, and keeps games it verified before until Steam answers for them. With a Steam Web API key, an owned app Steam has no details for on this PC is shown once the Steam store says it is a game.
  • Games that share a name are both kept. Two games with the same title in one store, like Prey (2006) and Prey (2017), no longer collapse into one.
  • Every owned Epic game shows up. Exo now also lists Epic games another store delivers, such as ones activated on the EA app; Install opens Epic Games Launcher, which sends you there. Deluxe and other editions are no longer dropped as add-ons, and a large Epic library has five minutes to list on the first run instead of 90 seconds.
  • Hours for Minecraft and Roblox. Exo now sees Minecraft (Java and Bedrock) and Roblox while they run, even when you start them outside Exo, and counts their hours. Roblox's earlier sessions come from its own logs and Minecraft Java's from each singleplayer world's stats, once. Roblox _Verified: on screen._ Minecraft
  • Fortnite's hours keep going up. For Epic games Exo dropped its own session time whenever Epic reported any hours, so hours froze when Epic's figure did. Exo now adds the time it saw since Epic's last figure, without counting anything twice.
  • Hours no longer creep up on their own. A background refresh could add Exo's minutes again to totals that already held them, for Riot and other non-Steam games.
  • Real covers for games no store sells. osu! and other local games looked up their art on Steam by title, found nothing (or a different game) and fell back to their icon. With a SteamGridDB key saved, Exo now finds them on SteamGridDB by their exact title.
  • Update all can include anti-cheat games. In Upscalers, with anti-cheat games waiting, Update all first names each game and its anti-cheat and says the ban risk plainly, then updates them too at your risk, or only the others. Riot games are never changed.
  • Exo's own dropdowns. Every dropdown in Devices opens Exo's menu instead of Windows' list.
  • Each fact once. Under the mouse: battery, signal, a fixed polling rate and firmware; sensitivity and a polling rate you can change stay in their panels only.
  • Updates are not blocked by programs Exo started. A store launcher Exo opened kept Exo's folder in use after Exo closed, which could stop the next update from swapping it.

3.5.3 — October 17, 2026

This update redesigns Devices so every mouse's settings fit on one screen, in order, and fixes linking games to a profile.

  • Every setting on one screen, in order. Settings now sit in sections (Sensor, Buttons, Device, Profiles), whole and in the fewest even columns that fit without scrolling, so a mouse with many settings, like a Razer Viper V3 Pro, no longer spreads them across the room. Every setting a mouse can change still shows, its own extras included; the mouse picture takes the room that is left.
  • More at a glance. Under the mouse: battery, signal (or how it is connected when it reports no signal), polling rate, sensitivity with its stage, and firmware.
  • Games show up when linking a profile. Choosing Games on a profile opened the list inside the profile's card, so a short card showed no games at all; it now opens over the whole room.
  • Razer lift-off mode stays chosen. Choosing Asymmetric on a Razer mouse could fail without a word when the mouse held lift-off and landing values Razer's own software never writes; Exo now starts from the nearest valid pair, so the choice sticks.

3.5.2 — October 17, 2026

This update brings Razer mice into Devices.

  • Razer mice show up. A Razer mouse such as the Viper V3 Pro never appeared in Devices: Razer keeps its settings on the part of the mouse Windows reserves for moving the pointer, and Exo stayed away from it. Exo now reaches that part for the mouse's settings only, the way OpenMouse Bridge and Chrome do, while the pointer stays Windows' own and keyboards are never touched.

3.5.1 — October 17, 2026

This update closes the holes a security review of Exo found, keeps Exo further away from anti-cheat, and makes text sizes and hover effects match across the app.

  • Spotify can only be reached by Exo. Exo used to talk to the Spotify it hosts over a debug port on your PC, which another program running as you could find and use to drive your signed-in Spotify. Exo now talks to it over a private connection only Exo holds; there is no port to find.
  • The mouse page stays in its lane. Exo Mouse, which runs inside Devices, can reach only its own mouse, profile and game-list calls. A message it sends straight to Exo is now refused instead of reaching the rest of Exo.
  • Logs hide secrets that carry no label. Exo's log already hid tokens and passwords written next to a label; it now also hides sign-in tokens written on their own, while download checksums, links and file paths stay readable for support.
  • Every attack from the review is kept as a test. Dangerous links, requests aimed at your PC or network, paths that try to escape a folder, tampered updates and corrupt files are all refused, and each is now a test that runs on every change.
  • More anti-cheat Exo never closes. When Exo has to stop a game, it now leaves FACEIT, PunkBuster, XIGNCODE3, GameGuard, ACE, EA's anti-cheat, Riot's Vanguard tray and every game's own BattlEye or Easy Anti-Cheat launcher running, in the whole game process tree, not only the top process. A close it refuses is no longer counted as done.
  • A broken upscaler list can't get in the way. If the list of newer DLSS, FSR and XeSS versions arrives damaged, Exo keeps the versions it ships with.
  • One set of text sizes. Settings, game pages, Library shelves, first run, Upscalers and the Activate key popover now use Exo's three text sizes, so nothing stands a size apart.
  • Hovers never fade. The music track list and the command menu's Clear search now light up with Exo's gray hover instead of turning see-through, like every other control.
  • DLSS preset names fit. Ray Reconstruction presets D and F read on one line.

3.5.0 — October 16, 2026

This update makes Music Exo's own player for Spotify, Apple Music and YouTube Music, with Jam, Friend activity, lyrics and your whole song library; ships Exo Shaders inside Exo; adds a Library > Upscalers sheet with DLSS presets; rebuilds Devices around your mouse; speeds up the Store and Discord; gives memory back from Steam, Epic and other store apps sitting behind Exo; and moves every room the same way. Equicord with Exo's plugin set, FakeNitro included, is now always on for Discord.

  • One Exo player for Spotify, Apple Music and YouTube Music. The Music room is Exo's own player for all three: your library down the left, Home, search, playlists, albums, artists and podcasts, a queue you can jump around in, like, shuffle and repeat, in Exo's black look. Each service still plays the music itself, hidden behind Exo and drawing nothing while it does, so your account, downloads and audio quality are the service's own. The Apple Music app for Windows is no longer needed: Apple's own web player does the playing.
  • Every song you saved, one click away. Songs sits under Home: Spotify's Liked Songs, Apple Music's library songs, YouTube Music's library songs, in one list that scrolls smoothly through thousands. Sort by date added, title, artist or album, narrow it with Filter, and play from any song; it plays on in the order you see.
  • Jam in Exo. Start a Spotify Jam from the player strip, copy its invite link, see who joined, remove someone, and leave or end it.
  • Friend activity. See what the people you follow on Spotify are playing, with a green Now for anyone listening right now.
  • Lyrics that follow the song. Now playing shows the song large with its lyrics; on Spotify and Apple Music the sung line lights up and stays in the middle, and clicking a line jumps there.
  • More of your library. Spotify's own filters (Playlists, Podcasts, Albums, Artists) and sort orders, folders that open in place, Home that starts with Recently played, podcasts with their episodes, and search that finds podcasts. Each song's menu adds it to the queue, likes it, adds it to a playlist or takes it out of yours.
  • Covers that are right. A playlist without its own picture shows its four-cover mosaic, YouTube Music's mixes and built-in playlists show theirs, and anything without a picture shows a quiet note instead of an empty square.
  • Signing in looks like Exo. Signed out, the Music room shows the service's mark and one Sign in button. You sign in on the service's own page or window; Exo never sees your password.
  • A steadier, faster Music room. A page stays put until the next one has loaded, covers keep their picture until the new one is ready, pages you have opened come back at once, and the mini player keeps its cover when you skip. An idle, paused player wakes the moment your pointer reaches the mini player or the Music tab, so the first click lands at once, and the volume stays live while music is paused.
  • Discord's settings answer at once. Changing text size, zoom or density in Discord took seconds and could swell Discord past 5 GB: Exo's reduced-motion rules gave every element a transition on everything. Exo now only shortens animations, in Discord, Spotify, Cider and YouTube Music.
  • Equicord is always on, FakeNitro included. Discord in Exo always runs Equicord with Exo's plugin set: no Quests, Nitro or Shop clutter, no tracking, lighter drawing, and FakeNitro (any emoji and sticker, sent as a link where Discord would refuse it, and source-quality streams). Discord's terms forbid client mods and FakeNitro; turn FakeNitro or any plugin off in Equicord's own plugin list if you'd rather not. Settings no longer has Optimize rows: Exo optimizes every hosted app by itself.
  • Discord in Exo's ink, at its own size. Discord's text and icons use Exo's cream and gray; its font, size and zoom are Discord's own, set in Discord's settings. The Wave buttons in the DM list are hidden.
  • Discord starts faster and stays out of the way. Discord keeps its compiled shaders and script cache between starts, no longer takes the foreground when it starts behind Exo or a game, no longer writes voice debug logs, and its unread count reaches the Discord tab and Exo's taskbar button the moment it changes.
  • Game pages that fit and open fast. A page opens in about half a second and never scrolls: trailers and screenshots on the left, buying, the description, details and System requirements on the right, Steam Deck and tags under the logo. Get on Steam shows at once and the price fills in; other stores follow a moment later. A game you point at is already loading when you click it.
  • Prices in your currency, more on every card. Steam prices in your Windows region's currency, review scores, Steam Deck ratings and top tags. On sale is Steam's own regional specials list.
  • A calmer Store. Shelves rise in once, the banner fades between games, Refresh shows a band of light while it works, On wishlist keeps a green check, and buttons never jump. Store links open in the store's own app when it is installed. The Store tab counts wishlisted games on sale that you don't own.
  • Library > Upscalers. Every installed game with older DLSS, FSR or XeSS than Exo has, as posters with their version jumps, and Update all. Exo reads DLSS Swapper's public list of builds once a day and uses a build only after it passes Exo's own checks (the vendor's signature, exact version, size and checksum); Restore puts the game's own files back. A running game waits until it closes, an anti-cheat game changes only once you allow it on its page, and Riot games never appear.
  • DLSS presets. On an NVIDIA PC, set the driver's forced preset for DLSS Super Resolution, Ray Reconstruction and Frame Generation, with what each model is good at and the best one for your card marked, and turn on NVIDIA's own DLSS overlay (Windows asks for administrator permission).
  • Exo Shaders ships with Exo. Library > Shaders is Exo Shaders, Exo's shader precompiler (a fork of SCSKiller, GPL-3.0-or-later, source at github.com/UhhErix/exo-shaders), now inside the installer. Each game shows one word of state; the queue, cache and About are one list each.
  • Refresh the library. A round button next to + reads every game again: sizes, every store's games, time played, last launched, Riot hours, achievements and art. A shine crosses the cards while it works.
  • A cleaner game page. The store sits centred above the cover, Time played, Last launched, Size and Achievements sit in one row, and buttons never make the page jump. Double-click an installed game to play it, and Ctrl+1 to Ctrl+5 open the rooms.
  • Riot and Xbox. Riot games installed anywhere are found, show Update when Riot needs to patch them and exact sizes; Teamfight Tactics is in the Library. Xbox games use their real names, Microsoft Store art and sizes.
  • Achievements and last launched. Achievements show on the game page (Steam's own summary first, so they come in fast), and a game started from any store app moves up in Last launched.
  • Devices is built around your mouse. A large picture of your mouse with its battery (and voltage or charge state), signal and its strength, polling rate and firmware, each shown once; its settings in compact cards beside it. A change shows a band of light on the mouse while it is written and a check when it lands.
  • More of the Finalmouse Starlight X. Each main switch has its own release point, lift-off has a custom distance from 1.0 to 1.7 mm, the receiver light adds TMR-DS and a custom color, and Tournament scroll is Bhop mode, its new name.
  • Quieter behind a game. With Devices hidden, Exo Mouse reads only the battery, every five minutes. A mouse that reconnects mid-game gets the game's profile back.
  • Store apps give their memory back. While Steam, the Epic Games Launcher, the EA app, Ubisoft Connect, GOG Galaxy or Battle.net sits hidden behind Exo, Exo hands the memory their built-in browser holds back to Windows. On a test PC Steam's helpers fell from about 280 MB to under 80 MB. Nothing is closed: games, downloads, the overlay, Steam Input, cloud saves and achievements work as before.
  • Lighter behind your games. Spotify runs without hardware acceleration behind Exo (about 160 MB lighter) with its logging and notifications off, Exo loads 1.4 MB less script at startup, and Exo's background checks do the same work with a fraction of the CPU.
  • One motion and one look, everywhere. Views rise in once, work shows a soft band of light, done draws a check, every selected pill glides to what you picked, and Reduce motion turns it all off. No button turns see-through on hover. Settings says less, and Text size reaches every room.
  • Exo never gets stuck behind itself. Exo started without the foreground could think it was in the background and stay throttled; it now checks Windows every few seconds.

3.4.0 — October 15, 2026

This update adds a Store, brings Exo Mouse to the Devices room, hosts SCSKiller's own app for shaders, gives downloads pause, play-time holds and download hours, reads Epic's new launcher, removes the Exo account, keeps Discord and Equicord current with a better plugin set, finishes the black themes in every music app, fixes the mini player volume, and rebuilds the upscaler swapper around official, verified files. It never changes files in games with anti-cheat unless you allow it for that game; Riot games are never changed.

  • No more Exo account. Sign-in, sign-up, handles and badges are gone, along with Settings > Exo account and its Download and Delete account buttons. Setup is two steps, your game stores then Music and Discord, and Settings is six sections. Your library, playtime, settings and keys never depended on the account and work exactly as before. Steam, Epic, GOG, Discord and your music app keep their own sign-ins.
  • Your old sign-in is cleaned off this PC. The first time this version starts, it deletes the sign-in an earlier Exo saved (auth.bin) and its cache of account responses. It does this on your PC only and sends nothing to the account service. If you made an account before, the privacy policy says what the service still holds and how to delete it.
  • Exo Mouse in the Devices room. Devices now shows Exo's own mouse settings, in Exo's look. The top line is your mouse: name, brand, connection and signal, and battery. Below it, only what your mouse has: DPI and DPI stages, polling rate, lift-off distance, motion sync, angle snapping, ripple control, click debounce, power mode, onboard profile, button remapping, lighting and the sleep timer. A setting your mouse fixes shows as its value. Every change is written to the mouse and read back. With more than one mouse plugged in, a pill switches between them.
  • No other app needed. Exo Mouse talks to your mouse directly and works with every mouse OpenMouse supports, dozens of brands. It needs no OpenMouse app, no vendor software and no driver.
  • Built on OpenMouse, open source. Exo Mouse is built on OpenMouse's open-source protocol library (AGPL) and is its own open-source program under the same license, at github.com/UhhErix/exo-mouse. Exo Launcher keeps its own license.
  • The Finalmouse Starlight X works. OpenMouse's library could not read a Starlight X at all: it ignored how the receiver reports DPI and polling rate. Exo Mouse fixes that and adds the Starlight X's own controls: mechanical or TMR analog main switches per side, actuation from 0.01 to 0.40 mm, rapid trigger from 150 to 250 µm, release point and its onboard profiles (only the ones turned on), plus the receiver light, tournament scroll and passthrough window. Polling shows as 1000 Hz with no choice, since PerfectPolling fixes it; lift-off offers the 1 mm and 2 mm presets, and a custom lift-off set in xpanel shows as its value. Exo reads the mouse no more often than its driver asks, every 10 seconds.
  • Your mouse, pictured. Devices shows your mouse's own render beside its settings (from OpenMouse's image library, for the models it has), with its name, link and battery under it.
  • Your mouse, looked after. When its battery drops to 20% Exo says so once (at most every 6 hours) and shows a small battery chip by the gear that opens Devices, until you charge it. A signal that stays weak gets one line saying to move the receiver. Devices shows the signal as bars and a word. A game-linked profile says when it comes on and when your settings go back. All of this works with Devices never opened: the room starts quietly a few seconds after Exo.
  • Mouse profiles with names. Save current stores everything Exo can set on your mouse under a name, as many as you like. Apply puts one back, the one that matches your mouse right now says Active, and Rename and Delete are beside it. Desktop is built in and always the default: your mouse goes back to it whenever no linked game is running, and Update saves your current settings into it. Link any other profile to games, picked by their covers, and it comes on when one starts, with or without the Devices room open. A switch made while a wireless mouse sleeps lands as soon as it wakes. Switching takes a fraction of a second. Profiles are saved in Exo's own data folder, and ones saved by an earlier build move there by themselves.
  • The OpenMouse web panel and Exo's own bridge are gone. Exo no longer loads the web panel or answers on OpenMouse Bridge's port, the game page no longer shows a mouse profile line, and Settings > Devices is gone. If you had switched to Exo's bridge, the first start of this version writes the profiles Exo kept back into the OpenMouse Bridge app's own settings. It does not start the app or put it back on Windows startup; Exo Mouse does not need it.
  • Discord stays optimized after it updates itself. When Discord installed an update, Equicord carried itself into the new version, and Exo took that for an Equicord you had installed yourself: it stopped applying its settings, Settings said Equicord was installed outside Exo, and Undo could not take it out. Exo now recognizes its own Equicord in the new version and puts its own loader back at the next start, and Undo returns Discord to stock either way. If Equicord's file goes missing, Discord starts as Discord ships it while Exo downloads it again, instead of not starting at all.
  • Run lets Discord update first. If Discord has an update waiting, Optimize Discord > Run lets it install before Equicord goes in, and says so. If Discord installed an update while it was open, Run's restart opens the new version and optimizes that one.
  • Equicord's October 8 build. A first install of Equicord is now the October 8 build, checked against its SHA-256. Equicord keeps itself up to date after that, and Quests stay hidden in it.
  • A safer, lighter plugin set. Optimize Discord no longer turns on FakeNitro, which sent Nitro emoji and stickers as links and raised stream quality without Nitro, against Discord's terms. It also no longer makes Discord think you have Nitro, which hid upsells but offered Nitro-only choices that then failed; the Nitro and Shop tabs, gift buttons and the Nitro offer count stay hidden. Plugins that sent Discord extra requests for unknown mentions and missing replies, loaded full-size images in chat, or got around YouTube's blocks are off.
  • Discord acts like Discord where it matters. Friends see what you play and when you type again (Discord's own Activity privacy settings decide that), Krisp noise suppression works in calls, friend and message request counts show, button tooltips and the device menus beside mute and deafen are back, and channel names, image grids and search are Discord's own. A call timer shows in the voice panel, and Equicord no longer opens a list of its new plugins after it updates. Plugins you changed in Equicord's own settings keep your choice.
  • The newest DLSS, FSR and XeSS, checked byte for byte. Upscaler updates now bring DLSS 310.9.1 (Super Resolution, Ray Reconstruction and Frame Generation), FSR from AMD's FidelityFX SDK 2.3.0 (upscaler 4.1.1, frame generation 4.0.1), FSR 3.1.4, and XeSS SDK 3.0.2 (frame generation 1.3.1, XeLL 1.3.2). Every file comes straight from NVIDIA's, AMD's or Intel's own GitHub repository and is used only if its SHA-256 matches the value built into Exo and it carries the vendor's signature. Exo no longer reads a third-party catalog or asks GitHub what is newest each time.
  • A simple upscaler list on the game page. Each upscaler the game uses, such as DLSS Super Resolution, DLSS Frame Generation, FSR or XeSS, gets one row: the version in green when it is the latest (310.9.1), or the old version still in the game in red when it is outdated, and Update and Restore sit above the list. A game that ships the same upscaler in two folders, or XeSS for both DirectX 11 and 12, still shows one row, red if any copy is outdated. No folders or notes. Update brings every file to the newest and never puts an older file over a newer one, and FSR and XeSS files that load each other move together. Restore puts the game's own files back and checks them against the saved copies byte for byte. When a game update replaces a file Exo changed, the new file becomes the original, and if Steam's file check puts the originals back, Update simply shows again. 32-bit and other non-x64 files are left alone.
  • Anti-cheat games change only if you allow it, one game at a time. A game with Easy Anti-Cheat, BattlEye, Ricochet, EA Javelin, Anti-Cheat Expert or another anti-cheat Exo knows, found by its store, its title or its files anywhere in the install, says which one and changes nothing. Allow changes asks you to confirm that swapping DLLs in a game with anti-cheat can get your account banned, that the risk is entirely yours and so is playing online with changed files; Exo keeps the originals and Restore puts them back. Stop allowing ends it and offers to restore. Exo checks the game again before every change and refuses if the anti-cheat is different or the game is running, and Update all never reaches a game you have not allowed. Riot games and games with Riot Vanguard are never changed, with no way to allow it.
  • Upscaler status opens at once. The sheet answers from what Exo found last time without going online or re-reading large files, and checks the folder again in the background.
  • DLSS presets. On a PC with an NVIDIA graphics card, the sheet points to DLSS override in the NVIDIA app, which is where a game's DLSS preset is chosen. Exo only replaces files.
  • The mini player shows your music's real volume. On a fresh start the volume in the top bar could sit at zero with the muted icon while your song played, until you touched it. It now shows the app's own level as soon as the app answers, keeps asking until it does, and only shows muted when the app is muted. A level you pick while Spotify or YouTube Music is still starting is applied once it is ready.
  • Optimized music apps are black all the way through. Spotify's chips, search field, buttons and the cards in the now-playing panel no longer turn into invisible boxes, and its menus, pop-ups, dialogs and tooltips are no longer gray: raised surfaces are a near-black with a thin edge, hover is a faint step up. A promo Exo hides takes its empty slot with it. YouTube Music's menus, Settings dialog, chips, Explore buttons and the top search result follow the same look, the Premium-only Download item is gone from its menus, and the artist photo fades to black under the name again. In Apple Music, menus and dialogs stand off the page, the dark fades over artwork stay, and headers that float over a scrolling page stay solid.
  • Shaders, with SCSKiller's own app inside Exo. A Shaders button beside Sort opens SCSKiller, the free shader precompiler, inside Exo at a large size, with Exo's title and close button and without the app's own title bar or window buttons. Everything SCSKiller does is its own: compiling, recordings, Before and after, official and community recordings, its cache tools, settings, updates and its Patreon builds. Exo starts it hidden, and when Exo closes it goes back to its tray and keeps compiling. Without it, Shaders offers Get SCSKiller, which opens its download page. Exo runs no part of SCSKiller itself, and removes the copy an earlier Exo downloaded.
  • A game's page says whether it is compiled. Its Shaders line reads SCSKiller's own records: Compiled when the app compiled the game for the graphics driver installed now (as soon as a compile ends), Driver changed or Game updated when it should compile again, Not supported when SCSKiller can't, and otherwise one button that opens Shaders.
  • One look for actions. Undo in Settings and Stop allowing in Upscalers are buttons like the ones beside them, choice buttons use the same text size as other buttons, the Optimize descriptions are one line each, and the Settings section list follows the section you are reading.
  • The new Epic Games Launcher. Epic's new launcher (2026, installed per user) keeps its installed games in a new list instead of the old manifests, so games it installed went missing from Exo. Exo now reads that list too, titles each game from the launcher's own catalog, finds the new launcher to start games and to install, update and uninstall through it, and notices when it installs or removes a game. The old launcher keeps working as before.
  • A Store in Exo. A new Store tab beside Library leads with the biggest games the way Steam's and Epic's stores do: a banner of the biggest launches of the last two months and the most pre-ordered games coming (each says Out now or when it comes), then New and trending, Most anticipated, Top sellers, On sale and Most wishlisted, ranked by Steam's own sales and pre-orders, and Epic's free games with when each one ends. Upcoming games show their release date. Search finds any game as you type. Games you already own say In library, and their Store page has a button to your copy.
  • Store game pages. A big page with the game's hero art and logo, its trailers playing right on the page in Steam's sharpest stream (1080p AV1 where the PC can play it), full-size screenshots, its description, reviews, release date, developer, publisher and genres, and its minimum and recommended PC requirements side by side. Get on Steam (or Wishlist on Steam before release) sits at the top, with the same game on Epic, GOG or Ubisoft when that official store sells it, and its price on each. No key resellers are listed.
  • Wishlist with sale alerts. Wishlist a game in the Store and Exo checks its price now and then, and tells you once when it goes on sale.
  • GG.deals in the Store. A GG.deals button on a game's Store page opens that game on GG.deals, for anyone who buys keys. Library pages stay as they were.
  • Download controls. Epic, GOG and Amazon downloads that Exo runs can be paused from the Downloads panel. In the new Settings > Downloads, downloads pause while you play and pick up when you stop (on by default), can be kept to hours you choose such as overnight, and games update by themselves while your PC is idle with no game running. The install folder moved there too.
  • Queued downloads start. Pressing Install on a second game while one downloads puts it in line, but when its turn came Exo checked a Steam list that leaves out games you own and haven't installed, so every queued Steam game was cancelled with "no longer available to the active store account". Queued games now start in order, still checked against Steam's current list of what you own.
  • Big downloads no longer "time out". Exo stopped waiting on a Steam install after two hours (an update after 90 minutes) even while Steam was still downloading, and the game page said "Exo did not answer in time" after ten. Exo now waits as long as Steam keeps downloading, and only gives up if Steam never starts or makes no progress for two hours.
  • Paused Steam downloads resume. After a restart, Resume refused every paused Steam game while any other game had a partial download in Steam's folder, which a cancelled download leaves behind. Steam keeps its own download queue, so Resume now always goes through.
  • Downloads pick up again after a restart. Downloads that were running or waiting in line when Exo closed now start again by themselves, in the same order, once the library loads. Each still passes the same checks as pressing Resume (same store account, still owned, not already installed); one that fails stays paused with its reason, and the rest carry on instead of all pausing with it.
  • The Downloads panel reads cleanly. A paused game's row says Paused instead of a long sentence that piled up over the game titles, and Cancel in the panel asks Sure? first, since rows move when a download starts.
  • Download progress keeps moving. A download that started from the queue could stay on Waiting… in Exo while Steam was well into it, because one refresh left Exo's progress display thinking an update was already on its way. It now follows every update.
  • Exo says when Steam can't reach its servers. When Steam's download log shows only connection failures for the last two minutes, the download's line says Steam can't reach its download servers instead of looking stuck.
  • Tidier download rows and game tools. A download's status and size share one line in the Downloads panel, and a game's tools (Open folder, Verify files, Remove, Replace cover, Refresh art) fit on one row.
  • A game in line says Queued. Its button on the game page reads Queued instead of Install, and pressing it twice takes it out of the line.
  • One stray click no longer cancels a download. The button that shows a download's progress also cancels it. The first press now reads Press again to cancel, and goes back after a few seconds.
  • Install starts again after an old paused request. A download left paused from an earlier session blocked Install on that game with no way to clear it from the game page. Pressing Install now replaces it.
  • Steam says why a download stopped. When Steam can't reach its download servers or runs out of disk space, the progress line says so instead of sitting at 0%.

3.3.1 — September 26, 2026

This update puts one Optimize button on every app Exo hosts, keeps Quests out of Discord, sharpens and steadies library covers, lets you drag the whole top bar, links the mini player to your music app's own volume, and adds a Devices room for your mouse.

  • Library covers stay sharp and stay put. Grid covers are drawn from full-quality art at twice the card size (most Steam covers now show at full size), fast scrolling no longer tears or mixes covers, and covers no longer vanish after Exo loses focus or sits minimized: a cover that failed to load retries on its own when it comes back into view.
  • Covers stay put when you come back from another room. A cover that finished loading while Discord, Music, Devices or Settings was open was thrown away, so the card came back blank until it loaded again. It now shows the moment you return, and a cover that could not load tries again when you come back to the Library.
  • Settings opens at once. Settings now shows what it knew last time right away and checks for changes behind it: your Exo account no longer waits on two trips to the account service, and the Music and Discord, Optimize and Devices rows are ready on the first open instead of saying Checking. Opening the Devices room no longer holds up Exo for up to three seconds while the OpenMouse panel loads, and a game's upscaler check answers from what Exo found last time instead of searching the whole game folder again after every start.
  • Covers hold still at the end of the library. With reduced motion on in Exo or in Windows, small scrolls up and down near the bottom of the library made the covers jump a row, swap places for a moment or blink out. Reduced motion now turns animations off instead of making every change on the page a very short one, which is what moved the rows out of step.
  • One Steam request per click. While Steam held an update in its queue, Exo asked Steam again every 12 seconds, starting Steam with the game's install link each time, for up to two minutes per click. Update and Install now ask once, and once more 20 seconds later only if Steam has not picked the request up.
  • Optimize each app with one button. Settings > Music and Discord has an Optimize row for every app you have installed: Discord, Spotify, Apple Music, Cider and YouTube Music. Each row says what it changes. Run applies it now and shows what it did, such as "Optimized · Equicord 2026-09-26, 42 plugins", and Undo puts the app back the way it ships. If the app has to restart to pick up the change, Exo says so while it restarts it, and it never restarts an app during a call or while music is playing; it asks you to try again later instead. Exo applies the optimization again each time the app updates itself. This replaces the Clean up Discord switch. If that switch was on, Discord stays optimized.
  • Quests really are gone from an optimized Discord. Equicord's Quest plugin reset its own settings the first time it started, which brought Quests and the Quest button back. Exo now sets up the plugin so its settings stay put. It also hides Quest badges and promos, sponsored Quest banners, Super Reaction animations and Super React as the default, and the Discover and app download buttons in the server list. Plugins you change in Equicord's own settings keep your choice when Exo applies the optimization again. The first Equicord download is now the September 26 build, checked against its SHA-256.
  • Spotify, optimized. On top of the black theme, Exo hides home-feed ads, merch and concert promos, the What's New button and install-app prompts. Canvas videos stop decoding, so each shows a still frame, and Spotify's own preferences for hiding home-page takeovers and promoted songs are turned on. If an update changes Spotify's files in a way Exo does not recognize, Exo leaves Spotify as it ships.
  • Optimized apps draw less, all the time. Optimize Discord now also adds an Exo performance theme to Equicord, listed after your own themes: animations finish at once, transitions are instant and glass blur is off, so hover, press and selection still show without the fade. Loading spinners keep turning, the speaking ring in voice stays, and spoilers stay blurred. Optimize Spotify, Cider and YouTube Music do the same inside each app. Turn the theme off in Equicord's Themes tab and it stays off; Undo removes it and nothing else. Discord's own GIF, emoji and sticker animation settings are synced to your Discord account, so Exo leaves them to you.
  • Discord steps back while you play. Exo already lowers the priority of Discord and your music app while Exo is behind something and they are silent. A Discord room left open behind a game launched from Exo now yields too. A call or a song keeps normal priority, so voice and music never crackle.
  • Spotify's window buttons stay gone. Exo hides Spotify's own minimize, maximize and close buttons inside the room, but a Spotify that started quickly, reloaded its page or took long to start could show them again. Exo now keeps hiding them for as long as Spotify runs, and the What's New, Friend Activity and profile buttons sit at the right edge where the window buttons were.
  • Apple Music, Cider and YouTube Music. Optimize Apple Music is the black background it already had, and you can now turn it off. It now also reaches the parts that stayed gray: the window's glass backdrop behind the app, the sidebar, the lighter content panel and cards, menus, pop-ups and dialogs, and pages opened later. Text and buttons keep Apple's colors, and panel edges become a thin dark line. Optimize Cider turns off Cider's blurred glass panels and smooth scrolling while Exo hosts it. Optimize YouTube Music hides the Premium upsells and stops drawing the video while the room is hidden, and the song keeps playing.
  • The whole top bar moves the window. While a song showed, the mini player and all the space around it refused to drag, and the bar could stay undraggable after an update check until you resized the window. Now every part of the bar that is not a button, tab or slider drags, including the pill's empty middle and the song's time. Double-click it to maximize or restore, drag it to a screen edge to snap, and hover the maximize button for Windows' snap layouts.
  • One volume for your music. The mini player's volume is now Spotify's and YouTube Music's own volume: move either slider and the other follows within a second. Exo used to turn the app down in the Windows volume mixer instead, which stacked with the app's own slider; that mixer level goes back to 100% once, with the app's volume lowered to match so nothing gets louder. Apple Music and Cider have no volume Exo can reach, so they keep the mixer level, and the mini player now follows changes made in the Windows mixer.
  • The mini player keeps up with your music. It now reads the song straight from YouTube Music and Spotify instead of from Windows, so it shows whenever a song is loaded, playing or paused, stays put while the app sleeps in the background, and never picks up a browser or another app playing sound. Its time counts second by second in step with the app, and play, pause, skip and seek always reach the app it shows. Apple Music and Cider only ever show their own song.
  • Devices, a new room after Music. The room is the OpenMouse panel itself, in Exo's black, for DPI, polling rate, buttons and lighting, with OpenMouse's announcement strip hidden. Exo is the panel's bridge: it answers the panel on OpenMouse Bridge's own port while Exo is open, so there is nothing to download and no extra app in your tray. Every mouse the OpenMouse panel supports works, because the panel's own drivers talk to the mouse; Exo only carries what they send. Exo accepts only OpenMouse's own site, so no other web page can reach your mouse through it.
  • Mouse profiles for your whole library. Every installed game in your library is on OpenMouse's game list next to OpenMouse's own games, so any of them can have a profile, and it switches when the game comes to the front. Exo never lists launchers or anti-cheat, and leaves Riot games to OpenMouse, which already lists VALORANT and League. When you have profiles, Exo keeps the OpenMouse panel loaded in the background, so they switch within seconds even while Exo is minimized. Profiles switch only while Exo is open. A game's sheet now shows the mouse profile it uses.
  • Use Exo instead of the OpenMouse Bridge app. If the OpenMouse Bridge app is on your PC, Exo leaves it in charge until you choose Use Exo's bridge in Settings > Devices. Exo then closes the app, takes it out of Windows startup and brings over your profiles; the app's own files are not changed. Undo takes you back: Exo starts the app and puts it back in Windows startup. Profiles you change while Exo's bridge is in use stay in Exo.
  • Mouse battery. Settings > Devices shows each mouse's battery as the OpenMouse panel reads it, and Exo tells you when a mouse drops to 20% or less, once every six hours per mouse, while Exo is open.
  • Covers for your games in OpenMouse. Your installed games show their covers on the OpenMouse Games page inside Exo, the same art as your library.
  • MambaSnake M5 Ultra works in the OpenMouse panel inside Exo. OpenMouse listed it without controls; in Exo it gets the DPI, polling rate, button and lighting settings of the Attack Shark R5 Ultra it is built on.

3.3.0 — September 25, 2026

This update rebuilds setup and Settings, shows your whole Steam library without a key, and leaves Discord exactly as Discord ships it unless you ask Exo to clean it up. Epic and GOG sign-in completes, keys you paste save, and Exo does less while it sits behind your game.

Requires Windows 11 x64.

  • Setup in three short steps. Your game stores, Music and Discord, and an optional Exo account, in one column with no keys to paste. The account step is skipped when the service can't sign anyone in, and its form sits on the page instead of in a box inside a box.
  • Settings, rebuilt. A section list on the left edge and one roomy column: General, Music and Discord, Game stores, Optional keys, Exo account and About. Settings that did nothing are gone, every row says what it does, and keys you paste (Steam, itch.io, SteamGridDB) now actually save. An Exo account the service can't reach offers Retry instead of a red error. The memory reading is gone from About.
  • Your whole Steam library shows up without a Web API key. Exo used to know which Steam games you own only if you pasted a Steam Web API key, so for most people installed games read "Ownership unverified" and games you own but have not installed never appeared. Exo now asks the Steam client you are signed in to which games are yours, so installed games are owned and uninstalled ones appear ready to install. What Steam confirms is remembered, so they stay while Steam is closed. With a key, libraries over 400 games are no longer cut short.
  • Steam starts itself when it needs to. If Steam is installed with a remembered sign-in but is not running, Exo starts it quietly once, waits for it to answer, and fills in what you own. The library never waits on it. Without a remembered sign-in Exo leaves Steam closed rather than opening its login window on every start.
  • Owned shelves show games only. Bonus content, soundtracks, SDKs, dev kits, test servers and tools stay off the Owned, not installed shelf, including ones remembered from before.
  • Epic and GOG sign-in completes. The sign-in window no longer runs an ad blocker or blocks Epic's and GOG's sign-in providers, so Google, Xbox, PlayStation, Steam and the others work.
  • Song saving and trophy pop-ups are gone. The mini player no longer has Save and Remove buttons, and Exo no longer saves songs to YouTube Music, Spotify or Apple Music (through Cider). The Spotify connection and the Spotify client ID and Cider API token settings are removed, and the keys saved for them are deleted on the next start. Exo also stops showing a pop-up when you unlock an achievement, and the notification settings that went with it are gone. Playback, seek, volume and opening the Music room are unchanged. Exo still reads achievement counts, now once after a game closes rather than every few seconds while you play. The launch and install-complete sounds are gone too.
  • Discord is left as Discord ships it. Exo used to put Equicord into Discord's files for everyone, and Discord's own updater could not build a new version from the changed files, which left Discord unable to start. Discord is now stock unless you turn on Clean up Discord in Settings, and what an earlier Exo changed is undone the next time Exo starts Discord. Off puts Discord's own files back. On, Exo checks before every Discord start and after any start that opens nothing: if a Discord update never finished, Exo puts Discord's own files back, moves the unfinished version aside, and starts Discord through its own updater so the update runs again. Equicord goes back in on the next start. An Equicord you installed yourself is left alone.
  • The Friends room is called Discord, in the top bar and the command palette, because that is what it holds.
  • Messages say what actually happened. Signing in to Steam or Riot opens that app and says to finish there, instead of reporting a connection that has not happened yet. A failed Epic or GOG install or update names the game and the reason instead of printing the tool's raw output. Cider's button says Get, because Exo can only open its download page, and the message after it says to finish in your browser. Errors are plain sentences throughout.
  • Library and window fixes. Activating a key hands it to the store's own redeem window and confirms the game when the store does, even after the popover has closed. Success messages are no longer red. The library toolbar no longer covers the first shelf. The mini player fits inside the top bar's pill. Minimize minimizes the window instead of hiding it.
  • Discord stays quiet behind music. Discord starts hidden with Exo, and a Discord that had never been on screen kept drawing while hidden: with Spotify playing, its graphics process woke about 45 times a second to animate the song in Discord's own panel. Exo now shows it once for a second through a single pixel over Exo's black background, which is enough for it to notice it is hidden; its hidden cost drops by about half. Discord and a playing Spotify behind a closed room also run their drawing processes in Windows efficiency mode, never their audio, network or DRM, and opening the room restores full speed at once. While Exo is minimized or hidden, its own memory in use drops from about 260 MB to about 40 MB.
  • Scrolling the library costs less. Library cards used to decode each saved poster at full size (usually 600 by 900) to draw it about 155 pixels wide. Cards now draw a smaller copy that Exo makes from that same file, sized for the largest card your screen can show at its scaling. The copy is made again whenever the cover changes, including a cover you picked yourself. The game sheet still shows the full image. Scrolling a 199-game library top to bottom and back now takes about half the page's CPU time, about 30% less CPU across Exo, and a third less page memory.
  • Less work while nothing changes. A library refresh that ends with the same games is no longer sent to the page again. Download progress redraws four times a second instead of on every line a store tool prints. Game sheets reuse the upscaler check while those files are unchanged instead of re-hashing hundreds of megabytes each time, and cover files are not re-read to answer the same question twice.
  • Works better with a keyboard and a screen reader. Focus comes back to a button that turned itself off while it worked, and stays inside the key popover, the game sheet and the command palette. Download buttons stop renaming themselves every second, so a screen reader is not interrupted by each percent. Library cards announce that they open a sheet, switches have a larger hit area, the command palette's gray text is readable, and a screen reader can confirm deleting your account with a click, since it cannot hold.
  • Exo no longer shares your status or game activity. While you were signed in, Exo kept a connection open to the Exo account service and sent it whether you were online and which game you were playing. About a minute after your library changed, it also uploaded your total hours, game counts, up to ten of your most-played games with their hours and achievement counts, and a random id for this PC. Exo sends none of that now. The account only signs you in and out, exports your data and deletes the account, and the id file is deleted on the next start. Whatever an earlier version sent is in your account export and is removed if you delete the account. The privacy statement is rewritten to match what Exo actually does.
  • Smaller app. Code for retired features, unused styles and host messages nothing called are removed, along with a Discord start-up setting Exo wrote that Discord never reads. An Exo account no longer syncs preferences, which needed a handle Exo no longer offers; your settings stay on this PC. Exo's own page now loads scripts only from Exo and allows nothing it does not show.

3.2.7 — September 23, 2026

This update makes Exo start about three times faster, use less memory, and keep Discord and your music app working through their own updates. It also removes two rooms and fixes a bug where Exo could move or close its own window.

Requires Windows 11 x64.

  • Discover and Devices are gone. Exo is Library, Friends and Music. The store catalog, store search, product pages, the wishlist, the GG.deals key and the mouse profile studio are removed along with their background work and cached files; old profile and catalog files are cleaned up on the next start. Buy links on a library game still open the vendor's store page.
  • Hold to delete. Deleting your account is a hold, not a click: the button fills over a second while you keep it pressed and fires only when the fill completes; letting go early cancels.
  • Segments slide. The Settings segmented controls move one thumb between choices instead of repainting each option.
  • Settings fits the window again. Every panel is visible at once with nothing scrolling, in its own box or as a page. The store list you can add from sits in two columns instead of one tall stack, the rows are a little tighter, and the memory reading is gone from About.
  • The mini player finds a player that starts quiet. Exo used to look for the player's Windows media session for one minute after launching it, then stop. Opening Music without playing anything meant the mini player, its transport and its volume stayed empty for the rest of the session. It now keeps looking for as long as the player is open, and the log records one line per change instead of one every two seconds.
  • A player that is stuck asking you something says so. When a music or chat client never opens its window, Exo used to leave the room reading only that the client did not open, while the prompt the client was waiting on stayed hidden because Exo hides boot windows on sight. Any such prompt is now shown again and named in the room, so a client blocked on a runtime update can actually be answered.
  • Exo no longer moves or closes itself with a hosted app. Windows reuses process numbers, and when the program that started Exo had exited, its number could be handed to a Cider or Discord helper. Exo then counted itself as part of that app: it moved its own window into the Music room until it slid off screen, and closing the app could close Exo mid-shutdown.
